# CodeKavach resources

Research papers, datasets, benchmarks, open-source tools, provider and platform documentation, competitor products and test applications that CodeKavach draws on. This file is the companion of [REFERENCE.md](REFERENCE.md), which lists the frameworks, standards, regulations and methodologies the tool is built on. Contributors and coding agents cite from these two files and do not introduce a source that is not listed here (see [AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md), section 9).

Total entries: 854. Verification date for every entry: 2026-09-21.

## How to read this file

- **Scope.** Section 1 holds research papers, section 2 datasets and benchmarks, section 3 open-source tools and libraries, section 4 LLM provider and open-model documentation, section 5 platform integration documentation, section 6 the competitor and adjacent product landscape, section 7 deliberately vulnerable applications and test suites, section 8 public audit report corpora.
- **Order.** Inside each table, entries are sorted by importance to the project (core, then important, then reference) and, within the same importance, newest first.
- **Links.** The first link is the canonical location (arXiv, DOI, repository or vendor page); a second link, where present, is the code repository, the peer-reviewed version or the companion page. Every entry was checked on the verification date: the URL was requested and the title, authors, licence or page content were compared with the entry. `doi.org` links resolve to publisher pages (ACM, IEEE, Springer) that may answer scripted requests with HTTP 403; for those entries the DOI metadata was confirmed through the Crossref API instead.
- **Licences.** The Licence column reproduces what the upstream repository or terms page stated on the verification date. CodeKavach is MIT-licensed and scans proprietary code, so copyleft, source-available, non-commercial and "no licence file" cases are called out in the Notes column. Third-party analysis engines are invoked as external processes or containers and are not imported, unless an issue says otherwise. Re-check a licence before bundling anything.
- **One home per entry.** A resource that matters to several sections is listed once, in the section where it is used most directly. A tool and the paper that describes it may both appear, and a project is listed twice only where two distinct features are relevant. About sixty entries also have a row in REFERENCE.md, by design: interchange formats and data feeds (SARIF, CycloneDX, purl, OSV-adjacent feeds such as EPSS, KEV and Vulnrichment, in-toto, RFC 3161), the risk-rating studies of section 1.11, the code-review studies of section 1.3, the AI threat taxonomies in section 1.9 and the audit report corpora of section 8. This file is authoritative for licence, role tag and integration notes; REFERENCE.md is authoritative for version, publisher and how the framework is used in the methodology. When one of these entries changes, update both rows in the same commit.
- **Standards.** Interchange formats that our tooling reads or writes (SARIF, CycloneDX, SPDX, OpenVEX, purl, MCP) appear next to the tools that implement them. Policy standards and regulations are in REFERENCE.md.

## Legend: role tags

| Tag | Meaning |
|-----|---------|
| `[integrated]` | Part of the default pipeline described in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`: CodeKavach calls it as a subprocess or container, imports it, consumes its data feed or emits its format. The tag records a design commitment; implementation status is tracked on the issue board |
| `[planned]` | Optional adapter, alternative engine or later-milestone integration; not part of the default install |
| `[evaluation]` | Used to measure CodeKavach: accuracy corpus, ground truth, attacker model or metric in the privacy-utility experiments |
| `[baseline]` | System, tool or product we compare against in the evaluation or in the landscape analysis |
| `[inspiration]` | Design input: architecture, algorithm, threat-model item, user-experience pattern or template we learn from without depending on it |
| `[related-work]` | Cited in the research paper as background or related work |

An entry carries at most two tags. In the paper tables of section 1 the tag is the first item of the last column; elsewhere it has its own column.

## 1. Research papers

Peer-reviewed papers and preprints. Venue/Year gives the peer-reviewed venue where one exists; arXiv-only work is marked as a preprint and should be cited as such. Some subsections end with a short table of technical reports, vendor write-ups or incident reports that are not research papers but support the same argument.

### 1.1 LLM and ML vulnerability detection

What language models and earlier deep-learning detectors can and cannot do on vulnerability detection, and how to evaluate them soundly. These papers set the accuracy axis of the privacy-utility study.

**Empirical studies of LLM vulnerability detection**

| Paper | Authors | Venue/Year | Link | Relevance to CodeKavach |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Comparison of Static Application Security Testing Tools and Large Language Models for Repo-level Vulnerability Detection | Xin Zhou, Duc-Manh Tran, Thanh Le-Cong et al. | arXiv preprint, 2024 | [arXiv:2407.16235](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.16235) | [related-work] Empirical justification for the hybrid pipeline (SAST candidates + LLM reasoning) and for choosing which OSS engines to integrate per language. |
| Large Language Models for Code Analysis: Do LLMs Really Do Their Job? | Chongzhou Fang, Ning Miao, Shaurya Srivastav et al. | USENIX Security 2024 | [USENIX](https://www.usenix.org/conference/usenixsecurity24/presentation/fang); [arXiv:2310.12357](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.12357) | [related-work] Direct prior work for the privacy-utility curve: gives expected degradation under renaming/obfuscation and a task set to replicate at L2-L4. |
| Evaluating LLMs for Real-World Web Vulnerability Detection | Sebastian Neef, Luca Jungnickel, Antonio Benjamin Buchholz et al. | AI&CCPS Workshop @ ARES 2026 | [arXiv:2606.21397](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21397) | [related-work] Closest published comparison of hosted vs. self-hosted models on PHP web bugs - directly informs the provider matrix (Claude/OpenAI/Gemini vs. Qwen via Ollama/vLLM). |
| Case Study: Fine-tuning Small Language Models for Accurate and Private CWE Detection in Python Code | Md. Azizul Hakim Bappy, Hossen A Mustafa, Prottoy Saha, Rajinus Salehat | arXiv preprint, 2025 | [arXiv:2504.16584](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.16584) | [baseline] [related-work] States the same motivation as CodeKavach (cloud LLMs cannot be used on proprietary code) and answers it with the opposite design: a 350M-parameter code model fine-tuned on 500 synthetic Python samples for the CWE Top 25 and run on-premises. Small, synthetic-data study; cite as the published form of the 'just self-host a small model' baseline in the evaluation plan, not for its accuracy figures. |
| LLM-based Vulnerability Detection at Project Scale: An Empirical Study | Fengjie Li, Jiajun Jiang, Dongchi Chen, Yingfei Xiong | arXiv preprint, 2026 | [arXiv:2601.19239](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.19239) | [baseline] [related-work] Baseline to compare against and a realistic expectation-setter for precision/recall and token cost at project scale. |
| Everything You Wanted to Know About LLM-based Vulnerability Detection But Were Afraid to Ask | Yue Li, Xiao Li, Hao Wu et al. | arXiv preprint, 2025 | [arXiv:2504.13474](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.13474) | [related-work] Supports the central design bet that a well-chosen context slice matters more than raw code volume; the counterpoint to SecLLMHolmes in related work. |
| Understanding the Effectiveness of Large Language Models in Detecting Security Vulnerabilities | Avishree Khare, Saikat Dutta, Ziyang Li et al. | ICST 2025; arXiv Nov 2023 | [arXiv:2311.16169](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.16169); [doi:10.1109/ICST62969.2025.10988968](https://doi.org/10.1109/ICST62969.2025.10988968) | [inspiration] [related-work] Per-CWE evidence for which bug classes can be triaged from a small slice (good news for L3) and which need wider context; reuse its prompt variants as baselines. |
| To Err is Machine: Vulnerability Detection Challenges LLM Reasoning | Benjamin Steenhoek, Md Mahbubur Rahman, Monoshi Kumar Roy et al. | arXiv preprint, 2024 | [arXiv:2403.17218](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.17218) | [related-work] Error taxonomy for the qualitative analysis of CodeKavach triage failures; motivates supplying pre-computed facts (taint path, guards) instead of asking the LLM to derive them. |

**Agentic and multi-agent detection**

| Paper | Authors | Venue/Year | Link | Relevance to CodeKavach |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| VulnLLM-R: Specialized Reasoning LLM with Agent Scaffold for Vulnerability Detection | Yuzhou Nie, Hongwei Li, Chengquan Guo et al. | arXiv preprint, 2025 | [arXiv:2512.07533](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.07533); [GitHub: ucsb-mlsec/VulnLLM-R](https://github.com/ucsb-mlsec/VulnLLM-R) | [related-work] Candidate self-hosted triage model for air-gapped deployments, where no privacy layer is needed at all - the 'upper-privacy' comparison arm of the study. |
| Revelio: Cost-Efficient Agentic Memory Safety Vulnerability Detection For Repository-Scale Codebases | Yiwei Hou, Hao Wang, Muxi Lyu et al. | arXiv preprint (Jun 2026) | [arXiv:2606.22263](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22263) | [related-work] State of the art for "cheap model + static ranking + deterministic confirmation"; its cost accounting is a template for reporting tokens and dollars per confirmed finding at each privacy level. |
| VulAgent: Hypothesis-Validation based Multi-Agent Vulnerability Detection | Ziliang Wang, Ge Li, Jia Li et al. | Findings of ACL 2026; arXiv 2509.11523 | [arXiv:2509.11523](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.11523); [ACL Anthology](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-acl.928/) | [inspiration] [related-work] Design inspiration for hypothesis-validation prompts; the hypothesis itself is a compact, largely identifier-free artefact suited to L4. |
| VulTrial - Let the Trial Begin: A Mock-Court Approach to Vulnerability Detection using LLM-Based Agents | Ratnadira Widyasari, Martin Weyssow, Ivana Clairine Irsan et al. | ICSE 2026; arXiv 2505.10961 | [arXiv:2505.10961](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.10961); [doi:10.1145/3744916.3773256](https://doi.org/10.1145/3744916.3773256) | [related-work] Optional 'adversarial review' mode for high-severity findings before they enter the report. |
| MAVUL: Multi-Agent Vulnerability Detection via Contextual Reasoning and Interactive Refinement | Youpeng Li, Kartik Joshi, Xinda Wang, Eric Wong | IEEE TPS-ISA 2025; arXiv 2510.00317 | [arXiv:2510.00317](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.00317); [doi:10.1109/TPS-ISA67132.2025.00040](https://doi.org/10.1109/TPS-ISA67132.2025.00040) | [related-work] Reference point for iterative analyst/reviewer loops. |

| Paper | Authors | Venue/Year | Link | Relevance to CodeKavach |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| LineVul: A Transformer-based Line-Level Vulnerability Prediction | Michael Fu, Chakkrit Tantithamthavorn | MSR 2022 | [doi:10.1145/3524842.3528452](https://doi.org/10.1145/3524842.3528452); [GitHub: awsm-research/LineVul](https://github.com/awsm-research/LineVul) | [baseline] [related-work] Baseline to compare against: a locally runnable, no-data-leaves model representing the 'fine-tuned small model' alternative to sending code to an LLM. |
| DeepDFA - Dataflow Analysis-Inspired Deep Learning for Efficient Vulnerability Detection | Benjamin Steenhoek, Hongyang Gao, Wei Le | ICSE 2024 | [arXiv:2212.08108](https://arxiv.org/abs/2212.08108); [GitHub: ISU-PAAL/DeepDFA](https://github.com/ISU-PAAL/DeepDFA) | [related-work] Evidence that abstract data-flow facts (not identifier text) carry much of the vulnerability signal - supporting the L4 abstract-IR idea; a cheap local baseline. |
| VulLLM - Generalization-Enhanced Code Vulnerability Detection via Multi-Task Instruction Fine-Tuning | Xiaohu Du, Ming Wen, Jiahao Zhu et al. | Findings of ACL 2024; arXiv 2406.03718 | [ACL Anthology](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-acl.625/); [GitHub: CGCL-codes/VulLLM](https://github.com/CGCL-codes/VulLLM) | [baseline] [related-work] Recipe for a locally hosted, fine-tuned open model (the air-gapped / L4-friendly deployment) and a robustness baseline under renaming attacks. |
| UniXcoder: Unified Cross-Modal Pre-training for Code Representation | Daya Guo, Shuai Lu, Nan Duan et al. | ACL 2022 | [arXiv:2203.03850](https://arxiv.org/abs/2203.03850); [GitHub: microsoft/CodeBERT](https://github.com/microsoft/CodeBERT) | [baseline] [related-work] Local embedding/baseline option alongside CodeBERT. |
| VulBERTa: Simplified Source Code Pre-Training for Vulnerability Detection | Hazim Hanif, Sergio Maffeis (Imperial College London) | IJCNN 2022 | [arXiv:2205.12424](https://arxiv.org/abs/2205.12424); [GitHub: ICL-ml4csec/VulBERTa](https://github.com/ICL-ml4csec/VulBERTa) | [baseline] [related-work] Alternative local baseline; small enough for air-gapped deployment comparisons. |
| CodeT5: Identifier-aware Unified Pre-trained Encoder-Decoder Models for Code Understanding and Generation | Yue Wang, Weishi Wang, Shafiq Joty, Steven C. H. Hoi (Salesforce) | EMNLP 2021 | [arXiv:2109.00859](https://arxiv.org/abs/2109.00859); [GitHub: salesforce/CodeT5](https://github.com/salesforce/CodeT5) | [inspiration] [related-work] Threat-model input: identifier-recovery objectives like CodeT5's show that models can be trained to predict masked names, i.e. a realistic de-pseudonymisation attacker for the leakage metric. |
| GraphCodeBERT: Pre-training Code Representations with Data Flow | Daya Guo, Shuo Ren, Shuai Lu et al. | ICLR 2021 | [arXiv:2009.08366](https://arxiv.org/abs/2009.08366); [GitHub: microsoft/CodeBERT](https://github.com/microsoft/CodeBERT) | [baseline] [related-work] Relevant to the pseudonymisation argument: data-flow-aware models are a plausible local baseline that should be less sensitive to renamed identifiers. |
| IVDetect - Vulnerability Detection with Fine-Grained Interpretations | Yi Li, Shaohua Wang, Tien N. Nguyen | ESEC/FSE 2021 | [arXiv:2106.10478](https://arxiv.org/abs/2106.10478); [GitHub: vulnerabilitydetection/VulnerabilityDetectionResearch](https://github.com/vulnerabilitydetection/VulnerabilityDetectionResearch) | [related-work] Related work on explainable, statement-level localisation, which CodeKavach needs for report evidence snippets. |
| SySeVR: A Framework for Using Deep Learning to Detect Software Vulnerabilities | Zhen Li, Deqing Zou, Shouhuai Xu et al. | IEEE TDSC 19(4), 2022 - early access 2021; arXiv 1807.06756 (2018) | [arXiv:1807.06756](https://arxiv.org/abs/1807.06756); [GitHub: SySeVR/SySeVR](https://github.com/SySeVR/SySeVR) | [related-work] Related work for slice-based representations (syntax candidate -> semantic slice is the same two-step shape as CodeKavach's candidate finding -> taint slice). |
| CodeBERT: A Pre-Trained Model for Programming and Natural Languages | Zhangyin Feng, Daya Guo, Duyu Tang et al. | Findings of EMNLP 2020 | [arXiv:2002.08155](https://arxiv.org/abs/2002.08155); [GitHub: microsoft/CodeBERT](https://github.com/microsoft/CodeBERT) | [related-work] Library option for local embeddings (finding de-duplication, similar-code retrieval for Vul-RAG-style knowledge lookup) without sending code out. |
| VulDeePecker: A Deep Learning-Based System for Vulnerability Detection | Zhen Li, Deqing Zou, Shouhuai Xu et al. | NDSS 2018 | [arXiv:1801.01681](https://arxiv.org/abs/1801.01681); [GitHub: CGCL-codes/VulDeePecker](https://github.com/CGCL-codes/VulDeePecker) | [baseline] [related-work] Historical baseline; its code-gadget idea is an ancestor of CodeKavach's vulnerability-relevant slice. |

| Uncovering the Limits of Machine Learning for Automatic Vulnerability Detection | Niklas Risse, Marcel Böhme | USENIX Security 2024 | [USENIX](https://www.usenix.org/conference/usenixsecurity24/presentation/risse); [arXiv:2306.17193](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.17193) | [inspiration] [related-work] Design inspiration for the experiment: CodeKavach's L2 pseudonymisation is a semantic-preserving transformation, so this paper supplies both the methodology (transform-then-test) and the expected failure mode to measure. |
| Top Score on the Wrong Exam: On Benchmarking in Machine Learning for Vulnerability Detection | Niklas Risse, Jing Liu, Marcel Böhme | ISSTA 2025 - Proc. ACM Softw. Eng. 2(ISSTA) | [arXiv:2408.12986](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.12986); [doi:10.1145/3728887](https://doi.org/10.1145/3728887) | [related-work] Justifies L3's design choice of sending a context-bearing taint slice rather than a lone function, and warns against reporting function-level F1 as the headline metric. |
| Data Quality for Software Vulnerability Datasets | Roland Croft, M. Ali Babar, Mehdi Kholoosi | ICSE 2023 | [arXiv:2301.05456](https://arxiv.org/abs/2301.05456) | [evaluation] [inspiration] Checklist for the evaluation chapter: de-duplicate, manually validate a sample, report label accuracy. |
| Revisiting the Performance of Deep Learning-Based Vulnerability Detection on Realistic Datasets (Real-Vul) | Partha Chakraborty, Krishna Kanth Arumugam, Mahmoud Alfadel et al. | IEEE TSE 2024 | [arXiv:2407.03093](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.03093) | [evaluation] [related-work] Supports evaluating on whole repositories with realistic base rates; cite when explaining expected precision in a bank audit setting. |
| An Empirical Study of Deep Learning Models for Vulnerability Detection | Benjamin Steenhoek, Md Mahbubur Rahman, Richard Jiles, Wei Le | ICSE 2023 | [arXiv:2212.08109](https://arxiv.org/abs/2212.08109); [doi:10.1109/ICSE48619.2023.00188](https://doi.org/10.1109/ICSE48619.2023.00188) | [evaluation] [related-work] Justifies reporting run-to-run variance and inter-model agreement in the evaluation, not single-run F1; useful when comparing several LLM providers at each privacy level. |

### 1.2 LLM and static-analysis hybrids, false-positive triage

The closest prior work to the CodeKavach pipeline: a deterministic analyser proposes candidates and an LLM triages, specifies or explains them.

**LLM plus static analysis**

| Paper | Authors | Venue/Year | Link | Relevance to CodeKavach |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Vul-RAG: Enhancing LLM-based Vulnerability Detection via Knowledge-level RAG | Xueying Du, Geng Zheng, Kaixin Wang et al. | ACM TOSEM 35(10), 2026 | [arXiv:2406.11147](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11147); [GitHub: KnowledgeRAG4LLMVulD/KnowledgeRAG4LLMVulD](https://github.com/KnowledgeRAG4LLMVulD/KnowledgeRAG4LLMVulD) | [inspiration] [related-work] Integrate the idea: a local CWE/CVE knowledge store supplies vulnerability knowledge in the prompt, so the pseudonymised slice needs fewer natural-language hints. Retrieval happens locally, so nothing extra leaks. |
| BugLens - The Hitchhiker's Guide to Program Analysis, Part II: Deep Thoughts by LLMs | Haonan Li, Hang Zhang, Kexin Pei, Zhiyun Qian | ASE 2025 | [arXiv:2504.11711](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.11711); [GitHub: seclab-ucr/BugLens](https://github.com/seclab-ucr/BugLens) | [inspiration] [related-work] Blueprint for the triage stage: structured reasoning steps (is the sink security-relevant? are the path constraints satisfiable? are there sanitisers?) rather than a single 'is this a bug?' prompt. |
| IRIS: LLM-Assisted Static Analysis for Detecting Security Vulnerabilities | Ziyang Li, Saikat Dutta, Mayur Naik | ICLR 2025 | [arXiv:2405.17238](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.17238); [GitHub: iris-sast/iris](https://github.com/iris-sast/iris) | [baseline] [inspiration] Primary design inspiration and baseline. CodeKavach mirrors the spec-inference + path-triage split but must (a) swap CodeQL for OSS engines because CodeQL's terms restrict analysing proprietary code without a GitHub licence, and (b) pseudonymise API names/paths before the LLM sees them. |
| LLMxCPG: Context-Aware Vulnerability Detection Through Code Property Graph-Guided Large Language Models | Ahmed Lekssays, Hamza Mouhcine, Khang Tran et al. | USENIX Security 2025 | [arXiv:2507.16585](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.16585); [USENIX](https://www.usenix.org/conference/usenixsecurity25/presentation/lekssays) | [inspiration] [related-work] Strongest external evidence for the L3 hypothesis: a CPG slice is not only smaller (less leakage) but can be more accurate than whole-function input. Reuse its slice-construction recipe on Joern; extend by pseudonymising the slice and measuring leakage, which the paper does not address. |
| LLift - The Hitchhiker's Guide to Program Analysis: A Journey with Large Language Models | Haonan Li, Yu Hao, Yizhuo Zhai, Zhiyun Qian (UC Riverside) | OOPSLA 2024 (published as 'Enhancing Static Analysis for Practical Bug Detection: An LLM-Integrated Approach') | [arXiv:2308.00245](https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.00245); [doi:10.1145/3649828](https://doi.org/10.1145/3649828) | [inspiration] [related-work] Prompt-engineering template for triage: decompose the question, feed function summaries on demand, ask for self-check. The 'progressive prompt' idea maps to L3 (send more slice only if the model asks). |
| LLMDFA: Analyzing Dataflow in Code with Large Language Models | Chengpeng Wang, Wuqi Zhang, Zian Su et al. | NeurIPS 2024 | [arXiv:2402.10754](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10754); [GitHub: chengpeng-wang/LLMDFA](https://github.com/chengpeng-wang/LLMDFA) | [inspiration] [related-work] Design inspiration for analysing code that does not build (common in audits) and for L4: LLMDFA's function summaries are an abstract IR that could be exchanged instead of code. Licence is non-commercial: study, do not embed. |
| Bridging Code Property Graphs and Language Models for Program Analysis (codebadger) | Ahmed Lekssays (QCRI) | Software Vulnerability Management Workshop @ ICSE 2026 | [arXiv:2603.24837](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.24837); [GitHub: qcri/codebadger](https://github.com/qcri/codebadger) | [inspiration] [related-work] Design inspiration for the L4 mode: the LLM asks questions of a local analysis server and receives answers rather than code. CodeKavach can place its pseudonymisation filter on exactly this tool-call boundary. GPL-3.0 means take the idea, not the code. |
| Evident - The Hitchhiker's Guide to Program Analysis, Part III: Mostly Harmless LLMs | Haonan Li, Tianyang Zhou, Manu Sridharan et al. | arXiv preprint, 2026 | [arXiv:2606.15122](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.15122) | [inspiration] [related-work] Policy input: CodeKavach should never let the LLM silently suppress a finding; LLM 'false positive' verdicts are downgraded/annotated, not deleted, unless a deterministic check agrees. |
| NESA (formerly LLMSA): Relational Neuro-Symbolic Static Program Analysis | Chengpeng Wang, Yifei Gao, Wuqi Zhang et al. | Proc. ACM Softw. Eng. 3(FSE), FSE 2026; arXiv 2412.14399 | [arXiv:2412.14399](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.14399); [doi:10.1145/3808161](https://doi.org/10.1145/3808161) | [related-work] Strong candidate model for L4 'abstract-IR-only questions': each LLM query is a tiny relational question over a snippet, which minimises what leaves the premises. |
| Towards Scalable and Cost-Efficient Vulnerability Detection: A Study on Automatic Query Generation | Ivana Clairine Irsan, Ratnadira Widyasari, Huihui Huang et al. | arXiv preprint (Sep 2026) | [arXiv:2609.10412](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.10412) | [related-work] Supports a privacy-friendly design option: the LLM only ever sees public CVE text and emits a rule, and the rule runs locally - no client code leaves. Complements QLCoder. |
| KNighter: Transforming Static Analysis with LLM-Synthesized Checkers | Chenyuan Yang, Zijie Zhao, Zichen Xie et al. | SOSP 2025 | [arXiv:2503.09002](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09002); [GitHub: ise-uiuc/KNighter](https://github.com/ise-uiuc/KNighter) | [related-work] Privacy-friendly pattern: the LLM sees only public patches/rule descriptions and emits a checker that runs locally on the client's code - an L4-compatible workflow for custom Semgrep/tree-sitter rules. |
| RepoAudit: An Autonomous LLM-Agent for Repository-Level Code Auditing | Jinyao Guo, Chengpeng Wang, Xiangzhe Xu et al. | ICML 2025 | [arXiv:2501.18160](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.18160); [GitHub: PurCL/RepoAudit](https://github.com/PurCL/RepoAudit) | [inspiration] [related-work] Architecture reference for demand-driven, function-at-a-time exploration, the access pattern that keeps the amount of code revealed per query small. Provides cost and precision numbers to compare with CodeKavach's L3 mode. |
| GPTScan: Detecting Logic Vulnerabilities in Smart Contracts by Combining GPT with Program Analysis | Yuqiang Sun, Daoyuan Wu, Yue Xue et al. | ICSE 2024 | [arXiv:2308.03314](https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.03314); [GitHub: GPTScan/GPTScan](https://github.com/GPTScan/GPTScan) | [inspiration] [related-work] Pattern for business-logic findings: LLM proposes, static check confirms. Solidity itself is out of scope; AGPL means no code reuse. |
| LLM4Vuln: A Unified Evaluation Framework for Decoupling and Enhancing LLMs' Vulnerability Reasoning | Yuqiang Sun, Daoyuan Wu, Yue Xue et al. | arXiv preprint, 2024 | [arXiv:2401.16185](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.16185) | [related-work] Experimental design template: CodeKavach's ablation should vary privacy level, knowledge injection and context size independently in the same factorial style. |
| Can Open-Source LLM Agents Replace Static Application Security Testing Tools? An Empirical Assessment | Derek Yohn, Luke Flancher, Mirajul Islam, Khaled Slhoub | arXiv preprint, 2026 | [arXiv:2606.11672](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11672) | [related-work] Supports the hybrid design (deterministic engines first, LLM second) and sets expectations for the self-hosted/air-gapped deployment where only open models are available - one of CodeKavach's headline configurations. |
| Artemis: Toward Accurate Detection of Server-Side Request Forgeries through LLM-Assisted Inter-Procedural Path-Sensitive Taint Analysis | Yuchen Ji, Ting Dai, Zhichao Zhou et al. | OOPSLA 2025 - Proc. ACM Program. Lang. 9(OOPSLA1) | [arXiv:2502.21026](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.21026); [doi:10.1145/3720488](https://doi.org/10.1145/3720488) | [related-work] PHP-specific reference for the hybrid pattern (LLM for API specification, deterministic engine for propagation). Only API names go to the model, which is close to what L3/L4 would allow anyway. |
| QLCoder: A Query Synthesizer for Static Analysis of Security Vulnerabilities | Claire Wang, Ziyang Li, Saikat Dutta, Mayur Naik (UPenn) | arXiv preprint, 2025 | [arXiv:2511.08462](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.08462) | [related-work] Same 'LLM writes the rule, rule runs locally' idea as KNighter but for taint queries; the vulnerable-vs-patched validation oracle is reusable for CodeKavach's rule-authoring assistant. |
| Chain-of-Thought Prompting of LLMs for Discovering and Fixing Software Vulnerabilities (VSP) | Yu Nong, Mohammed Aldeen, Long Cheng et al. | arXiv preprint, 2024 | [arXiv:2402.17230](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.17230) | [inspiration] [related-work] Prompt template for L3, where the payload already is the vulnerability-relevant statements. |
| Interleaving Static Analysis and LLM Prompting | Patrick J. Chapman, Cindy Rubio-González, Aditya V. Thakur | SOAP 2024 (13th ACM SIGPLAN Int. Workshop on the State Of the Art in Program Analysis); journal extension in STTT 2025 | [doi:10.1145/3652588.3663317](https://doi.org/10.1145/3652588.3663317); [doi:10.1007/s10009-025-00780-7](https://doi.org/10.1007/s10009-025-00780-7) | [inspiration] [related-work] Design pattern for small, targeted LLM queries issued from inside the analysis rather than shipping whole files - naturally compatible with minimal-disclosure prompts. |
| E&V: Prompting Large Language Models to Perform Static Analysis by Pseudo-code Execution and Verification | Yu Hao, Weiteng Chen, Ziqiao Zhou, Weidong Cui | arXiv preprint, 2023 | [arXiv:2312.08477](https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.08477) | [related-work] Evidence that verification loops, not bigger prompts, are what make LLM-driven data-flow reasoning usable; relevant to how much context the triage agent may request under L3. |
| GPTLens - Large Language Model-Powered Smart Contract Vulnerability Detection: New Perspectives | Sihao Hu, Tiansheng Huang, Fatih İlhan et al. | IEEE TPS-ISA 2023 | [arXiv:2310.01152](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.01152); [GitHub: git-disl/GPTLens](https://github.com/git-disl/GPTLens) | [inspiration] [related-work] Design inspiration for a generate-then-criticise triage loop; the critic can run on a cheaper or local model. |

**LLM triage of static-analysis alerts**

| Paper | Authors | Venue/Year | Link | Relevance to CodeKavach |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ZeroFalse: Improving Precision in Static Analysis with LLMs | Mohsen Iranmanesh, Sina Moradi Sabet, Sina Marefat et al. | arXiv preprint, 2025 | [arXiv:2510.02534](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.02534) | [inspiration] [related-work] Near-identical task framing to CodeKavach triage (SARIF in, verdict out). Reuse its CWE-specialised prompt idea and compare headline F1 at L1 vs. L3. |
| LLM4FPM - Utilizing Precise and Complete Code Context to Guide LLM in Automatic False Positive Mitigation | Jinbao Chen, Hongjing Xiang, Zuohong Zhao et al. | arXiv preprint, 2024 | [arXiv:2411.03079](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.03079) | [related-work] Direct input to slicer design - 'precise and complete but minimal' context is both the accuracy lever and the privacy lever. |
| Reducing False Positives in Static Bug Detection with LLMs: An Empirical Study in Industry | Xueying Du, Jiayi Feng, Yi Zou et al. | ICSE-SEIP 2026; arXiv 2601.18844 | [arXiv:2601.18844](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.18844); [doi:10.1145/3786583.3786910](https://doi.org/10.1145/3786583.3786910) | [related-work] Business case numbers for the report/paper introduction, and evidence that results transfer to enterprise code the LLM has never seen (as with a bank's code base). |
| Sifting the Noise: A Comparative Study of LLM Agents in Vulnerability False Positive Filtering | Yunpeng Xiong, Ting Zhang | ISSTA 2026 (to appear; announced on arXiv but not yet registered in Crossref) | [arXiv:2601.22952](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22952) | [baseline] [related-work] Baseline for 'agent with full repo access' - the upper bound on context that L3 slices compete with; also provides contamination-aware methodology. |
| Using LLMs to Adjudicate Static-Analysis Alerts with Error Reduction Techniques | William Klieber, David Svoboda, Lori Flynn, Ruben Martins (CMU SEI) | arXiv preprint (Jul 2026); earlier short version at HICSS 2025 | [arXiv:2607.09979](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09979); [doi:10.24251/hicss.2025.903](https://doi.org/10.24251/hicss.2025.903) | [inspiration] [related-work] Adopt self-consistency voting and 'abstain when inconsistent' to give each CodeKavach verdict a confidence level in the report. |
| LLM4PFA - Minimizing False Positives in Static Bug Detection via LLM-Enhanced Path Feasibility Analysis | Xueying Du, Kai Yu, Chong Wang et al. | arXiv preprint, 2025 | [arXiv:2506.10322](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10322) | [inspiration] [related-work] Technique to adopt for taint-path findings: constraints, not code, are what the solver needs - compatible with L4. |
| LLM4SA - Automatically Inspecting Thousands of Static Bug Warnings with Large Language Model: How Far Are We? | Cheng Wen, Yuandao Cai, Bin Zhang et al. | ACM TKDD 2024 | [doi:10.1145/3653718](https://doi.org/10.1145/3653718) | [baseline] [related-work] Earliest full 'alert -> slice -> LLM verdict' pipeline; baseline design for CodeKavach triage and for slice construction choices. |
| Detecting False Alarms from Automatic Static Analysis Tools: How Far are We? | Hong Jin Kang, Khai Loong Aw, David Lo | ICSE 2022 | [arXiv:2202.05982](https://arxiv.org/abs/2202.05982); [GitHub: soarsmu/SA\_retrospective](https://github.com/soarsmu/SA_retrospective) | [evaluation] [related-work] Methodological warning for the triage evaluation: de-duplicate alerts across splits, keep ground-truth-derived signals out of prompts/features, and always report a trivial baseline next to LLM triage results. |
| AdaTaint - LLM-Driven Adaptive Source-Sink Identification and False Positive Mitigation for Static Analysis | Shiyin Lin | CISAI 2025 (8th Int. Conf. on Computer Information Science and Artificial Intelligence), ACM ICPS; arXiv 2511.04023. Single-author paper in a low-selectivity venue - treat claims with caution | [arXiv:2511.04023](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.04023); [doi:10.1145/3773365.3773410](https://doi.org/10.1145/3773365.3773410) | [related-work] Reference for combining spec inference with FP filtering; treat the quantitative claims cautiously. |
| Towards Effective Complementary Security Analysis using Large Language Models | Jonas Wagner, Simon Müller, Christian Näther et al. | IEEE ISI 2025; arXiv 2506.16899 | [arXiv:2506.16899](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.16899); [doi:10.1109/ISI65680.2025.11201186](https://doi.org/10.1109/ISI65680.2025.11201186) | [related-work] The 'never lose a true positive' constraint is the right operating point for an audit tool; reuse as the triage acceptance criterion. |
| FuzzSlice: Pruning False Positives in Static Analysis Warnings Through Function-Level Fuzzing | Aniruddhan Murali, Noble Saji Mathews, Mahmoud Alfadel et al. | ICSE 2024 | [arXiv:2402.01923](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.01923); [doi:10.1145/3597503.3623321](https://doi.org/10.1145/3597503.3623321) | [related-work] Fully local (nothing leaves the machine) alternative to LLM triage for C/C++ memory warnings, and another consumer of the same slice extractor - a useful comparison arm in the privacy-utility study, where it sits at zero leakage. |
| Mitigating False Positive Static Analysis Warnings: Progress, Challenges, and Opportunities | Zhaoqiang Guo, Tingting Tan, Shiran Liu et al. | IEEE TSE 49(12), 2023 | [doi:10.1109/TSE.2023.3329667](https://doi.org/10.1109/TSE.2023.3329667) | [related-work] Related-work backbone for the triage chapter and a taxonomy for positioning LLM triage against classic ranking/pruning methods. |
| SkipAnalyzer: A Tool for Static Code Analysis with Large Language Models | Mohammad Mahdi Mohajer, Reem Aleithan, Nima Shiri Harzevili et al. | arXiv preprint, 2023 | [arXiv:2310.18532](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.18532) | [baseline] [related-work] Baseline for the triage step on Java resource-leak/NPE warnings (pairs with JLeaks). |
| Learning to Reduce False Positives in Analytic Bug Detectors | Anant Kharkar, Roshanak Zilouchian Moghaddam, Matthew Jin et al. | ICSE 2022 | [arXiv:2203.09907](https://arxiv.org/abs/2203.09907); [doi:10.1145/3510003.3510153](https://doi.org/10.1145/3510003.3510153) | [baseline] [related-work] Earliest neural baseline for the "analyser proposes, model filters" design; cite it to show the triage idea predates chat LLMs and to position CodeKavach against fine-tuned local classifiers, which need no code to leave the premises. |
| A Systematic Literature Review of Actionable Alert Identification Techniques for Automated Static Code Analysis | Sarah Heckman, Laurie Williams | Information and Software Technology 53(4), 2011 | [doi:10.1016/j.infsof.2010.12.007](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.infsof.2010.12.007) | [related-work] Historical anchor showing that alert triage is a long-standing problem; supplies vocabulary (actionable vs. unactionable alerts) for the paper. |

| Item | Author / publisher | Year | Link | Relevance to CodeKavach |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Semgrep Assistant triage-accuracy reports | Semgrep, Inc. | Vendor blog, 2025 | [semgrep.dev](https://semgrep.dev/blog/2025/building-an-appsec-ai-that-security-researchers-agree-with-96-of-the-time/); [related page](https://semgrep.dev/blog/2025/making-zero-false-positive-sast-a-reality-with-ai-powered-memory/) | [baseline] [inspiration] Industry baseline and feature inspiration (triage memories = local suppression knowledge base). Figures are self-reported by a vendor and should be cited as such. |

### 1.3 Automated code review and static analysis in practice

How code review and static analysis work in industry, what reviewers miss, and what ML-based review automation has achieved.

**Automated code review with ML and LLMs**

| Paper | Authors | Venue/Year | Link | Relevance to CodeKavach |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| An Insight into Security Code Review with LLMs: Capabilities, Obstacles, and Influential Factors | Jiaxin Yu, Peng Liang, Yujia Fu et al. | arXiv preprint, 2024 | [arXiv:2401.16310](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.16310) | [related-work] Closest study to CodeKavach's task framing (security review, not generic review). Its finding that commit messages help is in tension with stripping natural language at L2 - a concrete utility cost to measure; reuse its response-quality analysis to grade LLM explanations. |
| BitsAI-CR: Automated Code Review via LLM in Practice | Tao Sun, Jian Xu, Yuanpeng Li et al. | FSE 2025 Industry | [arXiv:2501.15134](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.15134); [doi:10.1145/3696630.3728552](https://doi.org/10.1145/3696630.3728552) | [evaluation] [inspiration] Adopt the generate-then-verify structure and an acted-upon metric for the dashboard to measure real-world precision. |
| AutoCommenter - AI-Assisted Assessment of Coding Practices in Modern Code Review | Manushree Vijayvergiya, Małgorzata Salawa, Ivan Budiselić et al. | AIware 2024 | [arXiv:2405.13565](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.13565); [doi:10.1145/3664646.3665664](https://doi.org/10.1145/3664646.3665664) | [related-work] Deployment lessons: link each comment to an authoritative rule (CodeKavach: CWE/OWASP/ASVS reference), prefer precision, and collect thumbs-up/down feedback. |
| CodeReviewer - Automating Code Review Activities by Large-Scale Pre-training | Zhiyu Li, Shuai Lu, Daya Guo et al. | ESEC/FSE 2022 | [arXiv:2203.09095](https://arxiv.org/abs/2203.09095); [GitHub: microsoft/CodeBERT](https://github.com/microsoft/CodeBERT/tree/master/CodeReviewer) | [baseline] [related-work] Dataset/baseline for review-comment wording; informs how findings are phrased as PR comments and GitHub issues. |
| A Survey of Code Review Benchmarks and Evaluation Practices in Pre-LLM and LLM Era | Taufiqul Islam Khan, Shaowei Wang, Haoxiang Zhang, Tse-Hsun Chen | arXiv preprint, 2026 | [arXiv:2602.13377](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.13377) | [evaluation] [related-work] Pointer list for review datasets and metrics. |
| MetaMateCR - AI-Assisted Fixes to Code Review Comments at Scale | Chandra Maddila, Negar Ghorbani, James Saindon et al. | FSE 2026 (34th ACM Int. Conf. on the Foundations of Software Engineering, industry/companion); arXiv 2507.13499 | [arXiv:2507.13499](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.13499); [doi:10.1145/3803437.3805217](https://doi.org/10.1145/3803437.3805217) | [related-work] Evidence that fine-tuned open-weight models can beat general frontier models on in-house review data; supports the self-hosted deployment story. |
| Automated Code Review In Practice | Umut Cihan, Vahid Haratian, Arda İçöz et al. | ICSE-SEIP 2025 | [arXiv:2412.18531](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.18531); [doi:10.1109/ICSE-SEIP66354.2025.00043](https://doi.org/10.1109/ICSE-SEIP66354.2025.00043) | [inspiration] [related-work] Adoption-risk input: noise costs time; supports batching findings into a report/Kanban rather than spamming PR comments. |
| Code Review Automation: Strengths and Weaknesses of the State of the Art | Rosalia Tufano, Ozren Dabić, Antonio Mastropaolo et al. | IEEE TSE 2024 | [arXiv:2401.05136](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.05136); [doi:10.1109/TSE.2023.3348172](https://doi.org/10.1109/TSE.2023.3348172) | [related-work] Sets expectations and supplies failure categories for the limitations section. |
| Resolving Code Review Comments with Machine Learning | Alexander Froemmgen, Jacob Austin, Peter Choy et al. | ICSE-SEIP 2024 | [doi:10.1145/3639477.3639746](https://doi.org/10.1145/3639477.3639746); [research.google](https://research.google/blog/resolving-code-review-comments-with-ml/) | [related-work] Out of scope for v1 (no auto-fix) but informs remediation-guidance text and the future roadmap. |
| LLaMA-Reviewer: Advancing Code Review Automation with Large Language Models through Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning | Junyi Lu, Lei Yu, Xiaojia Li et al. | ISSRE 2023 | [arXiv:2308.11148](https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.11148) | [related-work] Evidence that a self-hosted model can be cheaply adapted for review-style output in air-gapped deployments. |

**Code review practice and security review**

| Paper | Authors | Venue/Year | Link | Relevance to CodeKavach |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Why Don't Software Developers Use Static Analysis Tools to Find Bugs? | Brittany Johnson, Yoonki Song, Emerson Murphy-Hill, Robert Bowdidge | ICSE 2013 | [doi:10.1109/ICSE.2013.6606613](https://doi.org/10.1109/ICSE.2013.6606613) | [inspiration] [related-work] Requirements source: every CodeKavach finding must carry an explanation, evidence snippet and remediation, and triage must cut noise before anything reaches a developer. |
| Why Security Defects Go Unnoticed during Code Reviews? A Case-Control Study of the Chromium OS Project | Rajshakhar Paul, Asif Kamal Turzo, Amiangshu Bosu | ICSE 2021, pp. 1373-1385 | [doi:10.1109/ICSE43902.2021.00124](https://doi.org/10.1109/ICSE43902.2021.00124) | [related-work] Evidence for which weakness classes human reviewers miss, which helps prioritise where LLM assistance adds value, and a companion dataset of vulnerable code changes usable for diff-mode evaluation. |
| Lessons from Building Static Analysis Tools at Google | Caitlin Sadowski, Edward Aftandilian, Alex Eagle et al. | Communications of the ACM 61(4), 2018 | [doi:10.1145/3188720](https://doi.org/10.1145/3188720) | [related-work] Supports shipping CI/PR integration first and treating the dashboard as secondary; criteria reused for default rule-pack selection. |
| Tricorder: Building a Program Analysis Ecosystem | Caitlin Sadowski, Jeffrey van Gogh, Ciera Jaspan et al. | ICSE 2015 | [doi:10.1109/ICSE.2015.76](https://doi.org/10.1109/ICSE.2015.76); [research.google](https://research.google/pubs/tricorder-building-a-program-analysis-ecosystem/) | [inspiration] [related-work] Architecture inspiration for the plugin system and for a per-rule effective-false-positive budget with feedback buttons. |
| An Empirical Study on the Effectiveness of Security Code Review | Anne Edmundson, Brian Holtkamp, Emanuel Rivera et al. | ESSoS 2013 (LNCS 7781), pp. 197-212 | [doi:10.1007/978-3-642-36563-8\_14](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-642-36563-8_14) | [evaluation] [related-work] Supports the claim that manual security review has low and highly variable recall, motivating tool assistance; also a precedent for seeded-vulnerability evaluation on realistic (non-synthetic) code. |
| A Few Billion Lines of Code Later: Using Static Analysis to Find Bugs in the Real World | Al Bessey, Ken Block, Ben Chelf et al. | Communications of the ACM 53(2), 2010 | [doi:10.1145/1646353.1646374](https://doi.org/10.1145/1646353.1646374) | [related-work] Practical warnings for ingesting messy client repositories (non-building code, odd dialects) - a reason to rely on tree-sitter and compilation-free analysis. |
| Software Security (touchpoints) and Static Analysis for Security | Gary McGraw; Brian Chess and Gary McGraw | IEEE Security & Privacy 2(2) and 2(6), 2004 | [doi:10.1109/MSECP.2004.1281254](https://doi.org/10.1109/MSECP.2004.1281254); [doi:10.1109/MSP.2004.111](https://doi.org/10.1109/MSP.2004.111) | [related-work] Frames CodeKavach's scope honestly: code review finds implementation bugs, while architectural flaws need the LLM's reasoning or a separate risk analysis; cite in methodology annex. |
| Security Defect Detection via Code Review: A Study of the OpenStack and Qt Communities | Jiaxin Yu, Liming Fu, Peng Liang et al. | ESEM 2023 | [doi:10.1109/ESEM56168.2023.10304852](https://doi.org/10.1109/ESEM56168.2023.10304852); [arXiv:2307.02326](https://arxiv.org/abs/2307.02326) | [related-work] Recent quantitative support for the gap CodeKavach fills in PR review, and a model for the GitHub-issue text: reviewers' comments that included an explicit fix strategy were the useful ones. |
| Do I really need all this work to find vulnerabilities? An empirical case study comparing vulnerability detection techniques on a Java application | Sarah Elder, Nusrat Zahan, Rui Shu et al. | Empirical Software Engineering 27(6), 2022 | [doi:10.1007/s10664-022-10179-6](https://doi.org/10.1007/s10664-022-10179-6) | [evaluation] [related-work] Supports honest scoping language in reports (static review complements rather than replaces VAPT) and offers a realistic cost-effectiveness evaluation design to emulate. |
| Less is More: Supporting Developers in Vulnerability Detection during Code Review | Larissa Braz, Christian Aeberhard, Gül Çalikli, Alberto Bacchelli | ICSE 2022 | [arXiv:2202.04586](https://arxiv.org/abs/2202.04586); [doi:10.1145/3510003.3511560](https://doi.org/10.1145/3510003.3511560) | [inspiration] [related-work] Prompt-design analogue for LLM reviewers: a focused security instruction may beat an exhaustive checklist; test both prompt styles. |
| Why Don't Developers Detect Improper Input Validation? '; DROP TABLE Papers; -- | Larissa Braz, Enrico Fregnan, Gül Çalikli, Alberto Bacchelli | ICSE 2021 | [doi:10.1109/ICSE43902.2021.00054](https://doi.org/10.1109/ICSE43902.2021.00054); [arXiv:2102.06251](https://arxiv.org/abs/2102.06251) | [inspiration] [related-work] Design evidence for how findings should be presented: attach a concrete exploit scenario and point the reviewer at the location, rather than emitting a bare rule ID - this is what the LLM triage explanation adds over raw SAST output. |
| Scaling Static Analyses at Facebook | Dino Distefano, Manuel Fahndrich, Francesco Logozzo, Peter W. O'Hearn | Communications of the ACM 62(8), 2019 | [doi:10.1145/3338112](https://doi.org/10.1145/3338112) | [evaluation] [inspiration] Adopt 'fix rate' / 'acted-upon rate' as a product metric, and compositional summaries as the scalable basis for slicing. |
| Modern Code Review: A Case Study at Google | Caitlin Sadowski, Emma Soderberg, Luke Church et al. | ICSE-SEIP 2018 | [doi:10.1145/3183519.3183525](https://doi.org/10.1145/3183519.3183525) | [inspiration] [related-work] UX reference for the dashboard and PR integration (small, actionable, in-context comments). |
| A Security Perspective on Code Review: The Case of Chromium | Marco di Biase, Magiel Bruntink, Alberto Bacchelli | IEEE SCAM 2016, pp. 21-30 | [doi:10.1109/SCAM.2016.30](https://doi.org/10.1109/SCAM.2016.30) | [baseline] [related-work] Baseline for the claim that human review rarely surfaces security problems unaided, motivating an automated security reviewer in the PR loop; pairs with Paul et al. (ICSE 2021) on Chromium OS. |
| Identifying the Characteristics of Vulnerable Code Changes: An Empirical Study | Amiangshu Bosu, Jeffrey C. Carver, Munawar Hafiz et al. | FSE 2014, pp. 257-268 | [doi:10.1145/2635868.2635880](https://doi.org/10.1145/2635868.2635880) | [related-work] Evidence for PR-mode prioritisation heuristics (spend LLM budget on large changes, modified-rather-than-new files, less experienced authors) and a precedent for building a security-review dataset from review comments. |
| Expectations, Outcomes, and Challenges of Modern Code Review | Alberto Bacchelli, Christian Bird | ICSE 2013 | [doi:10.1109/ICSE.2013.6606617](https://doi.org/10.1109/ICSE.2013.6606617); [microsoft.com](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/publication/expectations-outcomes-and-challenges-of-modern-code-review/) | [related-work] Motivates explanation-rich findings (why this is a bug, data-flow narrative) over bare alerts. |
| Seven Pernicious Kingdoms: A Taxonomy of Software Security Errors | Katrina Tsipenyuk, Brian Chess, Gary McGraw | IEEE Security & Privacy 3(6), pp. 81-84 (Nov/Dec 2005) | [doi:10.1109/MSP.2005.159](https://doi.org/10.1109/MSP.2005.159); [cwe.mitre.org](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/700.html) | [related-work] Related-work citation for taxonomy design and a simple top-level grouping that maps cleanly to which analysis technique is needed (taint vs. typestate vs. configuration), useful when explaining which finding classes survive slicing at L3. |
| Design and Code Inspections to Reduce Errors in Program Development | Michael E. Fagan (IBM) | IBM Systems Journal 15(3), 1976 | [doi:10.1147/sj.153.0182](https://doi.org/10.1147/sj.153.0182) | [inspiration] [related-work] Methodology root for the 'Big-Four-style' audit process; CodeKavach's report stages (planning, individual analysis, review, rework tracking) follow the inspection model. |

**Empirical studies of SAST tool effectiveness**

| Paper | Authors | Venue/Year | Link | Relevance to CodeKavach |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| An Empirical Study of Static Analysis Tools for Secure Code Review | Wachiraphan Charoenwet, Patanamon Thongtanunam, Van-Thuan Pham, Christoph Treude | ISSTA 2024 | [arXiv:2407.12241](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.12241); [doi:10.1145/3650212.3680313](https://doi.org/10.1145/3650212.3680313) | [related-work] Directly on-topic (secure code review): supports the design of using SAST output as a prioritisation signal that an LLM then reasons over, rather than as ground truth. Its commit-level dataset is usable for CI-mode evaluation. |
| Comparison and Evaluation on Static Application Security Testing (SAST) Tools for Java | Kaixuan Li, Sen Chen, Lingling Fan et al. | ESEC/FSE 2023 | [doi:10.1145/3611643.3616262](https://doi.org/10.1145/3611643.3616262) | [evaluation] [inspiration] Evaluation template: reuse its CWE-mapping methodology and its real-CVE Java benchmark for the accuracy axis of the privacy-utility curve; cite its low real-world recall as the gap LLM reasoning is supposed to close. |
| An Empirical Study on the Effectiveness of Static C Code Analyzers for Vulnerability Detection | Stephan Lipp, Sebastian Banescu, Alexander Pretschner | ISSTA 2022 | [doi:10.1145/3533767.3534380](https://doi.org/10.1145/3533767.3534380) | [evaluation] [inspiration] Key motivation and evaluation design input: (1) justifies multi-engine candidate generation, (2) warns that Juliet-style synthetic results overstate recall, so CodeKavach's privacy-utility evaluation must include real CVE datasets. |
| An Extensive Comparison of Static Application Security Testing Tools | Matteo Esposito, Valentina Falaschi, Davide Falessi | EASE 2024 (Int. Conf. on Evaluation and Assessment in Software Engineering) | [arXiv:2403.09219](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.09219); [doi:10.1145/3661167.3661199](https://doi.org/10.1145/3661167.3661199) | [related-work] Cite to nuance the 'SAST = too many false positives' narrative: the bigger problem may be misses, which argues for CodeKavach letting the LLM also inspect un-flagged security-relevant slices, not just triage alerts. |
| Study of JavaScript Static Analysis Tools for Vulnerability Detection in Node.js Packages | Tiago Brito, Mafalda Ferreira, Miguel Monteiro et al. | IEEE Transactions on Reliability 72(4), Dec 2023 | [arXiv:2301.05097](https://arxiv.org/abs/2301.05097); [doi:10.1109/TR.2023.3286301](https://doi.org/10.1109/TR.2023.3286301) | [evaluation] [related-work] Evidence for the JS/TS plugin choices and a ready-made ground-truth dataset for that language. |

|---|---|---|---|---|

Not a security methodology and not used for rule coverage. Licence not stated, so nothing is copied. |

### 1.4 Privacy-preserving prompting and sanitisation

Techniques for using a remote LLM without disclosing the sensitive parts of the prompt, the formal privacy notions behind them, and the incidents that motivate the privacy layer.

**Prompt sanitisation and privacy-preserving prompting**

| Paper | Authors | Venue/Year | Link | Relevance to CodeKavach |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Operationalizing Data Minimization for Privacy-Preserving LLM Prompting | Jijie Zhou, Niloofar Mireshghallah, Tianshi Li | ICLR 2026, 2025 | [arXiv:2510.03662](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.03662); [OpenReview](https://openreview.net/forum?id=rpcnvW33EG) | [related-work] Direct conceptual parent of L3 'minimal vulnerability-relevant slice'. CodeKavach can adopt the same formulation (privacy-ordered transformation lattice L1<L2<L3<L4, search for the lowest-disclosure level that keeps the triage verdict) and report per-model minimisation headroom. |
| Preempt (Pr-epsilon-epsilon-mpt): Sanitizing Sensitive Prompts for LLMs | Amrita Roy Chowdhury, David Glukhov, Divyam Anshumaan et al. | NDSS 2026 (arXiv 2025) | [arXiv:2504.05147](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.05147); [doi:10.14722/ndss.2026.231277](https://doi.org/10.14722/ndss.2026.231277) | [related-work] Strongest theoretical backing for CodeKavach's pseudonymisation: identifiers and string literals are 'format-only' tokens, so keyed FPE/HMAC renaming gives a cryptographic rather than heuristic claim. Cite for the L2 security argument and borrow the two-category token model. |
| PAPILLON: Privacy Preservation from Internet-based and Local Language Model Ensembles | Li Siyan, Vethavikashini Chithrra Raghuram, Omar Khattab et al. | NAACL 2025, 2024 | [arXiv:2410.17127](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.17127); [GitHub: Columbia-NLP-Lab/PAPILLON](https://github.com/Columbia-NLP-Lab/PAPILLON) | [evaluation] [inspiration] Design inspiration for L3/L4: a local (Ollama/vLLM) model decides what the remote model needs to see. Its two-axis metric (response quality preserved vs. leakage rate) is the template for CodeKavach's privacy-utility curves. |
| Hide and Seek (HaS): A Lightweight Framework for Prompt Privacy Protection | Yu Chen, Tingxin Li, Huiming Liu, Yang Yu | arXiv preprint, 2023 | [arXiv:2309.03057](https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.03057) | [inspiration] [related-work] Closest published analogue of CodeKavach's pseudonymise -> remote LLM -> local de-pseudonymise loop (L2/L3). Cite as the natural-language precedent and reuse its adversarial 'can the anonymised text be inverted' evaluation idea; CodeKavach replaces the learned hide/seek models with deterministic AST-level renaming plus a local mapping vault. |
| Large Language Models are Advanced Anonymizers | Robin Staab, Mark Vero, Mislav Balunović, Martin Vechev | ICLR 2025, 2024 | [arXiv:2402.13846](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.13846); [GitHub: eth-sri/llm-anonymization](https://github.com/eth-sri/llm-anonymization) | [evaluation] [inspiration] Blueprint for CodeKavach's leakage evaluator and an optional 'adversarial hardening' mode: run a local attacker model against the outgoing slice, and if it recovers domain/business meaning, tighten the transformation before sending. |
| ProSan: The Fire Thief Is Also the Keeper - Balancing Usability and Privacy in Prompts | Zhili Shen, Zihang Xi, Ying He et al. | arXiv preprint, 2024 | [arXiv:2406.14318](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.14318) | [baseline] [related-work] One of the few prompt-sanitisation papers that includes a code task, so it is a direct comparison point. The importance-vs-risk weighting informs a CodeKavach heuristic: keep security-relevant API names (sinks/sources), rename everything domain-specific. |
| LLM-Redactor: An Empirical Evaluation of Eight Techniques for Privacy-Preserving LLM Requests | Justice Owusu Agyemang, Jerry John Kponyo, Elliot Amponsah et al. | arXiv preprint, 2026 | [arXiv:2604.12064](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.12064); [GitHub: jayluxferro/llm-redactor](https://github.com/jayluxferro/llm-redactor) | [baseline] [related-work] Closest published measurement of text-level protection applied to coding-agent traffic. Compares local-only inference, redaction with placeholder restoration, semantic rephrasing, TEE, split inference, FHE, MPC and DP noise on a 1,300-sample labelled leak benchmark. The best practical combination leaks 0.6% of PII but 31.3% of proprietary-code items, which is direct evidence that L1-style redaction does not protect code and that a code-aware layer (L2, L3) is needed. Preprint; the repository shows no licence file, so compare against it and do not reuse code. |
| MaskSQL: Safeguarding Privacy for LLM-Based Text-to-SQL via Abstraction | Sepideh Abedini, Shubhankar Mohapatra, D. B. Emerson et al. | Workshop on Regulatable ML @ NeurIPS 2025 | [arXiv:2509.23459](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.23459) | [inspiration] [related-work] Nearest analogue of the L2 to L4 idea in another code-like domain: schema identifiers and literal values are replaced by abstract symbols before the hosted LLM sees the prompt, and the generated SQL is mapped back locally. Cite for the abstraction-versus-redaction-versus-generalisation distinction and as evidence that reversible abstraction keeps most task accuracy; it has no slicing and no attacker-based leakage measurement. |
| PlanTwin: Privacy-Preserving Planning Abstractions for Cloud-Assisted LLM Agents | Guangsheng Yu, Qin Wang, Rui Lang, Shuai Su, Xu Wang | arXiv preprint, 2026 | [arXiv:2603.18377](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.18377) | [inspiration] [related-work] A cloud planner sees only a schema-constrained, de-identified graph of a private local environment (source code, files, credentials), while a local gatekeeper executes. Design input for L4 (abstract facts only) and for the rule that each additional disclosure passes a local policy check; related work for the agentic variant of triage. |
| Poisoned Identifiers Survive LLM Deobfuscation: A Case Study on Claude Opus 4.6 | Luis Guzmán Lorenzo | arXiv preprint arXiv:2604.04289, 2026 | [arXiv:2604.04289](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.04289) | [evaluation] [inspiration] Threat-model input for the leakage metric: an 'attacker LLM' recovering identifiers is a realistic adversary, so L2 renaming alone is not a guarantee. |
| Minions: Cost-efficient Collaboration Between On-device and Cloud Language Models | Avanika Narayan, Dan Biderman, Sabri Eyuboglu et al. | ICML 2025 (PMLR 267) | [arXiv:2502.15964](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.15964); [proceedings.mlr.press](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v267/narayan25a.html) | [inspiration] [related-work] Architecture reference for L4: the remote LLM asks abstract questions ('is the value reaching sink X validated?') and a local model or the static analyser answers them, so no code leaves the client. |
| Casper: Prompt Sanitization for Protecting User Privacy in Web-Based Large Language Models | Chun Jie Chong, Chenxi Hou, Zhihao Yao, Seyed Mohammadjavad Seyed Talebi | IEEE CSCloud 2025 (arXiv 2024) | [arXiv:2408.07004](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.07004); [doi:10.1109/CSCloud66326.2025.00027](https://doi.org/10.1109/CSCloud66326.2025.00027) | [inspiration] [related-work] Architecture precedent for CodeKavach's layered L1 detector (regex/entropy secrets -> NER/PII -> optional local-LLM pass) and for keeping the placeholder map on the client. |
| EmojiPrompt (formerly EmojiCrypt): Generative Prompt Obfuscation for Privacy-Preserving Communication with Cloud-based LLMs | Sam Lin, Wenyue Hua, Zhenting Wang et al. | NAACL 2025 (arXiv v1 Feb 2024 as 'EmojiCrypt') | [arXiv:2402.05868](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.05868); [ACL Anthology](https://aclanthology.org/2025.naacl-long.614/) | [baseline] [related-work] Related-work baseline for 'lossy but still useful' obfuscation. |
| PrivacyRestore: Privacy-Preserving Inference in Large Language Models via Privacy Removal and Restoration | Ziqian Zeng, Jianwei Wang, Junyao Yang et al. | ACL 2025, 2024 | [arXiv:2406.01394](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.01394) | [related-work] Related work illustrating the 'remove locally, restore elsewhere' pattern; CodeKavach restores on the client instead. Useful contrast when arguing why a provider-agnostic tool must be black-box. |
| Rescriber: Smaller-LLM-Powered User-Led Data Minimization for LLM-Based Chatbots | Jijie Zhou, Eryue Xu, Yaoyao Wu, Tianshi Li | CHI 2025, 2024 | [arXiv:2410.11876](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.11876); [doi:10.1145/3706598.3713701](https://doi.org/10.1145/3706598.3713701) | [inspiration] [related-work] UX evidence for CodeKavach's 'preview exactly what will leave the machine' screen in the dashboard/VS Code extension, and for using a small local model as the detector. |
| ConfusionPrompt: Practical Private Inference for Online Large Language Models | Peihua Mai, Youjia Yang, Ran Yan et al. | arXiv preprint, 2023 | [arXiv:2401.00870](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.00870) | [inspiration] [related-work] Idea source for an optional CodeKavach hardening: interleave decoy slices (from open-source code) with real slices so the provider cannot tell which logic belongs to the client. |
| DP-Prompt: Locally Differentially Private Document Generation Using Zero Shot Prompting | Saiteja Utpala, Sara Hooker, Pin Yu Chen | Findings of EMNLP 2023 | [arXiv:2310.16111](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.16111) | [evaluation] [related-work] Evaluation template: attacker = authorship/attribute classifier, utility = downstream task score. |
| InferDPT: Privacy-Preserving Inference for Closed-box Large Language Models | Meng Tong, Kejiang Chen, Jie Zhang et al. | IEEE Trans. Dependable and Secure Computing 22(5), 2025 (arXiv 2023) | [arXiv:2310.12214](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.12214); [doi:10.1109/TDSC.2025.3550389](https://doi.org/10.1109/TDSC.2025.3550389) | [baseline] [related-work] Baseline for the DP family in the related-work table; its perturb-then-locally-repair loop parallels CodeKavach's de-pseudonymisation and its embedding-inversion attack analysis is reusable for the leakage study. |
| Privacy-Preserving Prompt Tuning for LLM Services (RAPT), published as 'Privacy-Preserving Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning for LLM Services' | Yansong Li, Zhixing Tan, Paula Branco, Yang Liu | IEEE/ACM TASLP 33 (2025); arXiv 2023 | [arXiv:2305.06212](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.06212) | [related-work] Cite as the LDP branch of the design space. |
| Split-and-Denoise (SnD): Protect LLM Inference with Local Differential Privacy | Peihua Mai, Ran Yan, Zhe Huang et al. | ICML 2024, 2023 | [arXiv:2310.09130](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.09130) | [related-work] Representative of split-inference defences; relevant background for the L4 'abstract representation only' level and for why CodeKavach does not ship raw embeddings of client code (see embedding inversion entries). |
| TextObfuscator: Making Pre-trained Language Model a Privacy Protector via Obfuscating Word Representations | Xin Zhou, Yi Lu, Ruotian Ma et al. | Findings of ACL 2023 | [ACL Anthology](https://aclanthology.org/2023.findings-acl.337/) | [related-work] Related work for representation-level obfuscation; supports the argument that any L4 vector/IR representation needs its own inversion test. |

| No Free Lunch Theorem for Privacy-Preserving LLM Inference | Xiaojin Zhang, Yahao Pang, Yan Kang et al. | Artificial Intelligence 341 (2025), Elsevier (arXiv 2024) | [arXiv:2405.20681](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.20681); [doi:10.1016/j.artint.2025.104293](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.artint.2025.104293) | [related-work] Theoretical framing for the paper's central privacy-utility trade-off claim; CodeKavach supplies the empirical counterpart for code-audit tasks. |
| NIST SP 800-38G: Methods for Format-Preserving Encryption (FF1/FF3) and Rev. 1 second public draft | NIST (Morris Dworkin; Rev.1 draft with Nicky Mouha) | NIST Special Publication, 2016 | [csrc.nist.gov](https://csrc.nist.gov/pubs/sp/800/38/g/upd1/final); [related page](https://csrc.nist.gov/pubs/sp/800/38/g/r1/2pd) | [related-work] If CodeKavach offers keyed, reversible identifier pseudonyms without storing a mapping table, FF1 with a per-engagement key is the standards-based option; short identifiers fall below the safe domain size, so an HMAC-derived token plus an encrypted local vault is the default. |
| k-Anonymity: A Model for Protecting Privacy | Latanya Sweeney | Int. J. Uncertainty, Fuzziness and Knowledge-Based Systems 10(5), 2002 | [doi:10.1142/S0218488502001648](https://doi.org/10.1142/S0218488502001648) | [evaluation] [related-work] Conceptual tool for the leakage metric: a pseudonymised slice is 'k-anonymous' if it is structurally indistinguishable from at least k slices in a public code corpus. |
| Robust De-anonymization of Large Sparse Datasets | Arvind Narayanan, Vitaly Shmatikov | IEEE S&P 2008 | [doi:10.1109/SP.2008.33](https://doi.org/10.1109/SP.2008.33) | [related-work] Seminal result that removing identifiers does not anonymise high-dimensional data once the adversary holds auxiliary information (the Netflix Prize attack). The reason the leakage harness gives the attacker all public code as auxiliary knowledge and measures nearest-neighbour re-identification of pseudonymised slices instead of assuming that renamed code is anonymous. |
| The Limits of Word Level Differential Privacy | Justus Mattern, Benjamin Weggenmann, Florian Kerschbaum | Findings of NAACL 2022 | [arXiv:2205.02130](https://arxiv.org/abs/2205.02130) | [related-work] Evidence for rejecting token-level DP as CodeKavach's main mechanism; cited in the design-rationale section. |
| Privacy- and Utility-Preserving Textual Analysis via Calibrated Multivariate Perturbations | Oluwaseyi Feyisetan, Borja Balle, Thomas Drake, Tom Diethe | WSDM 2020, 2019 | [arXiv:1910.08902](https://arxiv.org/abs/1910.08902) | [evaluation] [related-work] Foundational citation for metric-DP text privatisation. |

Andrés, Nicolás Emilio Bordenabe, Catuscia Palamidessi | HotPETs / PETS 2013 (LNCS 7981) | [doi:10.1007/978-3-642-39077-7\_5](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-642-39077-7_5) | [related-work] Formal definition cited when CodeKavach states what guarantee (if any) applies to perturbed literals/constants. |
| Utility-Privacy Tradeoffs in Databases: An Information-Theoretic Approach | Lalitha Sankar, S. Raj Rajagopalan, H. Vincent Poor | IEEE Trans. Information Forensics and Security 8(6), 2013 | [doi:10.1109/TIFS.2013.2253320](https://doi.org/10.1109/TIFS.2013.2253320) | [related-work] Gives the information-theoretic vocabulary (mutual information between original and released artefact, utility as distortion) for CodeKavach's leakage-vs-accuracy plots. |
| On the Foundations of Quantitative Information Flow | Geoffrey Smith | FoSSaCS 2009 (LNCS 5504) | [doi:10.1007/978-3-642-00596-1\_21](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-642-00596-1_21) | [related-work] Justifies reporting leakage as attacker success rate (top-1/top-k identifier recovery, business-domain guess accuracy) instead of only similarity scores. |
