### 1.11 Risk rating and vulnerability prioritisation

Evidence on how reliable CVSS scoring is, how exploit prediction works and whether LLMs can assign severity; input to the risk-rating stage.

| Paper | Authors | Venue/Year | Link | Relevance to CodeKavach |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| On Predicting Vulnerability Severity Using In-Context Learning: An Industrial Case Study | Daniel Rodriguez-Cardenas, David N. Palacio, Anna Schmedding et al. | Journal of Software: Evolution and Process 38(6), June 2026, doi:10.1002/smr.70126; arXiv:2608.22089 | [arXiv:2608.22089](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22089); [doi:10.1002/smr.70126](https://doi.org/10.1002/smr.70126) | [related-work] Directly supports two CodeKavach claims: (1) confidentiality is a real industrial blocker for cloud-LLM severity triage, and (2) self-hosted open models can do snippet-level CVSS estimation with few-shot prompts. |
| AutoCVSS: Assessing the Performance of LLMs for Automated Software Vulnerability Scoring | Davide Sanvito, Giovanni Arriciati, Giuseppe Siracusano et al. | EMNLP 2025 Industry Track, pp. 564-575, doi:10.18653/v1/2025.emnlp-industry.38 | [ACL Anthology](https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-industry.38/) | [evaluation] [baseline] Baseline and method reference for the risk-rating stage where the LLM proposes a CVSS vector from a pseudonymised slice; informs which metrics need deterministic rules or human input. |
| Can LLMs Classify CVEs? Investigating LLMs Capabilities in Computing CVSS Vectors | Francesco Marchiori, Denis Donadel, Mauro Conti | TrustAICyberSec 2025 workshop, in 2025 IEEE Symposium on Computers and Communications (ISCC), doi:10.1109/ISCC65549.2025.11326354; arXiv:2504.10713 | [arXiv:2504.10713](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10713) | [related-work] Guides a hybrid design: deterministic derivation of AV/PR/UI from code context (entry point type, auth decorators) and LLM judgement only where needed; also a baseline for the privacy-utility study (does pseudonymisation hurt severity estimation?). |
| Automated Code-centric Software Vulnerability Assessment: How Far Are We? An Empirical Study in C/C++ | Anh The Nguyen, Triet Huynh Minh Le, M. Ali Babar | ACM/IEEE International Symposium on Empirical Software Engineering and Measurement (ESEM) 2024, pp. 72-83, doi:10.1145/3674805.3686670 | [arXiv:2407.17053](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.17053); [doi:10.1145/3674805.3686670](https://doi.org/10.1145/3674805.3686670) | [related-work] Closest prior work to CodeKavach's situation, where severity must be estimated from code because no CVE text exists for a client's private finding. |
| Shedding Light on CVSS Scoring Inconsistencies: A User-Centric Study on Evaluating Widespread Security Vulnerabilities | Julia Wunder, Andreas Kurtz, Christian Eichenmüller et al. | IEEE Symposium on Security and Privacy (S&P) 2024, pp. 1102-1121 | [arXiv:2308.15259](https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.15259); [doi:10.1109/SP54263.2024.00058](https://doi.org/10.1109/SP54263.2024.00058) | [evaluation] [related-work] Justifies two design choices: (1) severity is stored as a vector with a rationale per metric, not a bare number; (2) LLM-assigned CVSS is evaluated against an inter-rater band rather than a single 'correct' score. Cite when reviewers question LLM scoring accuracy. |
| Enhancing Vulnerability Prioritization: Data-Driven Exploit Predictions with Community-Driven Insights (EPSS v3) | Jay Jacobs, Sasha Romanosky, Octavian Suciu et al. | WEIS 2023; also IEEE EuroS&P Workshops 2023, pp. 194-206 | [arXiv:2302.14172](https://arxiv.org/abs/2302.14172); [doi:10.1109/EuroSPW59978.2023.00027](https://doi.org/10.1109/EuroSPW59978.2023.00027) | [related-work] Academic citation for using EPSS in the risk-rating formula and for the argument that CVSS alone is a poor prioritiser. |
| Conflicting Scores, Confusing Signals: An Empirical Study of Vulnerability Scoring Systems | Viktoria Koscinski, Mark Nelson, Ahmet Okutan et al. | arXiv preprint (Aug 2025) | [arXiv:2508.13644](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13644) | [related-work] Justifies reporting several prioritisation signals side by side (as CERT-In and SEBI now expect) instead of collapsing them into one opaque score, and warns that the 'priority' column must state which system produced it. |
| Prompting the Priorities: A First Look at Evaluating LLMs for Vulnerability Triage and Prioritization | Osama Al Haddad, Muhammad Ikram, Ejaz Ahmed, Young Lee | arXiv preprint, 2025 | [arXiv:2510.18508](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.18508) | [related-work] Evidence base and prompt designs for an SSVC-style prioritisation step, and a reminder to report agreement statistics (kappa) rather than accuracy alone in CodeKavach's evaluation. |
| Towards Explainable Vulnerability Detection with Large Language Models (LLMVulExp) | Qiheng Mao, Zhenhao Li, Xing Hu et al. | IEEE Transactions on Software Engineering 51(10):2957-2971, October 2025, doi:10.1109/TSE.2025.3605442; arXiv:2406.09701 (first version June 2024) | [arXiv:2406.09701](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.09701); [doi:10.1109/TSE.2025.3605442](https://doi.org/10.1109/TSE.2025.3605442) | [evaluation] [related-work] Reference for how to evaluate the quality of LLM-written finding descriptions and remediation text (accuracy, clarity, actionability rubrics), which is what ends up in the report. |
| CVSS: Ubiquitous and Broken | Henry Howland | ACM Digital Threats: Research and Practice 4(1), pp. 1-12 (online Feb 2022), 2023 | [doi:10.1145/3491263](https://doi.org/10.1145/3491263) | [related-work] Supports the design decision to show CVSS as severity only and to pair it with likelihood signals (KEV, EPSS, SSVC, OWASP RRM business impact); cite with Wunder et al. (S&P 2024) in the threats-to-validity discussion of severity labels. |
| Time to Change the CVSS? (Spring et al.) | Jonathan Spring, Eric Hatleback, Allen Householder et al. | IEEE Security & Privacy 19(2):74-78, March 2021 | [doi:10.1109/MSEC.2020.3044475](https://doi.org/10.1109/MSEC.2020.3044475); [sei.cmu.edu](https://www.sei.cmu.edu/library/towards-improving-cvss/) | [related-work] Related-work citation for why CodeKavach reports severity (CVSS) and priority/action (SSVC-like, business context) as separate columns. |

### 1.12 Structured output and inference cost

Engineering papers behind the verdict schema, prompt caching and model routing decisions.

| Paper | Authors | Venue/Year | Link | Relevance to CodeKavach |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Auditing Prompt Caching in Language Model APIs (with: CacheProbe, prompt-cache isolation in gateway APIs) | Gu, Li, Kuditipudi, Liang, Hashimoto (Stanford); CacheProbe: Ryan Fahey | ICML 2025 (per arXiv comment); CacheProbe: SAGAI '26 workshop at IEEE S&P 2026 (per arXiv comment) | [arXiv:2502.07776](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.07776); [arXiv:2605.30613](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.30613) | [inspiration] [related-work] Threat model + experiment: prompt caching is the main cost lever for CodeKavach (fixed rubric prefix), but the cached prefix must contain only CodeKavach's own public rubric, never client material; the per-finding slice goes after the cache breakpoint. |
| Let Me Speak Freely? Impact of format restrictions on LLM performance | Tam et al. (Appier AI Research, National Taiwan University) | EMNLP 2024 Industry Track | [arXiv:2408.02442](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.02442); [ACL Anthology](https://aclanthology.org/2024.emnlp-industry.91/) | [inspiration] [related-work] Design input: put a free-text `analysis` field FIRST in the verdict schema (or use a reasoning model / two-step reason-then-extract) so structure does not suppress the taint-path reasoning. Make this an ablation in the paper. |
| RouteLLM: learning to route between strong and weak LLMs | Ong, Almahairi, Wu et al. | arXiv, 2024 | [arXiv:2406.18665](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.18665); [GitHub: lm-sys/RouteLLM](https://github.com/lm-sys/RouteLLM) | [inspiration] [related-work] Cost design: triage easy candidate findings (e.g. high-confidence secrets, trivially dead taint paths) with a small local model and escalate only ambiguous ones to a frontier model; report the cost/accuracy effect as a secondary result. |

## 2. Datasets and benchmarks

Corpora used to measure detection accuracy and leakage. SAST test suites with per-test-case ground truth (OWASP Benchmark, Juliet and similar) are listed in section 7.2. Several widely used vulnerability datasets have documented label-noise and duplication problems; the Notes column says which ones are used for headline numbers and which only as historical baselines.

### 2.1 Vulnerability datasets

| Dataset / benchmark | Authors / maintainer | Licence | Link | Role tag | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PrimeVul - Vulnerability Detection with Code Language Models: How Far Are We? | Yangruibo Ding, Yanjun Fu, Omniyyah Ibrahim et al. (2025) | MIT (repo) | [arXiv:2403.18624](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.18624); [GitHub: DLVulDet/PrimeVul](https://github.com/DLVulDet/PrimeVul) | [evaluation] | C/C++ function-level dataset built with stricter labelling, de-duplication and a chronological train/test split, plus a paired vulnerable/patched evaluation. Evaluate on it: the paired (vulnerable vs. patched) protocol is the right accuracy metric for the privacy-utility curve, because it measures whether the LLM still distinguishes a bug from its fix after L1-L4 transformations rather than guessing from surface cues. |
| CVEfixes | Guru Prasad Bhandari, Amara Naseer, Leon Moonen (Simula) (2021) | MIT (collection code); dataset distributed separately via Zenodo | [arXiv:2107.08760](https://arxiv.org/abs/2107.08760); [GitHub: secureIT-project/CVEfixes](https://github.com/secureIT-project/CVEfixes) | [evaluation] | Automated collector that links NVD CVE records to their fixing commits and stores repository-, commit-, file- and method-level data in a relational database. Evaluate on it: the main multi-language source (Python, JS, Java, PHP, Go, C/C++) of real vulnerable/fixed pairs with CWE labels for measuring detection at each privacy level beyond C/C++. |
| DiverseVul | Yizheng Chen, Zhoujie Ding, Lamya Alowain et al. (2023) | No LICENSE file in repo (data hosted on Google Drive) - treat as research-use only | [arXiv:2304.00409](https://arxiv.org/abs/2304.00409); [GitHub: wagner-group/diversevul](https://github.com/wagner-group/diversevul) | [evaluation] | Large C/C++ dataset of vulnerable and non-vulnerable functions mined from security-fix commits across many projects and CWE classes. Secondary evaluation corpus and a source of per-CWE samples; cite its cross-project generalisation result when justifying why CodeKavach uses deterministic analysis first and the LLM only for triage. |
| D2A | Yunhui Zheng, Saurabh Pujar, Burn Lewis et al. (2021) | Apache-2.0 (repo, archived) | [arXiv:2102.07995](https://arxiv.org/abs/2102.07995); [GitHub: IBM/D2A](https://github.com/IBM/D2A) | [evaluation] | Labels static-analyser (Infer) alerts as likely true or false positives by differential analysis: alerts that disappear after a bug-fixing commit are treated as real. Evaluate on it: the closest public analogue to CodeKavach's 'SAST finding + trace -> LLM triage' task; used by LLM4FPM and others as the false-positive-mitigation benchmark. |
| ReVeal - Deep Learning based Vulnerability Detection: Are We There Yet? | Saikat Chakraborty, Rahul Krishna, Yangruibo Ding, Baishakhi Ray (2021) | MIT (repo) | [arXiv:2009.07235](https://arxiv.org/abs/2009.07235); [GitHub: VulDetProject/ReVeal](https://github.com/VulDetProject/ReVeal) | [evaluation] [inspiration] | Re-evaluates earlier DL detectors on a realistic Chromium/Debian dataset and finds their performance drops by more than half. Methodology input: motivates de-duplication, realistic class imbalance and project-level splits in the CodeKavach evaluation protocol. |
| ARVO: Atlas of Reproducible Vulnerabilities for Open-Source Software | Xiang Mei, Jordi Del Castillo, Pulkit Singh Singaria et al. (2024) | No LICENSE file in ARVO-Meta repo | [arXiv:2408.02153](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.02153); [GitHub: n132/ARVO-Meta](https://github.com/n132/ARVO-Meta) | [evaluation] | Rebuilds more than 6,000 OSS-Fuzz-discovered vulnerabilities across 311 C/C++ projects into re-compilable. Source of real, reproducible C/C++ bugs with precise fix commits for building project-scale test repositories. |
| CleanVul | Yikun Li, Ting Zhang, Ratnadira Widyasari et al. (2024) | No LICENSE file in GitHub repo; Hugging Face dataset card (yikun-li/CleanVul) declares MIT | [arXiv:2411.17274](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.17274); [GitHub: yikun-li/CleanVul](https://github.com/yikun-li/CleanVul) | [evaluation] | Uses an LLM-plus-heuristics filter to separate genuinely vulnerability-fixing function changes from unrelated edits in security commits, producing a cleaner multi-language function-level dataset. Cleaner multi-language pairs for evaluation; also an example of using an LLM as a label-noise filter, which mirrors CodeKavach's triage step. |
| JLeaks | Tianyang Liu, Weixing Ji, Xiaohui Dong et al. (2024) | MIT (repo) | [doi:10.1145/3597503.3639162](https://doi.org/10.1145/3597503.3639162); [GitHub: Dcollectors/JLeaks](https://github.com/Dcollectors/JLeaks) | [evaluation] | Repository of real-world Java resource-leak bugs collected from hundreds of open-source projects, with the leaking resource type. Test set for the CWE-772/CWE-404 resource-leak checker and for LLM triage of leak warnings (pairs with SkipAnalyzer-style filters). |
| MegaVul | Chao Ni, Liyu Shen, Xiaohu Yang et al. (2024) | GPL-3.0 (repo) | [arXiv:2406.12415](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12415); [GitHub: Icyrockton/MegaVul](https://github.com/Icyrockton/MegaVul) | [evaluation] [baseline] | Large C/C++ (later Java) function-level dataset mined from CVE-linked commits. Optional extra C/C++ and Java evaluation data; its pre-abstracted function variants are a ready-made comparison point for CodeKavach L2 pseudonymisation. |
| MoreFixes: A Large-Scale Dataset of CVE Fix Commits Mined through Enhanced Repository Discovery | Jafar Akhoundali, Sajad Rahim Nouri, Kristian Rietveld, Olga Gadyatskaya (2024) | Mixed (repo LICENSE.txt): MIT for CVEfixes-derived code, Apache-2.0 for Prospector-derived code, CC BY 4.0 for GitHub Advisory data, NVD data as public service | [doi:10.1145/3663533.3664036](https://doi.org/10.1145/3663533.3664036); [GitHub: JafarAkhondali/Morefixes](https://github.com/JafarAkhondali/Morefixes) | [evaluation] | Extends the CVEfixes pipeline with better discovery of the repositories and commits behind each CVE. Larger and more recent multi-language alternative to CVEfixes for building per-language test sets (PHP, JS/TS, Java, Go, C#) at commit and function granularity. |
| ReposVul | Xinchen Wang, Ruida Hu, Cuiyun Gao et al. (2024) | not stated | [arXiv:2401.13169](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.13169); [doi:10.1145/3639478.3647634](https://doi.org/10.1145/3639478.3647634) | [evaluation] | Repository-level vulnerability dataset (C, C++, Java, Python) that untangles fix commits. Repository-level evaluation with caller/callee context, needed to test L3 minimal-slice extraction rather than isolated functions. |
| The FormAI Dataset: Generative AI in Software Security Through the Lens of Formal Verification | Norbert Tihanyi, Tamas Bisztray, Ridhi Jain et al. (2023) | MIT (repo) | [arXiv:2307.02192](https://arxiv.org/abs/2307.02192); [GitHub: FormAI-Dataset/FormAI-dataset](https://github.com/FormAI-Dataset/FormAI-dataset) | [evaluation] | 112,000 compilable C programs generated by GPT-3.5, each labelled by the ESBMC bounded model checker with vulnerability type. Low-contamination test suite for alert adjudication (used that way by the CMU SEI study listed in section 1.2); synthetic, so complement it with real-project data. |
| Vul4J: A Dataset of Reproducible Java Vulnerabilities Geared Towards the Study of Program Repair Techniques | Quang-Cuong Bui, Riccardo Scandariato, Nicolas E. Diaz Ferreyra (2022) | GPL-3.0 (framework repo) | [doi:10.1145/3524842.3528482](https://doi.org/10.1145/3524842.3528482); [GitHub: tuhh-softsec/vul4j](https://github.com/tuhh-softsec/vul4j) | [evaluation] | Curated set of real Java CVEs, each with a buildable vulnerable revision, the human patch and proof-of-vulnerability tests. Small but fully reproducible Java ground truth to complement CWE-Bench-Java; run it as an external test harness (GPL-3.0) rather than bundling it. |
| CrossVul | Georgios Nikitopoulos, Konstantina Dritsa, Panos Louridas, Dimitris Mitropoulos (2021) | CC BY 4.0 (Zenodo dataset) | [doi:10.1145/3468264.3473122](https://doi.org/10.1145/3468264.3473122); [doi:10.5281/zenodo.4734050](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.4734050) | [evaluation] | Cross-language dataset of vulnerable files and their patched counterparts, keyed by CVE/CWE. File-level multi-language test material (PHP, JS, Java, Go, C#) for the plugin languages that C/C++-only benchmarks do not cover. |
| Big-Vul (A C/C++ Code Vulnerability Dataset with Code Changes and CVE Summaries) | Jiahao Fan, Yi Li, Shaohua Wang, Tien N. Nguyen (2020) | MIT (repo) | [doi:10.1145/3379597.3387501](https://doi.org/10.1145/3379597.3387501); [GitHub: ZeoVan/MSR\_20\_Code\_vulnerability\_CSV\_Dataset](https://github.com/ZeoVan/MSR_20_Code_vulnerability_CSV_Dataset) | [baseline] | CVE-linked C/C++ dataset that pairs vulnerable functions with their fixing commits and CVE metadata (CWE, CVSS, summaries). Use only as a historical baseline and as a cautionary example in the threats-to-validity section; do not report headline accuracy on it. |
| Devign | Yaqin Zhou, Shangqing Liu, Jingkai Siow et al. (2019) | not stated | [arXiv:1909.03496](https://arxiv.org/abs/1909.03496); [sites.google.com](https://sites.google.com/view/devign) | [evaluation] | Graph-neural-network vulnerability detector over a joint AST/CFG/DFG code graph. Background for the 'graph representation' related-work paragraph; the FFmpeg/QEMU split is a small sanity-check set for the triage prompt. |
| Draper VDISC - Automated Vulnerability Detection in Source Code Using Deep Representation Learning | Rebecca L. Russell, Louis Kim, Lei H. Hamilton et al. (2018) | Dataset on OSF (osf.io/d45bw) - check the OSF page for terms before redistribution | [arXiv:1807.04320](https://arxiv.org/abs/1807.04320); [doi:10.1109/ICMLA.2018.00120](https://doi.org/10.1109/ICMLA.2018.00120) | [baseline] | Early large-scale function-level C/C++ corpus (over a million functions) labelled by the findings of three static analysers. Historical baseline and a cautionary example of SAST-derived labels; do not use as ground truth for triage accuracy. |

### 2.2 LLM vulnerability-detection, triage and code-review benchmarks

| Dataset / benchmark | Authors / maintainer | Licence | Link | Role tag | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CWE-Bench-Java | Ziyang Li, Saikat Dutta, Mayur Naik (UPenn / Cornell) (2024) | MIT | [GitHub: iris-sast/cwe-bench-java](https://github.com/iris-sast/cwe-bench-java); [arXiv:2405.17238](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.17238) | [evaluation] | 120 manually validated CVEs in real, buildable Java projects (path traversal, command injection, XSS, code injection) with fix commits and vulnerable methods identified. Evaluate on it: whole-repository Java taint-style benchmark, ideal for end-to-end runs of ingest -> SAST -> slice -> LLM triage, and for comparing against IRIS and CodeQL numbers. |
| SecLLMHolmes - LLMs Cannot Reliably Identify and Reason About Security Vulnerabilities (Yet?) | Saad Ullah, Mingji Han, Saurabh Pujar et al. (2024) | GPL-3.0 (repo) | [arXiv:2312.12575](https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.12575); [GitHub: ai4cloudops/SecLLMHolmes](https://github.com/ai4cloudops/SecLLMHolmes) | [evaluation] [inspiration] | Automated framework testing LLMs over 228 code scenarios and eight dimensions (determinism, reasoning faithfulness, robustness to augmentations, real CVEs). Core evidence and tooling for the research question: its robustness tests (renaming, library-function renaming, dead code) are exactly the perturbations CodeKavach L2 applies, so reuse the protocol to quantify utility loss per privacy level. GPL-3.0: run as an external harness, do not vendor into the MIT code base. |
| RealVuln: Benchmarking Rule-Based, General-Purpose LLM, and Security-Specialized Scanners on Real-World Code | John Pellew, Faizan Raza (2026) | not stated | [arXiv:2604.13764](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.13764) | [evaluation] | Open benchmark of 26 intentionally vulnerable Python repositories with 796 hand-labelled entries (676 vulnerabilities and 120 false-positive traps). Evaluate on it for Python, with caution: targets are educational/CTF apps likely present in LLM training data and the top scanner is the authors' commercial product, so treat rankings as indicative. |
| SastBench: A Benchmark for Testing Agentic SAST Triage | Jake Feiglin, Guy Dar (2026) | not stated | [arXiv:2601.02941](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.02941) | [evaluation] | Benchmark for SAST-triage agents that pairs real CVEs as true positives with filtered real SAST findings as approximate false positives. Evaluate on it: a realistic true/false-positive mix is what CodeKavach's triage stage sees in practice. Run the triage stage at each privacy level on SastBench to obtain the utility axis of the trade-off curve. |
| SecVulEval | Md Basim Uddin Ahmed, Nima Shiri Harzevili, Jiho Shin et al. (2026) | No LICENSE file in repo (all rights reserved by default) | [arXiv:2505.19828](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19828); [GitHub: basimbd/SecVulEval](https://github.com/basimbd/SecVulEval) | [evaluation] | C/C++ benchmark with statement-level vulnerability labels and the contextual information (callees, types, globals) needed to reason about each CVE. Evaluate on it for statement-level localisation with explicit context, mapping well to L3 slices. |
| CASTLE Benchmark | Richard A. Dubniczky, Krisztofer Zoltán Horvát, Tamás Bisztray et al. (2025) | MIT (repo) | [arXiv:2503.09433](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09433); [GitHub: CASTLE-Benchmark/CASTLE-Benchmark](https://github.com/CASTLE-Benchmark/CASTLE-Benchmark) | [evaluation] | Hand-written compilable C micro-programs covering 25 CWEs, scored with a metric that penalises false positives, used to compare static analysers. Evaluate on it: one of few benchmarks that ranks SAST engines and LLMs with the same metric, matching CodeKavach's hybrid design; MIT licence allows bundling in CI tests. |
| JitVul - Benchmarking LLMs and LLM-based Agents in Practical Vulnerability Detection for Code Repositories | Alperen Yildiz, Sin G. Teo, Yiling Lou et al. (2025) | not stated | [ACL Anthology](https://aclanthology.org/2025.acl-long.1490/); [arXiv:2503.03586](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.03586) | [evaluation] | Pairs vulnerable and patched commits for 879 CVEs and evaluates plain LLMs against ReAct agents that can fetch inter-procedural context. Pairwise repo-level protocol to measure whether L3 slices carry enough context compared with an agent that may browse the entire repo. |
| eyeballvul | Timothee Chauvin (2024) | MIT (repo) | [arXiv:2407.08708](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.08708); [GitHub: timothee-chauvin/eyeballvul](https://github.com/timothee-chauvin/eyeballvul) | [evaluation] | Continuously updated benchmark that maps OSV vulnerability records to repository revisions. Contamination-resistant, multi-language, repo-scale evaluation; pick revisions after each model's cut-off to keep the privacy-utility numbers honest. |
| VulDetectBench | Yu Liu, Lang Gao, Mingxin Yang et al. (2024) | MIT (repo) | [arXiv:2406.07595](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.07595); [GitHub: Sweetaroo/VulDetectBench](https://github.com/Sweetaroo/VulDetectBench) | [evaluation] | Five tasks of rising difficulty - existence, CWE type, key objects/functions, root-cause line, trigger line. Task ladder to reuse when reporting which capabilities survive pseudonymisation (type identification may survive; line-level root cause may not). |
| VulEval - Towards Repository-Level Evaluation of Software Vulnerability Detection | Xin-Cheng Wen, Xinchen Wang, Yujia Chen et al. (2024) | not stated | [arXiv:2404.15596](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.15596); [doi:10.1109/TSE.2026.3662145](https://doi.org/10.1109/TSE.2026.3662145) | [evaluation] | Repository-level evaluation with three linked tasks: function-level detection, retrieval of the vulnerability-relevant callee/caller dependencies. Candidate external benchmark for the PII component; check language coverage (Indian languages) before adopting. |

Ready-made attacker benchmark and metric suite for JS/TS; CodeKavach can plug its pseudonymiser in as one more 'obfuscator' and report LLM recovery under the same metrics. |
| CodeCrash: Exposing LLM Fragility to Misleading Natural Language in Code Reasoning | Man Ho Lam, Chaozheng Wang, Jen-tse Huang, Michael R. Lyu (2025) | not stated | [arXiv:2504.14119](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.14119) | [evaluation] | Stress-tests code reasoning when comments, prints and identifiers carry misleading natural-language cues. Argument in favour of stripping comments at L2: removing developer comments not only protects business context but can also remove misleading cues; needs to be measured per finding type. |

(2024) | MIT (toolkit) | [arXiv:2408.12787](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.12787); [GitHub: QinbinLi/LLM-PBE](https://github.com/QinbinLi/LLM-PBE) | [evaluation] [inspiration] | Toolkit and benchmark that packages data-extraction, membership-inference. Reusable harness for the attacker side of the evaluation (prompt-leakage and extraction probes against the self-hosted models CodeKavach supports). |

(2022) | Apache-2.0 (code) | [arXiv:2212.10264](https://arxiv.org/abs/2212.10264); [GitHub: amazon-science/recode](https://github.com/amazon-science/recode) | [evaluation] | Benchmark of 30+ semantics-preserving perturbations on docstrings, function names, variable names. Off-the-shelf transformation library and robustness metrics (robust pass@k, robust drop) that CodeKavach can adapt to report 'robust detection rate' under its own pseudonymisation. |
| The Text Anonymization Benchmark (TAB) | Ildikó Pilán, Pierre Lison, Lilja Øvrelid et al. (2022) | MIT (repo) | [arXiv:2202.00443](https://arxiv.org/abs/2202.00443); [GitHub: NorskRegnesentral/text-anonymization-benchmark](https://github.com/NorskRegnesentral/text-anonymization-benchmark) | [evaluation] | Corpus of European Court of Human Rights judgments annotated for direct and quasi-identifiers. Methodological model for a small gold-standard 'code anonymisation benchmark': annotate which identifiers/strings/comments are business-revealing vs. security-relevant and score CodeKavach's masking with TAB-style weighted recall/precision. |
| CodeSearchNet Challenge: Evaluating the State of Semantic Code Search | Hamel Husain, Ho-Hsiang Wu, Tiferet Gazit et al. (2019) | MIT (code); data under the licences of the source repositories | [arXiv:1909.09436](https://arxiv.org/abs/1909.09436); [GitHub: github/CodeSearchNet](https://github.com/github/CodeSearchNet) | [evaluation] | About six million functions in six languages (two million with documentation) with a semantic code search benchmark. Public 'background corpus' for the k-anonymity-style re-identification test and a source of decoy functions; respect upstream licences when redistributing samples. |

Methodology template: CodeKavach's injection evaluation should likewise report utility-under-attack and targeted attack success rate (e.g. 'vulnerable finding marked false positive') rather than detector accuracy alone. |

(EPFL, Microsoft and collaborators) (2025) | not stated | [arXiv:2501.10868](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.10868) | [evaluation] | Roughly ten thousand real-world JSON Schemas used to measure coverage, efficiency and output quality of constrained-decoding engines (Guidance, Outlines, llama.cpp, XGrammar, OpenAI, Gemini). Evaluate: justifies the choice of guided-decoding back-end and warns that engines silently differ in which schema features they support - CodeKavach's schema should be tested against each target engine in CI. |

Model selection: the only common leaderboard that reports dollars per run and covers CodeKavach's target languages. |
| BigCodeBench | Zhuo et al., BigCode (2024) | Apache-2.0 | [arXiv:2406.15877](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.15877); [bigcode-bench.github.io](https://bigcode-bench.github.io/) | [evaluation] | 1,140 function-level tasks requiring diverse library calls and complex instructions. Secondary model-selection signal (library-API understanding matters for recognising dangerous sinks). |

InjecAgent (github.com/uiuc-kang-lab/InjecAgent): the repository has no LICENSE file and the README states no licence, so default copyright applies - cite and re-implement, do not vendor its data or code into an MIT project | [arXiv:2312.14197](https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.14197); [ACL Anthology](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-acl.624/) | [evaluation] | BIPIA is the first benchmark of indirect prompt injection across e-mail, web, table. Evaluation: BIPIA's code-QA split and code attack set are a ready-made starting corpus for measuring injection success against CodeKavach's triage prompt at each privacy level. |
| LiveCodeBench | Jain et al. (UC Berkeley, MIT, Cornell) (2024) | MIT (code) | [arXiv:2403.07974](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.07974); [livecodebench.github.io](https://livecodebench.github.io/leaderboard.html) | [evaluation] [inspiration] | Continuously refreshed benchmark of competition problems released after model cut-off dates, scoring generation, self-repair. Model selection signal for which local models can actually reason about code; cite alongside CodeKavach's own security-specific evaluation since general coding skill is only a proxy for vulnerability triage. |

Model selection signal for repository-scale reasoning (relevant to cross-function taint paths). |

## 3. Open-source tools and libraries

Engines, libraries and data sources. Read the Notes column before adding a dependency: several well-known projects are copyleft, source-available, archived or carry rule packs under a different licence from the engine.

**Licence watch-list** (details in the tables). Source-available or proprietary terms: the Semgrep-maintained rule registry (Semgrep Rules License v1.0) and the archived opengrep-rules (LGPL-2.1 plus Commons Clause), the CodeQL CLI (proprietary terms for code that is not open source), Bearer CLI and Arize Phoenix (Elastic License 2.0), Brakeman (Brakeman Public Use License, non-commercial), SysReptor (community licence). AGPL-3.0: TruffleHog, SVF, Slither, MegaLinter, cve-search, Vulnerability-Lookup, OWASP Cervantes, Renovate, the Trail of Bits Semgrep rules, Vulnhuntr, Codemodder, OWASP WrongSecrets. GPL tools that are run as separate processes only: Cppcheck, Flawfinder, Hadolint, Pandoc, Universal Ctags, srcML, Exploit-DB/SearchSploit. Share-alike or non-commercial data: Secrets Patterns DB and VulnerableCode data (CC-BY-SA-4.0), Piiranha (CC-BY-NC-ND-4.0), CIS Benchmarks (CC BY-NC-SA 4.0). Open-weight models with custom terms are covered in section 4.3.

### 3.1 SAST engines

**Multi-language engines**

| Tool | Licence | Link | Role tag | Notes incl. licence caveats |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Opengrep | LGPL-2.1 | [GitHub: opengrep/opengrep](https://github.com/opengrep/opengrep); [opengrep.dev](https://www.opengrep.dev/) | [integrated] | Community fork of the Semgrep CE engine created after the December 2024 licence/feature changes. Integrate as the default pattern/taint engine: single static binary is easy to bundle in an air-gapped Docker image and to call from Python via subprocess with SARIF output; restored fingerprints help stable finding IDs across scans. LGPL engine is invoked as a separate process, so CodeKavach's MIT code is unaffected. Note the rule problem remains: opengrep/opengrep-rules was archived (Nov 2025) and carries LGPL-2.1 + Commons Clause, so rules must be own-authored. |
| Semgrep Community Edition (CE) | LGPL-2.1 (engine only; rules are licensed separately) | [GitHub: semgrep/semgrep](https://github.com/semgrep/semgrep); [semgrep.dev](https://semgrep.dev/blog/2024/important-updates-to-semgrep-oss/) | [planned] | Pattern-matching SAST engine (OCaml core, Python CLI wrapper) covering 30+ languages with YAML rules that look like the target code; outputs JSON, SARIF, JUnit and text. Integrate as an optional local engine invoked by subprocess (pip install semgrep; --sarif/--json), never imported as a library so LGPL stays at arm's length. Ship only CodeKavach-authored MIT rules; do not bundle the semgrep-rules registry (see Semgrep Rules License entry). |
| CodeQL (CLI, query libraries and codeql-action) | Queries/libraries: MIT (github/codeql). CLI/engine: proprietary 'GitHub CodeQL Terms and Conditions' | [codeql.github.com](https://codeql.github.com/); [GitHub: github/codeql-cli-binaries](https://github.com/github/codeql-cli-binaries/blob/main/LICENSE.md) | [baseline] | Semantic code analysis engine that builds a relational database of a codebase and evaluates QL (Datalog-style, object-oriented) queries over it. Baseline only, never bundled. Use under the academic-research clause to measure CodeKavach's detection accuracy against CodeQL on open-source benchmarks (OWASP Benchmark, CWE-Bench-Java, Juliet). For client (bank) code, CodeKavach must only consume a CodeQL SARIF that the client produced under its own GHAS licence; the plugin docs must state this explicitly. |
| OpenTaint | Apache-2.0 (core engine); MIT (CLI, GitHub Action, GitLab template, rules) | [GitHub: seqra/opentaint](https://github.com/seqra/opentaint) | [planned] | New open-source inter-procedural taint engine positioned as a free alternative to Semgrep Pro and CodeQL. Evaluate as the Java/Kotlin deep-taint engine: it is the only permissively licensed (Apache-2.0 + MIT rules) inter-procedural option that can legally run on proprietary bank code, which CodeQL cannot. |
| Bearer CLI | Elastic License 2.0 (source-available, NOT OSI open source) | [GitHub: Bearer/bearer](https://github.com/Bearer/bearer); [docs.bearer.com](https://docs.bearer.com/) | [inspiration] | SAST scanner for Go, Java, JS/TS, PHP, Python and Ruby whose distinguishing feature is sensitive-data-flow analysis: it classifies 120+ personal/health data types and reports where they flow (logs, third parties, storage), producing both security and privacy (GDPR-style) reports. Design inspiration + optional plugin. Bearer's data-type classifier is directly relevant to CodeKavach's L1 PII detection and to a 'privacy findings' report section. Licence trap: ELv2 forbids offering the software as a hosted/managed service, so it cannot be part of a hosted CodeKavach; keep it as an opt-in, client-installed engine and never bundle it. |
| Datadog Static Analyzer | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: DataDog/datadog-static-analyzer](https://github.com/DataDog/datadog-static-analyzer) | [inspiration] | Rust static analyzer that parses with tree-sitter and runs rules written as tree-sitter queries plus JavaScript logic. Design inspiration for CodeKavach's own tree-sitter rule engine (query + small script per rule, SARIF out). |
| GitLab SAST rules (sast-rules) | Mixed per directory: rules/gitlab = MIT (repo LICENSE, 'MIT Expat'); rules/lgpl = LGPL; rules/lgpl-cc = LGPL-2.1 + Commons Clause (rules derived from semgrep-rules) | [GitLab: gitlab-org/security-products](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/security-products/sast-rules) | [inspiration] | Rule repository behind GitLab's Semgrep-based SAST analyzer. Model for organising CodeKavach's rule pack by licence directory so the MIT distribution can be built by excluding non-MIT trees. The rules/gitlab (MIT) subset is a candidate seed for the CodeKavach rule pack; the lgpl-cc tree must not be redistributed in a hosted offering. |
| SonarQube Community Build | LGPL-3.0 (server core); bundled language analyzers released after 29 Nov 2024 are under the Sonar Source-Available License v1 (SSALv1, non-compete, NOT OSI open source) | [docs.sonarsource.com](https://docs.sonarsource.com/sonarqube-community-build/); [GitHub: SonarSource/sonarqube](https://github.com/SonarSource/sonarqube) | [baseline] | Self-hosted code-quality server (formerly 'Community Edition', renamed Community Build in late 2024) with rule-based analyzers for ~20 languages. Baseline / interoperability only: import its issues through the Web API or generic issue format if a client already runs it. Licence caveat: CodeKavach is arguably a 'competing' product under SSALv1, so never bundle or redistribute Sonar analyzers; only consume issues exported by a client-operated instance. |
| Third-party Semgrep-syntax rule packs (Trail of Bits, 0xdea, elttam) | AGPL-3.0 (Trail of Bits); MIT (0xdea); MIT (elttam) | [GitHub: trailofbits/semgrep-rules](https://github.com/trailofbits/semgrep-rules); [GitHub: 0xdea/semgrep-rules](https://github.com/0xdea/semgrep-rules) | [inspiration] | Independent rule collections written in Semgrep/Opengrep YAML and not covered by the Semgrep Rules License: Trail of Bits publishes ... Rule-sourcing options given that semgrep-rules and opengrep-rules cannot be shipped: the MIT packs (0xdea, elttam) may be vendored with attribution into CodeKavach's MIT rule pack; the Trail of Bits pack is AGPL-3.0, so fetch it at run time as an optional user-installed pack and never bundle it in the image. |
| Code Pathfinder | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: shivasurya/code-pathfinder](https://github.com/shivasurya/code-pathfinder); [codepathfinder.dev](https://codepathfinder.dev/) | [inspiration] | Go-based static analysis engine that parses with tree-sitter, builds a call graph and per-function variable dependency graphs. Design reference for CodeKavach's own tree-sitter-based taint/slicing engine (same parse -> call graph -> dependency graph -> taint summary pipeline) and an optional Python cross-file engine. |
| Microsoft DevSkim | MIT | [GitHub: microsoft/DevSkim](https://github.com/microsoft/DevSkim); [related page](https://github.com/microsoft/DevSkim/wiki/Analyze-Command) | [planned] | Regex/JSON-rule based security linter with a .NET CLI and IDE extensions (VS, VS Code); language-agnostic, no data-flow. Integrate as a cheap, MIT-licensed first-pass engine; its JSON rule schema (pattern + scopes + fix-its + severity) is a good model for CodeKavach's own lightweight rule format. |

**Language-specific analysers**

| Tool | Licence | Link | Role tag | Notes incl. licence caveats |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Bandit | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: PyCQA/bandit](https://github.com/PyCQA/bandit); [bandit.readthedocs.io](https://bandit.readthedocs.io/en/latest/) | [integrated] | AST-based security linter for Python with plugin tests (B1xx-B7xx) for dangerous calls, weak crypto, injection-prone APIs, hardcoded passwords etc. No data-flow or taint tracking. Integrate in-process: it is a pure-Python, Apache-2.0 library, so CodeKavach can import bandit.core directly or call the CLI. Serves as the Python candidate generator whose high false-positive rate is the motivating case for LLM triage; also the rule-based baseline used in several 2025-26 LLM-vs-SAST papers. |
| Cppcheck | GPL-3.0 | [GitHub: cppcheck-opensource/cppcheck](https://github.com/cppcheck-opensource/cppcheck); [cppcheck.sourceforge.io](https://cppcheck.sourceforge.io/) | [integrated] | C/C++ analyzer that works without a build system, using its own parser and value-flow analysis to find undefined behaviour, buffer errors, leaks and misuse of APIs. Integrate by subprocess (GPL-3.0 is fine for a separately executed tool, but do not link or copy code). Particularly valuable for audits because it needs no compile database. Lipp et al. (ISSTA 2022) show its recall on real CVEs is low, which supports the LLM-assisted approach. |
| gosec | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: securego/gosec](https://github.com/securego/gosec) | [integrated] | Go security checker that inspects the Go AST and SSA form for issues such as hardcoded credentials, SQL string building, weak crypto, unsafe file permissions and integer overflows. Integrate by subprocess (single Go binary, Apache-2.0) with `-fmt sarif`. Requires a buildable module for SSA-based rules, so CodeKavach should degrade gracefully when the client repo does not compile. |
| Psalm (taint analysis mode) | MIT | [psalm.dev](https://psalm.dev/docs/security_analysis/); [GitHub: vimeo/psalm](https://github.com/vimeo/psalm) | [integrated] | PHP static type checker with a built-in inter-procedural taint analysis (`--taint-analysis`) that tracks user input to SQL, HTML, shell, file, SSRF and other sink types. Integrate as the primary PHP taint engine (MIT; run via bundled PHP + composer in the Docker image). Its taint traces feed L3 slicing for PHP, the language where banks' legacy portals often live. |
| Pysa (Python Static Analyzer) | MIT | [GitHub: facebook/Pysa](https://github.com/facebook/Pysa); [pyre-check.org](https://pyre-check.org/docs/pysa-basics/) | [integrated] | Inter-procedural taint analysis for Python used at Meta/Instagram scale, driven by .pysa model files declaring sources, sinks, sanitizers and rules. Integrate as the deep Python taint engine (MIT). Its source-to-sink traces (with intermediate frames) are exactly the 'taint path' that L3 sends in pseudonymised form. Caveats: needs a type-checkable project and model-writing effort; OCaml binary via pip, Linux/macOS only. Track the repo move so install docs do not point at the archived [facebook/pyre-check](https://github.com/facebook/pyre-check) (archived June 2026). |
| SpotBugs + Find Security Bugs | LGPL-3.0 (FindSecBugs), LGPL-2.1 (SpotBugs) | [GitHub: find-sec-bugs/find-sec-bugs](https://github.com/find-sec-bugs/find-sec-bugs); [GitHub: spotbugs/spotbugs](https://github.com/spotbugs/spotbugs) | [integrated] | SpotBugs is the successor of FindBugs and analyses JVM bytecode; the Find Security Bugs plugin adds detectors for 144 vulnerability types including a bytecode-level taint analysis for injection classes, with coverage for Java, Kotlin, Scala, Groovy and Android. Integrate by subprocess for Java/Kotlin when compiled classes are available (or after a best-effort build inside the sandbox). LGPL tools run as separate JVM processes, so no licence impact on MIT code. Weakness to note in the paper: needs bytecode, which audit clients often cannot provide. |
| .NET / Roslyn analyzers (Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.NetAnalyzers) | MIT | [learn.microsoft.com](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/fundamentals/code-analysis/overview); [GitHub: dotnet/roslyn-analyzers](https://github.com/dotnet/roslyn-analyzers) | [planned] | Compiler-integrated analyzers shipped with the .NET SDK; the CA2100, CA3xxx and CA5xxx families cover SQL injection review, injection sinks. Integrate for C#: run `dotnet build` with analysis level raised and ErrorLog SARIF inside the sandbox. Requires a restorable/buildable solution, so provide an Opengrep-only fallback for non-building audits. |
| Brakeman | Brakeman Public Use License (non-commercial; code before 15 Jun 2018 was MIT) | [GitHub: presidentbeef/brakeman](https://github.com/presidentbeef/brakeman); [related page](https://github.com/presidentbeef/brakeman/blob/main/LICENSE.md) | [planned] | Static analyzer specialised for Ruby on Rails that understands Rails conventions (routes, params, ActiveRecord, templates) and performs a simplified data-flow from request parameters to sinks; outputs JSON, SARIF, HTML, CSV and more. Optional plugin only, never bundled. The licence allows analysing your own software for free but names 'component of a value-added service/product' and SaaS as Commercial Use needing a paid licence. |
| RuboCop (Security cops) | MIT | [GitHub: rubocop/rubocop](https://github.com/rubocop/rubocop); [docs.rubocop.org](https://docs.rubocop.org/rubocop/cops_security.html) | [planned] | Ruby linter whose Security department flags `eval`, `Kernel#open` with dynamic input, `Marshal.load`, unsafe YAML and JSON loading and similar calls. Pattern-level only, no taint tracking, so far weaker than Brakeman on Rails; listed because it is the only permissively licensed Ruby engine that may be bundled, which matters while Brakeman's licence restricts service use. Run it with `--only Security` and a CodeKavach-supplied configuration, ignoring the repository's `.rubocop.yml`: a repo-supplied RuboCop configuration that loaded code was the entry point of the CodeRabbit compromise (section 1.9). |
| Clang Static Analyzer and clang-tidy | Apache-2.0 WITH LLVM-exception | [clang-analyzer.llvm.org](https://clang-analyzer.llvm.org/); [clang.llvm.org](https://clang.llvm.org/extra/clang-tidy/) | [planned] | The Clang Static Analyzer performs path-sensitive symbolic execution of C, C++ and Objective-C within a translation unit (with optional cross-TU mode). Integrate through CodeChecker or scan-build when a compile_commands.json is available. Permissive licence. |
| CodeChecker | Apache-2.0 WITH LLVM-exception | [GitHub: Ericsson/codechecker](https://github.com/Ericsson/codechecker); [codechecker.readthedocs.io](https://codechecker.readthedocs.io/en/latest/) | [inspiration] | Python-based driver, result store and web UI for Clang Static Analyzer, clang-tidy, Cppcheck, GCC -fanalyzer and Infer. Design inspiration for CodeKavach's findings store (run diffing, review status, suppression comments) and a ready-made wrapper for the C/C++ plugin. Written in Python, so parts can be reused directly under its permissive licence. |
| eslint-plugin-security | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: eslint-community/eslint-plugin-security](https://github.com/eslint-community/eslint-plugin-security) | [planned] | ESLint rule set flagging Node.js security hot-spots such as eval with expressions, non-literal require/fs paths, child_process use. Integrate via a bundled Node runtime: run ESLint with a CodeKavach-controlled flat config and a SARIF formatter, ignoring the client's own ESLint config to get reproducible results. |
| Flawfinder | GPL-2.0 | [GitHub: david-a-wheeler/flawfinder](https://github.com/david-a-wheeler/flawfinder); [dwheeler.com](https://dwheeler.com/flawfinder/) | [planned] | Single-file Python program that lexically scans C/C++ for calls to risky functions (strcpy, system, random...) and ranks hits by risk level with CWE identifiers; no parsing or data-flow. Integrate as a trivial-to-run C/C++ candidate generator (pure Python, subprocess to respect GPL-2.0). Its very high false-positive rate makes it a good stress test for the LLM triage stage at different privacy levels. |
| Infer | MIT | [GitHub: facebook/infer](https://github.com/facebook/infer); [doi:10.1145/3338112](https://doi.org/10.1145/3338112) | [planned] | Compositional inter-procedural analyzer (OCaml) for Java, C, C++ and Objective-C based on separation logic/bi-abduction (Pulse). Optional deep engine for C/C++/Java memory-safety findings (MIT). Needs to intercept the build (`infer run - make`), so it fits CI mode better than one-off audits. |
| MobSF + mobsfscan | LGPL-3.0 (mobsfscan), GPL-3.0 (MobSF) | [GitHub: MobSF/mobsfscan](https://github.com/MobSF/mobsfscan); [GitHub: MobSF/Mobile-Security-Framework-MobSF](https://github.com/MobSF/Mobile-Security-Framework-MobSF) | [planned] | mobsfscan is a Python CLI/library that finds insecure patterns in Android and iOS source (Java, Kotlin, Swift, Objective-C) using MobSF's rules on top of semgrep and libsast, with JSON, SARIF, SonarQube and HTML output. Integrate njsscan as a pip dependency invoked via its CLI (keeps LGPL separate). Note it depends on semgrep under the hood, so Semgrep CE's platform limits (no native Windows) carry over. |
| Security Code Scan | LGPL-3.0 | [GitHub: security-code-scan/security-code-scan](https://github.com/security-code-scan/security-code-scan); [security-code-scan.github.io](https://security-code-scan.github.io/) | [planned] | Roslyn-based security analyzer for C# and VB.NET with inter-procedural taint analysis for SQLi, XSS, path traversal, XXE, open redirect and more. Evaluate as the JS/TS call-graph provider for the own taint/slicing engine: tree-sitter gives syntax only, and JS call resolution (callbacks, dynamic property access, module loading) is the hard part of building inter-procedural L3 slices for Node.js code. |

Apache-2.0 is compatible with an MIT project (keep the NOTICE/licence text if code is copied). |

**Aggregators and orchestration**

| Tool | Licence | Link | Role tag | Notes incl. licence caveats |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ASH - Automated Security Helper | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: awslabs/automated-security-helper](https://github.com/awslabs/automated-security-helper); [awslabs.github.io](https://awslabs.github.io/automated-security-helper/) | [inspiration] | Actively maintained Python orchestrator (v3) that installs and runs Bandit, Semgrep, Opengrep, Checkov. Closest living analogue of CodeKavach's local deterministic stage (Python, plugin scanners, SARIF-centred, offline switch) now that Horusec, Salus and sast-scan are dormant. |
| Coinbase Salus | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: coinbase/salus](https://github.com/coinbase/salus) | [inspiration] | Ruby-based Docker container that decides which scanners apply to a repository (Bandit, Brakeman, gosec, Semgrep, npm/yarn audit, etc.), runs them with central configuration and produces a combined report for CI. Design reference for organisation-wide default configuration with per-repo overrides; unmaintained, so not a dependency. |
| Curated tool lists: analysis-tools.dev and OWASP Source Code Analysis Tools | MIT (analysis-tools list) | [GitHub: analysis-tools-dev/static-analysis](https://github.com/analysis-tools-dev/static-analysis); [owasp.org](https://owasp.org/www-community/Source_Code_Analysis_Tools) | [related-work] | Two maintained catalogues of static analyzers by language, including licence and maintenance status flags. Use to justify tool selection in the paper's methodology section and to re-check periodically for new engines or deprecations. |
| Horusec | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: ZupIT/horusec](https://github.com/ZupIT/horusec) | [inspiration] | Go-based orchestrator that runs a set of open-source scanners (Bandit, Brakeman, gosec, Semgrep, Gitleaks, Trivy and its own regex engines) in Docker and merges results, with an optional web dashboard. Design reference only for the orchestrator/plugin model; do not depend on it. |
| MegaLinter | AGPL-3.0 | [GitHub: oxsecurity/megalinter](https://github.com/oxsecurity/megalinter); [megalinter.io](https://megalinter.io/latest/) | [inspiration] | Docker-based meta-runner that orchestrates 100+ linters and security scanners (Bandit, Semgrep, gitleaks, Trivy, Checkov, DevSkim, etc.) with unified configuration and an aggregated SARIF reporter. Design reference for flavoured Docker images and per-linter descriptors. AGPL-3.0: do not copy code or build the hosted product on it; if used at all, run the upstream image unmodified. |
| OWASP secureCodeBox | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: secureCodeBox/secureCodeBox](https://github.com/secureCodeBox/secureCodeBox); [securecodebox.io](https://www.securecodebox.io/) | [inspiration] | Kubernetes-native toolchain that runs security scanners as custom resources. Reference for the hosted/cluster deployment mode: scanner-as-job isolation and hook-based result routing. Too heavy for the default single-container or air-gapped install. |
| ShiftLeft / AppThreat sast-scan ('Scan') | Apache-2.0 (ShiftLeft repo); MIT (archived AppThreat original) | [GitHub: ShiftLeftSecurity/sast-scan](https://github.com/ShiftLeftSecurity/sast-scan); [GitHub: AppThreat/sast-scan](https://github.com/AppThreat/sast-scan) | [related-work] | Docker image bundling many OSS scanners behind one CLI with SARIF and HTML reports. Historical reference for a SARIF-first aggregator; do not depend on it. Its successor ideas live on in dep-scan and atom. |
| Super-linter | MIT | [GitHub: super-linter/super-linter](https://github.com/super-linter/super-linter) | [inspiration] | Containerised bundle of linters for dozens of languages and config formats, runnable as a GitHub Action or under any OCI runtime. Reference for packaging many analyzers in one reproducible image and for GitHub Action ergonomics; the MIT alternative to AGPL-licensed MegaLinter if any orchestration code were to be borrowed. |

**Licence terms that constrain bundling**

| Tool | Licence | Link | Role tag | Notes incl. licence caveats |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Semgrep licensing (engine LGPL-2.1, Semgrep Rules License v1.0) | LGPL-2.1 (CE engine); Semgrep Rules License v1.0 (registry rules) | [docs.semgrep.dev](https://docs.semgrep.dev/licensing); [GitHub: semgrep/semgrep-rules](https://github.com/semgrep/semgrep-rules/blob/develop/LICENSE) | [inspiration] | Semgrep CE is LGPL-2.1, but Semgrep-maintained rules are under a source-available licence limited to internal business use: vendors may not use them in competing products or SaaS offerings. CodeKavach (especially a hosted deployment or a paid audit service built on it) must not redistribute the Semgrep registry rules. Ship own rules plus permissively licensed community rules; let clients fetch Semgrep rules themselves for internal use. |
| Semgrep Rules License v1.0 (semgrep-rules registry) | Semgrep Rules License v1.0 (source-available, NOT open source) | [semgrep.dev](https://semgrep.dev/legal/rules-license); [GitHub: semgrep/semgrep-rules](https://github.com/semgrep/semgrep-rules) | [inspiration] | Licence that has governed the Semgrep-maintained community rule pack since December 2024. Threat/legal input: a consulting or hosted CodeKavach that ships or runs these rules for a client (bank) is exactly the 'available to others as a service' case. CodeKavach must (a) never vendor semgrep-rules in its MIT repo or Docker image, (b) let the client fetch rules under their own licence acceptance. |
| CodeQL CLI licence terms | Proprietary terms (CLI); MIT (query libraries) | [GitHub: github/codeql-cli-binaries](https://github.com/github/codeql-cli-binaries/blob/main/LICENSE.md); [GitHub: github/codeql](https://github.com/github/codeql) | [inspiration] | The CodeQL CLI may be used free of charge for academic research and for analysing open-source codebases hosted on GitHub.com. The queries repo itself is MIT. Hard constraint: CodeKavach cannot bundle or silently run CodeQL against a bank's proprietary code. Use it only in the research evaluation on OSS benchmarks, or as an optional engine the client enables under its own GHAS licence. |

### 3.2 Parsers and program-analysis frameworks

**Parsers and structural search**

| Tool | Licence | Link | Role tag | Notes incl. licence caveats |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ast-grep | MIT | [GitHub: ast-grep/ast-grep](https://github.com/ast-grep/ast-grep); [ast-grep.github.io](https://ast-grep.github.io/) | [integrated] | Rust CLI and library for structural search, lint and rewrite on tree-sitter ASTs using code-like patterns and YAML rules. Integrate as an importable library: `pip install ast-grep-py` gives in-process pattern matching over the same tree-sitter trees CodeKavach already builds, which is ideal for (1) fast candidate-finding rules and (2) the L2 pseudonymiser's structural rewrites (rename identifiers, blank string literals) without regex hacks. MIT, no licence friction. |
| tree-sitter (+ py-tree-sitter bindings) | MIT | [GitHub: tree-sitter/tree-sitter](https://github.com/tree-sitter/tree-sitter); [GitHub: tree-sitter/py-tree-sitter](https://github.com/tree-sitter/py-tree-sitter) | [integrated] | Incremental, error-tolerant GLR parser generator and runtime that yields concrete syntax trees for hundreds of languages and has an S-expression query language for pattern matching. py-tree-sitter provides the official Python bindings as pre-built wheels. Core dependency: the single parsing layer for ingest, for L1/L2 token classification (identifiers, strings, comments are node types, so pseudonymisation is grammar-driven rather than regex-driven), for function-boundary extraction in L3 and for the L4 abstract IR. |
| tree-sitter-language-pack | MIT (vendored code must be permissively licensed, no copyleft) | [GitHub: xberg-io/tree-sitter-language-pack](https://github.com/xberg-io/tree-sitter-language-pack); [PyPI](https://pypi.org/project/tree-sitter-language-pack/) | [integrated] | Pre-compiled bundle of tree-sitter grammars (README currently claims 371 languages at ABI 14) with Python, Node, Go, Java, C# and other bindings. Core dependency: one wheel gives every grammar CodeKavach needs with no compiler in the air-gapped image, and the permissive-only policy keeps the MIT distribution clean. Pin the version: grammar updates change node types and can silently break queries and pseudonymisation rules. |
| LibCST | MIT | [GitHub: Instagram/LibCST](https://github.com/Instagram/LibCST); [libcst.readthedocs.io](https://libcst.readthedocs.io/) | [planned] | Concrete syntax tree library for Python that preserves all formatting and comments and provides visitors, transformers. Use for high-fidelity Python pseudonymisation at L2: scope-aware renaming guarantees that every occurrence of an identifier gets the same pseudonym and that the transformed code still parses, which the leakage/utility experiments depend on. |
| Comby | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: comby-tools/comby](https://github.com/comby-tools/comby); [comby.dev](https://comby.dev/) | [planned] | Language-aware structural search-and-replace tool (OCaml) that matches balanced delimiters. Fallback rewriting tool for languages without a good tree-sitter grammar when pseudonymising comments/strings (L2). |
| srcML | GPL-3.0 | [srcml.org](https://www.srcml.org/); [GitHub: srcML/srcML](https://github.com/srcML/srcML) | [inspiration] | Converts C, C++, C# and Java source into an XML document that interleaves the original text (including comments, whitespace and preprocessor lines) with syntactic markup, and converts back losslessly; queried with XPath. Reference / research comparator for lossless round-tripping, which is the property L2 pseudonymisation and local de-pseudonymisation need. GPL-3.0 and only four languages, so tree-sitter byte offsets are the chosen mechanism instead. |
| Universal Ctags | GPL-2.0 | [GitHub: universal-ctags/ctags](https://github.com/universal-ctags/ctags); [ctags.io](https://ctags.io/) | [planned] | Maintained fork of Exuberant Ctags that produces a definition index (functions, classes, members, macros) for well over 100 languages, with JSON output. Fallback symbol inventory for languages lacking a tree-sitter grammar; the identifier list seeds the pseudonym dictionary. Run as a subprocess (GPL-2.0). |

**Program-analysis frameworks**

| Tool | Licence | Link | Role tag | Notes incl. licence caveats |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Tai-e | LGPL-3.0 | [GitHub: pascal-lab/Tai-e](https://github.com/pascal-lab/Tai-e); [doi:10.1145/3597926.3598120](https://doi.org/10.1145/3597926.3598120) | [planned] | Modern Java static-analysis framework designed for developer friendliness, offering a clean IR. Optional Java deep-taint backend and the best teaching reference for the team's own taint engine design (plugin hooks on a pointer-analysis solver). Runs as a JVM process on bytecode; LGPL-3.0 is fine at process boundary. |
| Souffle Datalog | UPL-1.0 | [GitHub: souffle-lang/souffle](https://github.com/souffle-lang/souffle); [doi:10.1007/978-3-319-41540-6\_23](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-41540-6_23) | [planned] | Datalog dialect and compiler that synthesises parallel C++ from logic programs, built for large-scale static analysis. Candidate engine for CodeKavach's own taint rules if they are expressed as Datalog over tree-sitter-derived facts: fact files (.facts TSV) are easy to emit from Python, and UPL-1.0 is permissive. |
| Soot and SootUp | LGPL-2.1 | [GitHub: soot-oss/SootUp](https://github.com/soot-oss/SootUp); [doi:10.1007/978-3-031-57246-3\_13](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-57246-3_13) | [related-work] | Soot is the long-standing Java/Android bytecode analysis and transformation framework built around the Jimple IR. Reference / substrate: needed only indirectly (FlowDroid, Joern's bytecode frontend). |
| PhASAR | MIT | [GitHub: secure-software-engineering/phasar](https://github.com/secure-software-engineering/phasar); [doi:10.1007/978-3-030-17465-1\_22](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-17465-1_22) | [planned] | LLVM-based framework for writing inter-procedural data-flow analyses for C/C++ using IFDS, IDE and monotone solvers. Optional C/C++ taint backend with the friendliest licence (MIT) among LLVM-IR frameworks; requires compiling the target to LLVM IR, so it is practical in CI mode but rarely in third-party audits. |
| SVF (Static Value-Flow analysis) | AGPL-3.0 | [GitHub: SVF-tools/SVF](https://github.com/SVF-tools/SVF); [doi:10.1145/2892208.2892235](https://doi.org/10.1145/2892208.2892235) | [baseline] | Scalable inter-procedural pointer and value-flow analysis on LLVM IR that builds sparse value-flow graphs used for memory-leak. Reference / research baseline. Licence trap: AGPL-3.0 means a hosted CodeKavach that links or modifies SVF would have to publish corresponding source; only ever call it as an external, optional tool. |
| Doop | UPL-1.0 (current LICENSE, (c) 2017 PLAST lab and Martin Bravenboer); original 2008-2009 Doop code under an MIT-style licence (LICENSE-doop-orig); bundled Scaler/Zipper have their own licence | [GitHub: plast-lab/doop](https://github.com/plast-lab/doop); [doi:10.1145/1640089.1640108](https://doi.org/10.1145/1640089.1640108) | [inspiration] | Declarative pointer-analysis framework for Java bytecode in which context-sensitive points-to. Reference for the Datalog style of analysis specification, which maps well onto L4 'abstract facts only' questions (facts such as flows(src, sink) reveal no identifiers once pseudonymised). |
| WALA (T.J. Watson Libraries for Analysis) | EPL-2.0 | [GitHub: wala/WALA](https://github.com/wala/WALA) | [inspiration] | Java library providing class-hierarchy, call-graph, pointer analysis, an RHS/IFDS tabulation solver and a context-sensitive slicer over system dependence graphs, for Java bytecode and JavaScript front ends. Reference implementation of inter-procedural slicing (its Slicer class is one of few production-grade SDG slicers). |

**Code property graphs and slicing**

| Tool | Licence | Link | Role tag | Notes incl. licence caveats |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Joern (open-source code property graph platform) | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: joernio/joern](https://github.com/joernio/joern); [docs.joern.io](https://docs.joern.io/cpg-slicing/) | [integrated] | Builds a code property graph (AST + CFG + PDG merged in one queryable graph) for C/C++, Java, JVM bytecode, JS/TS, Python, Kotlin, PHP, Go, Ruby, Swift, C# and binaries (via Ghidra). Integrate as the heavyweight cross-language taint/slicing backend for privacy level L3: run joern-parse + joern-slice locally (JVM, JDK 21) via subprocess, read the JSON slice, then pseudonymise only the slice before it goes to the LLM. Apache-2.0 so it can be bundled in the Docker image. |
| AppThreat atom | MIT | [GitHub: AppThreat/atom](https://github.com/AppThreat/atom) | [planned] | Standalone CPG-style intermediate representation generator (built on 'chen', a Joern-derived library) for C/C++, Java, JS/TS, Python, PHP, Ruby. Integrate candidate for L3: MIT licence, native binary (no JVM needed), and its 'reachables' slices are already source-to-sink shaped JSON, which is what the privacy layer needs. |
| Fraunhofer AISEC cpg | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: Fraunhofer-AISEC/cpg](https://github.com/Fraunhofer-AISEC/cpg) | [planned] [baseline] | Kotlin/JVM library that extracts a code property graph from C/C++, Java, Go, LLVM-IR and (with less mature frontends) Python and TypeScript. Alternative backend to Joern for the CPG plugin interface, and a useful comparison point for robustness on non-building bank repositories (a very common audit situation). Apache-2.0 permits bundling. |
| dg (LLVM dependence-graph library and llvm-slicer) | MIT | [GitHub: mchalupa/dg](https://github.com/mchalupa/dg); [doi:10.1016/j.simpa.2020.100038](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.simpa.2020.100038) | [planned] | C++ library implementing pointer analysis, data- and control-dependence analysis and dependence graphs for LLVM bitcode. Optional C/C++ slicing backend when the client code compiles to LLVM bitcode: gives sound dependence-based slices for the L3 experiments and a baseline against the Joern/tree-sitter slicer. |

**Code intelligence and indexing**

| Tool | Licence | Link | Role tag | Notes incl. licence caveats |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| multilspy | MIT | [GitHub: microsoft/multilspy](https://github.com/microsoft/multilspy) | [planned] | Python library that downloads, launches and drives language servers (Java, Python, Rust, C#, TypeScript/JavaScript, Go and others) behind one API for definitions, references, hover and completions. Use as a pure-Python way to get semantic facts (call targets, references, types) for the slicer without writing per-language resolvers. |
| SCIP and LSIF code-intelligence index formats | Apache-2.0 (SCIP) | [GitHub: scip-code/scip](https://github.com/scip-code/scip); [microsoft.github.io](https://microsoft.github.io/language-server-protocol/specifications/lsif/0.6.0/specification/) | [planned] | LSIF is Microsoft's graph dump format for pre-computed language-server answers; SCIP is Sourcegraph's Protobuf-based successor with human-readable symbol strings and indexers for TypeScript, Java/Kotlin/Scala, Python, Go, Rust, C/C++, C# and Ruby. Use for precise cross-file definition/reference resolution, which tree-sitter cannot provide: SCIP indexes let the slicer follow a call into another file and let the pseudonymiser rename a symbol consistently across the repository. |

Reference only: both need build integration and server infrastructure disproportionate to an audit tool. |

Candidate primary secrets engine going forward (MIT, single static binary, air-gap friendly when validation is off). The BPE token-efficiency idea is design inspiration for CodeKavach's own 'is this literal secret-like?' heuristic in the L1/L2 string redactor. |

Importable directly as a Python library inside the CodeKavach core (licence-compatible), giving an in-process L1 detector with no subprocess; its baseline concept maps to CodeKavach's 'accepted risk / suppressed finding' store, and its entropy plugins are the reference implementation for the entropy-based detection baseline. |
| Gitleaks | MIT | [GitHub: gitleaks/gitleaks](https://github.com/gitleaks/gitleaks) | [integrated] [inspiration] | Go-based regex + entropy + keyword secret scanner for git history, directories and stdin, driven by a TOML rule file and emitting JSON/SARIF/CSV. Integrate as a default L1 secrets engine (subprocess, SARIF/JSON output) and reuse its TOML rule corpus as one input to the redaction pattern set; it is also a baseline tool in SecretBench-style evaluation. Plan a migration path to Betterleaks because of the maintenance freeze. |
| Titus | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: praetorian-inc/titus](https://github.com/praetorian-inc/titus); [praetorian.com](https://www.praetorian.com/blog/titus-open-source-secret-scanner/) | [planned] | Go secrets scanner that replaces Nosey Parker: 487 rules derived from Nosey Parker and Kingfisher, Hyperscan/Vectorscan matching. Alternative high-throughput engine for very large monorepos; rule corpus is Apache-2.0 so patterns can be imported into CodeKavach's redaction pattern set with attribution. |
| Kingfisher | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: mongodb/kingfisher](https://github.com/mongodb/kingfisher); [mongodb.com](https://www.mongodb.com/company/blog/product-release-announcements/introducing-kingfisher-real-time-secret-detection-validation) | [planned] [inspiration] | Rust secret scanner using a multithreaded Vectorscan regex engine with language-aware verification, hundreds of built-in rules. Optional fast engine; its source-code-aware verification step (parsing to confirm a match sits in a string/assignment context) is design inspiration for combining tree-sitter context with regex hits in CodeKavach to cut false positives. |
| CredSweeper | MIT | [GitHub: Samsung/CredSweeper](https://github.com/Samsung/CredSweeper); [GitHub: Samsung/CredData](https://github.com/Samsung/CredData) | [planned] | Python credential scanner that couples pattern rules with a machine-learning validation stage to suppress false positives. Python-native, MIT, and runs fully offline - a good in-process second opinion for the L1 detector, and an ML-filter baseline to compare against CodeKavach's LLM-based triage of secret candidates. |
| Secrets Patterns DB | CC-BY-SA-4.0 | [GitHub: mazen160/secrets-patterns-db](https://github.com/mazen160/secrets-patterns-db) | [planned] | Open collection of over 1,600 secret-detection regular expressions in a tool-agnostic YAML format with exporters for TruffleHog and Gitleaks. Extra recall for the L1 redaction pattern set. Licence caveat: CC-BY-SA-4.0 is share-alike - load it as an optional external rule pack fetched at install time rather than vendoring the regexes into the MIT repository. |
| TruffleHog | AGPL-3.0 | [GitHub: trufflesecurity/trufflehog](https://github.com/trufflesecurity/trufflehog) | [planned] | Secret scanner with hundreds of provider-specific detectors and optional live verification of candidate credentials against the provider API. Optional engine invoked strictly as an external subprocess with verification disabled (--no-verification) so no client secret is sent to third parties. |
| secretlint | MIT | [GitHub: secretlint/secretlint](https://github.com/secretlint/secretlint) | [inspiration] | Pluggable Node.js linter for credentials with opt-in rule packages per provider, documented rule IDs and SARIF output. Reference for rule-ID/documentation conventions in the VS Code extension and for JS/TS-first projects; optional plugin rather than default engine. |

Historical baseline only; shows the pre-commit-hook deployment model. Not integrated. |
| GitHub secret scanning and push protection | Proprietary service | [docs.github.com](https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/concepts/secret-security/secret-scanning) | [baseline] | GitHub's native partner-pattern secret scanning with push protection; provider partnerships allow automatic token revocation. Cite as prior art and mine its rule set, but do not integrate: the project is archived. Its de-duplication-by-secret-value idea is reused for report noise reduction. |

Design inspiration for key-aware detection in config files (password=, connectionString=) which regex/entropy tools miss; can be imported as a Python library. Use the maintained adeptex fork - the Skyscanner repo was archived on 25 Oct 2023. |
