| Privado (open-source PII data-flow scanner for code) | GPL-3.0 and LGPL-3.0 (COPYING and COPYING.LESSER in the repo) | [GitHub: Privado-Inc/privado](https://github.com/Privado-Inc/privado) | [inspiration] | Static analyser that discovers personal-data elements in source code (110+ element types by README) and traces their ... One of the few tools that look for PII handling in code rather than PII values in text. Copyleft licence: external process only, never vendored. |
| scrubadub | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: LeapBeyond/scrubadub](https://github.com/LeapBeyond/scrubadub); [scrubadub.readthedocs.io](https://scrubadub.readthedocs.io/en/stable/) | [planned] | Lightweight Python library that replaces PII in free text with typed placeholders via pluggable detectors. Minimal-dependency fallback and a simple reference for the placeholder format ({{EMAIL-1}}) used in L1. |

### 3.5 Software composition analysis and SBOM

**Scanners, SBOM generators and libraries**

| Tool | Licence | Link | Role tag | Notes incl. licence caveats |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| OSV-Scanner | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: google/osv-scanner](https://github.com/google/osv-scanner); [google.github.io](https://google.github.io/osv-scanner/usage/offline-mode/) | [integrated] | Lockfile/SBOM/container scanner backed by OSV.dev with precise version-range matching, experimental guided remediation. Default SCA engine: Apache-2.0, open data, JSON/SARIF output and a documented --offline mode for air-gapped banks. Only package names and versions are queried, which should still be routed through a local DB mirror at L3/L4. |
| Trivy | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: aquasecurity/trivy](https://github.com/aquasecurity/trivy); [related page](https://github.com/aquasecurity/trivy/security/advisories/GHSA-69fq-xp46-6x23) | [planned] [inspiration] | All-in-one scanner for OS packages, language dependencies, IaC misconfiguration (absorbing tfsec), secrets, licences and SBOM generation over filesystems, repos and container images. Second SCA/IaC engine and the container-image scanner. The 2026 compromise is a direct threat-model input: CodeKavach must pin scanner binaries by digest, verify signatures, and run third-party engines in a no-network sandbox so that a trojanised scanner cannot exfiltrate client code. |
| Grype + Syft | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: anchore/grype](https://github.com/anchore/grype); [GitHub: anchore/syft](https://github.com/anchore/syft) | [integrated] | Syft builds SBOMs (CycloneDX, SPDX, Syft JSON) from directories and images; Grype matches an SBOM or target against its own aggregated vulnerability DB (grype-db) and can annotate results with EPSS and KEV. Syft is the SBOM producer for the compliance annex; Grype gives a second, independently curated matcher for cross-checking OSV-Scanner, following research showing SCA tools disagree substantially. |
| OWASP dep-scan | MIT | [GitHub: owasp-dep-scan/dep-scan](https://github.com/owasp-dep-scan/dep-scan); [depscan.readthedocs.io](https://depscan.readthedocs.io/) | [integrated] | Python SCA and risk-audit tool built on cdxgen SBOMs and the AppThreat vulnerability DB. Licence-compatible, Python-native and the only mainstream OSS SCA with multi-language reachability - the closest match to CodeKavach's 'vulnerability-relevant slice' idea applied to dependencies. Integrate or use as the reachability baseline. |
| cdxgen | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: cdxgen/cdxgen](https://github.com/cdxgen/cdxgen); [cdxgen.github.io](https://cdxgen.github.io/cdxgen/) | [planned] | Polyglot CycloneDX SBOM generator covering many languages and build systems, with evidence and call-stack enrichment. SBOM generator for multi-language repos where Syft's coverage is weaker; input to dep-scan and Dependency-Track. |
| govulncheck | BSD-3-Clause | [pkg.go.dev](https://pkg.go.dev/golang.org/x/vuln/cmd/govulncheck); [GitHub: golang/vuln](https://github.com/golang/vuln) | [planned] | Official Go vulnerability checker that uses static call-graph analysis to report only vulnerabilities whose affected symbols are actually reachable. Gold-standard example of symbol-level reachability shipped in a mainstream toolchain; integrate for Go targets and cite as design inspiration for slice-level dependency findings. |
| OWASP Dependency-Check | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: dependency-check/DependencyCheck](https://github.com/dependency-check/DependencyCheck); [owasp.org](https://owasp.org/projects/dependency-check) | [planned] | CPE-based SCA that derives evidence from artefacts (JARs, .NET assemblies, etc.) and matches against NVD. Optional engine for Java/.NET binaries without lockfiles. |
| pip-audit | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: pypa/pip-audit](https://github.com/pypa/pip-audit) | [planned] | Audits Python environments and requirements files against the PyPI Advisory Database / OSV, with CycloneDX output. In-process Python SCA (importable) for Python targets and for CodeKavach's own supply-chain hygiene in CI. |
| Python SBOM and purl libraries: cyclonedx-python-lib, spdx tools-python, packageurl-python | Apache-2.0 (cyclonedx-python-lib, spdx/tools-python); MIT (packageurl-python) | [GitHub: CycloneDX/cyclonedx-python-lib](https://github.com/CycloneDX/cyclonedx-python-lib); [GitHub: package-url/packageurl-python](https://github.com/package-url/packageurl-python) | [planned] | Reference Python data models and serialisers for CycloneDX, a parser/validator/writer for SPDX documents. In-process building blocks for the Python 3.12 core: emit and validate the SBOM/VEX annex without shelling out, and normalise every component to a purl before OSV/EPSS/KEV joins. All three are licence-compatible with MIT. |
| Retire.js | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: RetireJS/retire.js](https://github.com/RetireJS/retire.js); [retirejs.github.io](https://retirejs.github.io/retire.js/) | [planned] | Detects vulnerable JavaScript libraries by filename, URL, hash and content signatures - i.e. vendored/minified copies that lockfile-based SCA cannot see. Covers vendored front-end libraries (jquery.min.js committed into a bank's legacy app), a common real-audit finding. |
| ScanCode Toolkit | Apache-2.0 (code); CC-BY-4.0 (reference datasets) | [GitHub: aboutcode-org/scancode-toolkit](https://github.com/aboutcode-org/scancode-toolkit); [scancode-toolkit.readthedocs.io](https://scancode-toolkit.readthedocs.io/en/stable/) | [planned] | Scans a codebase for licences, copyrights, package manifests and dependencies using full-text licence matching rather than approximate regexes. Licence-compliance leg of SCA (copyleft-in-proprietary-code findings that bank audits expect). Runs fully offline. |
| OpenSSF Scorecard, Best Practices Badge and OSPS Baseline | Apache-2.0 (ossf/scorecard and ossf/security-baseline LICENSE files); the Best Practices Badge application (coreinfrastructure/best-practices-badge) is MIT | [scorecard.dev](https://scorecard.dev/); [baseline.openssf.org](https://baseline.openssf.org/) | [planned] | Scorecard runs automated checks of an open-source repository's security posture ... Dependency-risk signal in the SCA section of reports, and a to-do list for the CodeKavach repository itself: a security tool should publish a good Scorecard result. |
| cargo-audit / RustSec Advisory Database | Apache-2.0 OR MIT (tooling); advisory data public domain (CC0-style) | [GitHub: rustsec/rustsec](https://github.com/rustsec/rustsec); [GitHub: rustsec/advisory-db](https://github.com/rustsec/advisory-db) | [planned] | Audits Cargo.lock against the community RustSec advisory DB, which is also exported to OSV. Plugin for Rust targets; the advisory DB is an example of openly licensed ecosystem data safe to mirror offline. |
| Eclipse Steady | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: eclipse-steady/steady](https://github.com/eclipse-steady/steady); [doi:10.1007/s10664-020-09830-x](https://doi.org/10.1007/s10664-020-09830-x) | [planned] | Code-centric SCA for Java and Python that identifies vulnerable code constructs from fix commits and combines static and dynamic reachability analysis to tell whether the application can execute them. Research-grade reference implementation of code-centric (not metadata-centric) SCA; informs how CodeKavach can join its own call graph with vulnerable-symbol data. |
| GuardDog and the OpenSSF malicious-packages dataset | Apache-2.0 (both) | [GitHub: DataDog/guarddog](https://github.com/DataDog/guarddog); [GitHub: ossf/malicious-packages](https://github.com/ossf/malicious-packages) | [planned] | GuardDog flags malicious packages by running YARA source-code rules and package-metadata heuristics. Extends SCA from 'known CVE' to 'known or suspected malware' - important after the 2025-2026 wave of registry and CI supply-chain attacks. |
| npm audit | Artistic-2.0 (npm CLI) | [docs.npmjs.com](https://docs.npmjs.com/cli/v10/commands/npm-audit/) | [inspiration] | Built-in npm command that submits the dependency tree to the registry's advisory endpoint (GitHub Advisory Database) and reports vulnerable paths. Reference behaviour for JS projects; not used in air-gapped mode because it needs the registry. Note that it sends the full dependency tree to a remote endpoint. |
| OSS Review Toolkit (ORT) | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: oss-review-toolkit/ort](https://github.com/oss-review-toolkit/ort); [oss-review-toolkit.org](https://oss-review-toolkit.org/) | [inspiration] | Pipeline of analyzer, scanner, advisor, evaluator and reporter stages for automating open-source compliance: resolves dependencies, runs licence scanners, pulls vulnerability advisories and applies policy rules. Reference architecture for a policy-driven SCA/licence pipeline and a possible back-end for the licence annex. |
| OWASP Dependency-Track | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: DependencyTrack/dependency-track](https://github.com/DependencyTrack/dependency-track); [dependencytrack.org](https://dependencytrack.org/) | [planned] [inspiration] | Server platform that ingests CycloneDX SBOMs and continuously re-evaluates them against NVD, GHSA, OSV and other sources. Optional downstream: CodeKavach exports its SBOM to a client's existing Dependency-Track. Also design inspiration for the dashboard's portfolio view. |
| Renovate and Dependabot | AGPL-3.0 (Renovate); MIT (dependabot-core) | [GitHub: renovatebot/renovate](https://github.com/renovatebot/renovate); [GitHub: dependabot/dependabot-core](https://github.com/dependabot/dependabot-core) | [inspiration] | Automated dependency-update bots; Dependabot also surfaces GHSA-based alerts natively in GitHub. Out of scope to embed (CodeKavach does no auto-fix) but the report should state whether the repo has one enabled, and the GitHub App should avoid duplicating Dependabot alerts. Renovate's AGPL licence is a reason not to bundle it. |
| Safety CLI and safety-db | MIT (CLI, per pyproject.toml and PyPI; licence texts live in a LICENSES/ directory, there is no root LICENSE file). safety-db data: non-commercial only - the README says CC BY-NC-SA 4.0, but the LICENSE.txt in the repo is the CC BY-NC 4.0 text (no ShareAlike); either way commercial use needs a paid licence. | [GitHub: pyupio/safety](https://github.com/pyupio/safety); [GitHub: pyupio/safety-db](https://github.com/pyupio/safety-db) | [inspiration] | Python dependency scanner; the free database is delayed and licensed for non-commercial use only. Licence-trap example: do not bundle safety-db in an MIT tool used in paid audits. Prefer pip-audit with the PyPA/OSV data. |
| sbomqs | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: interlynk-io/sbomqs](https://github.com/interlynk-io/sbomqs) | [planned] | CLI that scores the quality of SPDX/CycloneDX SBOMs and validates them against compliance profiles (the README names BSI, NTIA minimum elements and FSCT). Quality gate for the SBOM CodeKavach emits (and for SBOMs supplied by the client) before vulnerability matching. |

**SBOM and VEX formats and guidance**

| Tool | Licence | Link | Role tag | Notes incl. licence caveats |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CycloneDX (ECMA-424) | Apache-2.0 (specification repo) | [cyclonedx.org](https://cyclonedx.org/specification/overview/); [ecma-international.org](https://ecma-international.org/publications-and-standards/standards/ecma-424/) | [integrated] | Security-oriented BOM standard covering SBOM, SaaSBOM, CBOM, ML-BOM, VDR and VEX, with JSON/XML/protobuf schemas. Default SBOM format for the compliance annex and the interchange format between cdxgen/Syft, dep-scan and Dependency-Track; CycloneDX VEX carries CodeKavach's reachability verdicts. |
| Package-URL (purl) specification - ECMA-427 | MIT (specification repo) | [GitHub: package-url/purl-spec](https://github.com/package-url/purl-spec); [ecma-international.org](https://ecma-international.org/publications-and-standards/standards/ecma-427/) | [planned] | Canonical scheme (pkg:type/namespace/name@version?qualifiers#subpath) for identifying a software package across ecosystems. Normalise every dependency finding to a purl so results from OSV-Scanner, Trivy, Grype and dep-scan can be de-duplicated and correlated in the unified finding schema. |
| NTIA Minimum Elements for an SBOM and CISA 2025 Minimum Elements | US Government work | [ntia.gov](https://www.ntia.gov/report/2021/minimum-elements-software-bill-materials-sbom); [cisa.gov](https://www.cisa.gov/resources-tools/resources/2025-minimum-elements-software-bill-materials-sbom) | [inspiration] | Baseline data fields, automation support and practices an SBOM must satisfy; CISA's 2025 document updates the 2021 elements. Checklist that CodeKavach validates its generated SBOM against and cites in the compliance annex. |
| SPDX (ISO/IEC 5962:2021; SPDX 3.0) | Community Specification License 1.0 | [spdx.dev](https://spdx.dev/use/specifications/); [iso.org](https://www.iso.org/standard/81870.html) | [planned] | Licence- and provenance-oriented SBOM standard; v2.2.1 is an ISO standard and v3.0 adds security, AI and dataset profiles. Second export format, because regulators and procurement often ask for SPDX specifically; SPDX licence identifiers are used throughout the licence-compliance part of the report. |
| OpenVEX and CSAF 2.0 VEX | CC0-1.0 (OpenVEX spec); OASIS IPR policy (CSAF) | [GitHub: openvex/spec](https://github.com/openvex/spec); [docs.oasis-open.org](https://docs.oasis-open.org/csaf/csaf/v2.0/os/csaf-v2.0-os.html) | [planned] | Formats for asserting whether a product is affected by a vulnerability (not_affected, affected, fixed, under_investigation) with machine-readable justifications such as vulnerable_code_not_in_execute_path. Output format for SCA triage: when the local reachability engine plus LLM conclude a CVE is not reachable, CodeKavach emits a VEX statement rather than silently dropping the finding. |
| BSI TR-03183-2: Cyber Resilience Requirements - Part 2: Software Bill of Materials (SBOM) | Published free of charge by BSI | [bsi.bund.de](https://www.bsi.bund.de/EN/Themen/Unternehmen-und-Organisationen/Standards-und-Zertifizierung/Technische-Richtlinien/TR-nach-Thema-sortiert/tr03183/TR-03183_node.html) | [inspiration] | Part 2 of the German BSI guideline that interprets the EU Cyber Resilience Act for manufacturers: it sets the formal and technical requirements an SBOM must meet (current version 2.1.0). Second SBOM conformance profile for the compliance annex (alongside NTIA/CISA and CERT-In) for clients with EU exposure; validate generated CycloneDX against it via sbomqs. |
| OWASP Software Component Verification Standard (SCVS) | CC-BY-SA-4.0 | [owasp.org](https://owasp.org/projects/software-component-verification-standard); [GitHub: OWASP/Software-Component-Verification-Standard](https://github.com/OWASP/Software-Component-Verification-Standard) | [inspiration] | Control catalogue for software supply-chain assurance (inventory, SBOM, build environment, package management, component analysis, pedigree and provenance) at three verification levels. Control IDs for the supply-chain section of the compliance annex, next to ASVS for application controls. |

### 3.6 Infrastructure as code, container and CI/CD pipeline scanning

| Tool | Licence | Link | Role tag | Notes incl. licence caveats |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Checkov | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: bridgecrewio/checkov](https://github.com/bridgecrewio/checkov); [checkov.io](https://www.checkov.io/) | [integrated] | Python policy-as-code scanner for Terraform, CloudFormation, Kubernetes, Helm, ARM/Bicep, Dockerfile, GitHub Actions and more, with graph-based cross-resource checks. Default IaC engine: Python-native, Apache-2.0 and its checks carry CIS/PCI/SOC2 mappings useful for the compliance annex. Run without a Prisma Cloud API key so nothing is uploaded. |
| tfsec (merged into Trivy) and Trivy misconfiguration scanning | MIT (tfsec); Apache-2.0 (Trivy) | [GitHub: aquasecurity/tfsec](https://github.com/aquasecurity/tfsec); [trivy.dev](https://trivy.dev/docs/latest/scanner/misconfiguration/) | [planned] | Terraform static analyser whose checks now live in Trivy's misconfiguration scanner; the tfsec repo description states that tfsec is now part of Trivy. Use 'trivy config' rather than tfsec; keep tfsec rule IDs as aliases because clients still reference them. |
| Hadolint | GPL-3.0 | [GitHub: hadolint/hadolint](https://github.com/hadolint/hadolint) | [planned] | Haskell Dockerfile linter that parses the Dockerfile AST and runs ShellCheck on RUN instructions. Dockerfile best-practice findings (unpinned base images, root user, curl\|bash). GPL-3.0: invoke as a separate binary only and do not bundle it into MIT-licensed distributions without a licence notice. |
| KICS | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: Checkmarx/kics](https://github.com/Checkmarx/kics); [kics.io](https://kics.io/) | [planned] | Go IaC scanner with a large library of Rego queries across Terraform, Kubernetes, Docker, Ansible, CloudFormation, OpenAPI and others. Second IaC engine for coverage breadth (Ansible, OpenAPI); queries are readable Rego, useful as a rule-authoring reference. |
| Kubescape | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: kubescape/kubescape](https://github.com/kubescape/kubescape); [kubescape.io](https://kubescape.io/) | [planned] | Kubernetes security scanner for manifests, Helm charts and live clusters with NSA-CISA, MITRE ATT&CK and CIS framework scoring. Framework-mapped Kubernetes findings for the compliance annex; repo-scanning mode only (CodeKavach does not touch live clusters). |
| zizmor and poutine (static analysis of CI/CD pipelines) | MIT (zizmor); Apache-2.0 (poutine) | [GitHub: zizmorcore/zizmor](https://github.com/zizmorcore/zizmor); [GitHub: boostsecurityio/poutine](https://github.com/boostsecurityio/poutine) | [planned] | zizmor statically audits GitHub Actions workflows (also Dependabot and pre-commit configs) for template injection, credential persistence and leakage. Treat .github/workflows and .gitlab-ci.yml as auditable "infrastructure code": run zizmor (MIT, single binary, offline-capable) in the IaC stage and on CodeKavach's own Action. |
| cfn-lint | MIT-0 | [GitHub: aws-cloudformation/cfn-lint](https://github.com/aws-cloudformation/cfn-lint) | [planned] | CloudFormation template validator with a rule engine and SARIF output. CloudFormation coverage alongside Checkov; licence-compatible and Python-native. |
| CIS Docker Benchmark (and CIS Kubernetes Benchmark) | PDF benchmarks are under CC BY-NC-SA 4.0 per the CIS Terms of Use for Non-Member Products; commercial use requires CIS approval / SecureSuite membership | [cisecurity.org](https://www.cisecurity.org/benchmark/docker) | [inspiration] | Consensus hardening baselines that most container and Kubernetes scanners map their checks to. Control IDs for the compliance annex. Cite control numbers; do not reproduce benchmark text inside the MIT repo. |
| Clair | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: quay/clair](https://github.com/quay/clair); [quay.github.io](https://quay.github.io/clair/) | [planned] | Server-based static vulnerability analysis of container image layers with pluggable distro/language matchers. Alternative container-image matcher to Trivy/Grype when the client already runs Quay; low priority for source-code audits. |
| Conftest / Open Policy Agent | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: open-policy-agent/conftest](https://github.com/open-policy-agent/conftest) | [planned] | Runs user-written Rego policies against structured configuration (YAML, JSON, HCL, Dockerfile, etc.). Extension point for client-specific policies (for example a bank's internal cloud baseline) without changing CodeKavach code. |
| Dockle | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: goodwithtech/dockle](https://github.com/goodwithtech/dockle) | [planned] | Container image linter checking CIS Docker Benchmark items and Dockerfile best practices on built images. Optional image-level check when the client supplies built images alongside source. |
| KubeLinter | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: stackrox/kube-linter](https://github.com/stackrox/kube-linter); [docs.kubelinter.io](https://docs.kubelinter.io/) | [planned] | Static checks for Kubernetes YAML and Helm charts focused on security and production readiness. Lightweight Kubernetes manifest engine for CI mode. |
| Terrascan (archived) | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: tenable/terrascan](https://github.com/tenable/terrascan) | [inspiration] | OPA/Rego-based IaC scanner for Terraform, Kubernetes, Helm and Dockerfiles; the repository was archived on 20 November 2025. Do not integrate; listed so that the survey records why it was excluded. |

### 3.7 Vulnerability and exploit intelligence sources

**Data feeds, APIs and databases**

| Tool | Licence | Link | Role tag | Notes incl. licence caveats |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CISA Known Exploited Vulnerabilities (KEV) catalog | CC0-1.0 | [cisa.gov](https://www.cisa.gov/known-exploited-vulnerabilities-catalog); [related page](https://www.cisa.gov/sites/default/files/feeds/known_exploited_vulnerabilities.json) | [integrated] | Authoritative list of CVEs with confirmed in-the-wild exploitation, with remediation due dates and a ransomware-use flag; published as JSON and CSV. Hard escalator in risk rating: any dependency CVE on KEV is reported as at least High regardless of CVSS, and highlighted in the executive summary. |
| OSV.dev and the OSV schema | Apache-2.0 (code and schema); data under each upstream source's licence (e.g. GHSA CC-BY-4.0, PyPA CC-BY-4.0, RustSec public domain) | [osv.dev](https://osv.dev/); [ossf.github.io](https://ossf.github.io/osv-schema/) | [integrated] | Aggregated open vulnerability database keyed by package ecosystem and precise version/commit ranges, with a free API, bulk data dumps and a common JSON schema adopted by GHSA, PyPA. Primary vulnerability source. Bulk dumps are mirrored into the Docker image so air-gapped deployments work and no dependency list leaves the client network. The OSV schema is CodeKavach's internal advisory format. |
| OWASP OpenCRE (Common Requirement Enumeration) | CC0-1.0 (as shown on GitHub repo page) | [opencre.org](https://www.opencre.org/); [GitHub: OWASP/OpenCRE](https://github.com/OWASP/OpenCRE) | [integrated] | A linking layer that connects equivalent requirements across ASVS, WSTG, Cheat Sheets, Proactive Controls, CWE, CAPEC, NIST 800-53, NIST SSDF, ISO 27001, PCI DSS and others. Integrate: instead of hand-maintaining a CWE -> {ASVS, NIST, ISO, PCI} crosswalk, snapshot OpenCRE data at build time and ship it as the offline mapping table that drives the compliance annex (required for air-gapped deployments). Indian regulator clauses (RBI/SEBI/CERT-In) are not in OpenCRE and need a local overlay. |
| CVE Program cvelistV5 | CVE Terms of Use (free use with attribution) | [GitHub: CVEProject/cvelistV5](https://github.com/CVEProject/cvelistV5); [cve.org](https://www.cve.org/Legal/TermsOfUse) | [integrated] | Official bulk repository of CVE Records in CVE JSON 5 format, updated every few minutes, including CNA and ADP containers. Authoritative ID/description source and the carrier of CNA-assigned CVSS/CWE, which matters more after the NVD enrichment cut-back. Git-cloneable for air-gapped mirrors. |
| EPSS - Exploit Prediction Scoring System (API and data) | Free to use; published openly by FIRST | [first.org](https://www.first.org/epss/); [api.first.org](https://api.first.org/epss/) | [integrated] | Daily probability (0-1) and percentile that a CVE will be exploited in the next 30 days, available via JSON API, daily CSV and a GitHub mirror. Second axis of SCA risk rating next to CVSS (likelihood vs. severity); daily CSV can be mirrored for offline use. Only CVE IDs are sent if the API is used. |
| GitHub Advisory Database | CC-BY-4.0 | [GitHub: github/advisory-database](https://github.com/github/advisory-database); [GitHub: advisories](https://github.com/advisories) | [integrated] | Curated, package-centric advisories (GHSA IDs) in OSV format for npm, PyPI, Maven, NuGet, Go, RubyGems, Rust, Composer and others, including community-contributed and malware advisories. Offline-mirrorable ecosystem advisories with good affected-range data; consumed via OSV. Attribution is required in reports. |
| NVD CVE API 2.0 (and the April 2026 enrichment change) | US Government public-domain data; API terms require the 'uses the NVD API but is not endorsed or certified by the NVD' notice | [nvd.nist.gov](https://nvd.nist.gov/developers/vulnerabilities); [nist.gov](https://www.nist.gov/news-events/news/2026/04/nist-updates-nvd-operations-address-record-cve-growth) | [integrated] | REST API for CVE records with CVSS, CWE and CPE enrichment; rate-limited, with API keys for higher throughput. Source of CVSS vectors and CWE mappings for risk rating - but CodeKavach must fall back to CNA-provided scores in cvelistV5, CISA Vulnrichment and GHSA when NVD enrichment is absent, and must say which source each score came from. |
| CISA Vulnrichment | CC0-1.0 | [GitHub: cisagov/vulnrichment](https://github.com/cisagov/vulnrichment) | [planned] | CISA's ADP enrichment of CVE records with SSVC decision points, and CVSS/CWE/CPE where the CNA supplied none. Fallback enrichment source for CVSS/CWE and a ready-made SSVC 'exploitation/automatable/technical impact' signal. |
| CWE REST API | CWE Terms of Use | [GitHub: CWE-CAPEC/REST-API-wg](https://github.com/CWE-CAPEC/REST-API-wg); [cwe-api.mitre.org](https://cwe-api.mitre.org/api/v1/cwe/version) | [planned] | Official REST API for retrieving CWE weaknesses, categories, views and relationships as JSON. Build-time source for CodeKavach's bundled CWE catalogue (names, parents, Top 25 views), so that CWE data in reports can be refreshed without scraping. |
| Exploit-DB and SearchSploit | GPL-2.0 (repository and SearchSploit) | [exploit-db.com](https://www.exploit-db.com/); [GitLab: exploit-database/exploitdb](https://gitlab.com/exploit-database/exploitdb) | [planned] | Archive of public exploits and PoCs with CVE cross-references, plus an offline search CLI over a git-cloneable copy. 'Public exploit exists' flag in risk rating, by looking up CVE IDs in the local CSV index. Only metadata is used; exploit code is never executed. Keep it as an external data directory because of GPL-2.0. |
| VulnerableCode | Apache-2.0 (code); CC-BY-SA-4.0 (data) | [GitHub: aboutcode-org/vulnerablecode](https://github.com/aboutcode-org/vulnerablecode); [public.vulnerablecode.io](https://public.vulnerablecode.io/) | [planned] | Open database and aggregation pipeline for package vulnerabilities keyed by Package URL (purl), with a public instance and API. purl-first data model matches CycloneDX SBOM components; useful as a self-hostable aggregator. The share-alike data licence requires care if data is redistributed inside the image. |
| ENISA European Vulnerability Database (EUVD) | EU public service | [euvd.enisa.europa.eu](https://euvd.enisa.europa.eu/); [related page](https://euvd.enisa.europa.eu/apidoc) | [planned] | EU vulnerability database established under NIS2 and live since May 2025. Additional enrichment source and a compliance talking point for clients with EU operations. |
| cve-search | AGPL-3.0 | [GitHub: cve-search/cve-search](https://github.com/cve-search/cve-search); [cve-search.org](https://www.cve-search.org/) | [inspiration] | Self-hosted tool that imports CVE, CPE, CWE and CAPEC data into a local MongoDB with a web UI and API. Shows the local-mirror pattern CodeKavach needs for air-gapped deployments; AGPL means run it as a separate service if used at all. |
| deps.dev (Open Source Insights) API | Free public API; deps.dev-generated data (resolved dependencies, advisory statistics, associations) is CC-BY 4.0; upstream registry/advisory data keeps its own licence | [deps.dev](https://deps.dev/); [docs.deps.dev](https://docs.deps.dev/api/v3/) | [planned] | Resolved dependency graphs, licences, advisories and OpenSSF Scorecard data for npm, PyPI, Maven, Go, Cargo and NuGet packages. Online-mode enrichment for transitive graphs and package-health signals; sends package coordinates only. Disabled in air-gapped mode. |
| GitLab Advisory Database (gemnasium-db) and the open-source mirror advisories-community | MIT (advisories-community, 30-day delayed); GitLab Advisory Database Terms (gemnasium-db, restrictive) | [GitLab: gitlab-org/advisories-community](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/advisories-community); [GitLab: gitlab-org/security-products](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/security-products/gemnasium-db) | [inspiration] | Package-level advisory database behind GitLab Dependency Scanning. Licence-trap note: only the advisories-community mirror may be bundled; expect a 30-day lag versus GHSA/OSV. |
| Metasploit Framework module database | BSD-3-Clause | [GitHub: rapid7/metasploit-framework](https://github.com/rapid7/metasploit-framework); [docs.metasploit.com](https://docs.metasploit.com/) | [planned] | Exploitation framework whose module metadata lists CVE references, reliability rank and disclosure dates. Weaponised-exploit signal for risk rating (a Metasploit module implies low attacker skill); metadata lookup only. |
| Nuclei templates | MIT | [GitHub: projectdiscovery/nuclei-templates](https://github.com/projectdiscovery/nuclei-templates); [GitHub: projectdiscovery/nuclei](https://github.com/projectdiscovery/nuclei) | [planned] | Community YAML templates for detecting CVEs and misconfigurations over the network, many tied to CVE IDs. A template's existence is a strong 'remotely checkable/exploitable' signal for a dependency CVE; also suggested as DAST follow-up in the report. |
| PoC-in-GitHub and trickest/cve | CC0-1.0 (PoC-in-GitHub index); MIT (trickest/cve) | [GitHub: nomi-sec/PoC-in-GitHub](https://github.com/nomi-sec/PoC-in-GitHub); [GitHub: trickest/cve](https://github.com/trickest/cve) | [planned] | Automatically collected indexes that map CVE IDs to GitHub repositories claiming to contain proof-of-concept code. Cheap 'PoC available' indicator. |
| PyPA Advisory Database | CC-BY-4.0 | [GitHub: pypa/advisory-database](https://github.com/pypa/advisory-database) | [planned] | Community-maintained OSV-format advisories (PYSEC IDs) for packages on PyPI; the primary data source of pip-audit and an OSV.dev upstream. Offline Python advisory feed with a licence compatible with commercial audit use (attribution only) - the recommended replacement for safety-db. |
| Snyk Vulnerability Database | Proprietary; data feeds are licensed separately as commercial products | [security.snyk.io](https://security.snyk.io/); [docs.snyk.io](https://docs.snyk.io/scan-fix-and-prevent/scan-with-snyk/snyk-open-source/manage-vulnerabilities/snyk-vulnerability-database) | [inspiration] | Analyst-curated vulnerability database covering open-source packages, often with advisories ahead of NVD. Comparison and manual lookup only. No open data licence was found, so it must not be scraped or bundled. |
| VulnCheck KEV | Proprietary; free community tier | [vulncheck.com](https://www.vulncheck.com/kev); [docs.vulncheck.com](https://docs.vulncheck.com/community/vulncheck-kev) | [planned] | Commercially curated known-exploited list that the vendor says tracks well over twice as many exploited CVEs as CISA KEV and lists them earlier, with exploit references. Optional online enrichment for hosted deployments. Not redistributable, so it cannot be baked into the air-gapped image; coverage claims are the vendor's. |
| Vulnerability-Lookup and GCVE | AGPL-3.0 | [GitHub: vulnerability-lookup/vulnerability-lookup](https://github.com/vulnerability-lookup/vulnerability-lookup); [gcve.eu](https://gcve.eu/) | [inspiration] | Successor to cve-search that correlates many sources (CVE, GHSA, PySec, CSAF feeds, national databases) with sightings and comments; GCVE is CIRCL's decentralised vulnerability-ID allocation initiative. Resilience reference: the vulnerability-ID ecosystem is fragmenting (CVE funding scare, NVD cut-back, GCVE, EUVD), so CodeKavach's advisory model must accept multiple ID schemes and aliases. |
| Vulners | Proprietary; API key required; free plan limited to 100 monthly API credits (paid plans from EUR 475/month as read on 2026-09-21) | [vulners.com](https://vulners.com/); [docs.vulners.com](https://docs.vulners.com/) | [planned] | Commercial aggregator correlating CVEs with exploits, advisories and its own AI risk score. Optional enrichment connector for hosted mode; not part of the open-source default. |

### 3.8 Reporting and document generation

**Document generation libraries**

| Tool | Licence | Link | Role tag | Notes incl. licence caveats |

| docxtpl (python-docx-template) | LGPL-2.1-only | [GitHub: elapouya/python-docx-template](https://github.com/elapouya/python-docx-template); [docxtpl.readthedocs.io](https://docxtpl.readthedocs.io/) | [integrated] | Renders a Word document that contains Jinja2 tags, so designers lay out the report in Word and code only supplies the context (loops over findings, rich text, inline images, sub-documents). Main DOCX path: ship a default CodeKavach .docx template and let each bank or consultancy drop in its own branded template without touching code, as Ghostwriter does. |
| Jinja2 | BSD-3-Clause | [GitHub: pallets/jinja](https://github.com/pallets/jinja) | [integrated] | The de-facto Python templating engine, used by docxtpl, by most HTML report generators and in FastAPI stacks. Single templating layer for HTML, Markdown, Typst and issue-body output. Enable autoescape for HTML and use SandboxedEnvironment for client-supplied templates because code snippets, comments and LLM output are attacker-influenced and could otherwise cause XSS or SSTI inside the report or dashboard. |
| Pygments | BSD-2-Clause | [pygments.org](https://pygments.org/); [GitHub: pygments/pygments](https://github.com/pygments/pygments) | [integrated] | Syntax highlighter for 500+ languages with HTML, LaTeX, RTF, SVG and image formatters plus line-number and highlighted-line options. Render text code-snippet evidence with line numbers and highlight the vulnerable lines and the taint path in HTML/PDF; for DOCX, walk the token stream and emit coloured python-docx runs. Evidence remains text, not screenshots, so it is searchable and diffable. |
| python-docx | MIT | [GitHub: python-openxml/python-docx](https://github.com/python-openxml/python-docx); [python-docx.readthedocs.io](https://python-docx.readthedocs.io/) | [integrated] | Pure-Python library for creating and editing Word .docx files: paragraphs, styles, tables, images, headers/footers, sections. Programmatic DOCX building blocks (finding tables, severity-coloured cells, code-snippet paragraphs in a monospace style) and the engine underneath docxtpl. |
| WeasyPrint | BSD-3-Clause | [GitHub: Kozea/WeasyPrint](https://github.com/Kozea/WeasyPrint); [doc.courtbouillon.org](https://doc.courtbouillon.org/weasyprint/stable/) | [integrated] | Python HTML/CSS to PDF engine with strong CSS Paged Media support (running headers/footers, page counters, table of contents with page numbers, bookmarks). Default PDF renderer: one Jinja2 HTML template yields both the HTML report and, via print CSS, the PDF. Deterministic and works air-gapped; needs Pango system libraries in the Docker image. No JavaScript, so charts must be pre-rendered SVG. |

Matplotlib (Agg/SVG backends) for charts embedded in PDF/DOCX: severity distribution, CWE distribution, risk heat map, trend across scans. Plotly for the interactive HTML report and dashboard. |
| Pandoc (with pypandoc) | GPL-2.0-or-later (pypandoc wrapper: MIT) | [pandoc.org](https://pandoc.org/); [GitHub: jgm/pandoc](https://github.com/jgm/pandoc) | [planned] | Universal document converter: Markdown to DOCX (with a reference.docx for styling), HTML, LaTeX/PDF, and many more. Convenient Markdown-first path for secondary formats. Licence caveat: Pandoc is GPL-2.0-or-later, so call it as an external executable and never link or bundle it into the MIT wheel; pypandoc (MIT) is only a thin wrapper. |
| Playwright for Python (page.pdf) | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: microsoft/playwright-python](https://github.com/microsoft/playwright-python); [playwright.dev](https://playwright.dev/python/docs/api/class-page#page-pdf) | [planned] | Browser automation library whose Chromium driver can print any page to PDF with headers, footers, margins and tagged output. Optional high-fidelity PDF backend when the HTML report uses JavaScript charts (the approach SysReptor uses). |
| pypdf | BSD-3-Clause | [GitHub: py-pdf/pypdf](https://github.com/py-pdf/pypdf); [pypdf.readthedocs.io](https://pypdf.readthedocs.io/en/stable/user/encryption-decryption.html) | [planned] | Pure-Python PDF manipulation library: merge, split, stamp, set document metadata, add attachments and encrypt or decrypt files with RC4 or AES (up to the PDF 2.0 AES-256 scheme) when the 'cryptography' extra is installed. Post-processing step after WeasyPrint/Typst: write report metadata (engagement ID, classification, commit hash), attach the SARIF/JSON evidence bundle. |
| ReportLab (open-source toolkit) | BSD-3-Clause (open-source toolkit) | [PyPI](https://pypi.org/project/reportlab/); [reportlab.com](https://www.reportlab.com/opensource/) | [planned] | Mature programmatic PDF generation library (Platypus flowables, tables, vector charts) with no external system dependencies. Fallback PDF backend for minimal/air-gapped installs where Pango or a browser is unavailable, and for drawing severity charts straight into PDF. |
| Typst (and typst-py bindings) | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: typst/typst](https://github.com/typst/typst); [GitHub: messense/typst-py](https://github.com/messense/typst-py) | [planned] | Modern markup-based typesetting system written in Rust: LaTeX-quality PDF output, scripting, fast incremental compile, single static binary. Candidate for the premium print-quality PDF: generate a .typ file from the findings JSON and compile locally with no TeX installation. |
| XlsxWriter and openpyxl | XlsxWriter BSD-2-Clause; openpyxl MIT | [GitHub: jmcnamara/XlsxWriter](https://github.com/jmcnamara/XlsxWriter); [foss.heptapod.net](https://foss.heptapod.net/openpyxl/openpyxl) | [planned] | XlsxWriter writes richly formatted .xlsx workbooks (conditional formatting, charts, data validation, autofilter); openpyxl reads and modifies existing workbooks. Findings register / tracker workbook that audit and GRC teams expect beside the PDF: one row per finding with status, owner, due date, severity-coloured cells and drop-down validation. |
| Gotenberg | MIT | [GitHub: gotenberg/gotenberg](https://github.com/gotenberg/gotenberg); [gotenberg.dev](https://gotenberg.dev/) | [planned] | Stateless Docker service exposing an HTTP API that turns HTML, Markdown, URLs and Office documents into PDF using headless Chromium and LibreOffice, plus merge and PDF/A conversion routes. Drop-in rendering side-car for the self-hosted and air-gapped Docker deployments: keeps Chromium/LibreOffice out of the Python image, converts the generated DOCX to a matching PDF, and works offline. |

Optional 'board read-out' deliverable. |

Core interchange format: every bundled OSS engine is normalised into SARIF internally, and SARIF is a first-class export. codeFlows carry the taint path that L3 slicing is derived from; `taxonomies` carries CWE/OWASP mappings. |

Run Multitool 'validate' in CI as the conformance oracle for CodeKavach's SARIF; make sure output renders in the Microsoft VS Code viewer; consider the MIT web component for the dashboard's result browser. |
| sarif-tools and sarif-om (Microsoft) | MIT | [GitHub: microsoft/sarif-tools](https://github.com/microsoft/sarif-tools); [GitHub: microsoft/sarif-python-om](https://github.com/microsoft/sarif-python-om) | [planned] | sarif-tools is a Python CLI/library to summarise, diff, filter and convert SARIF to CSV, HTML, Word and more. Use sarif-tools for SARIF diff (new vs. fixed findings between scans) and quick conversions in tests. sarif-om has had no release since 2019, so prefer own pydantic models validated against the official JSON schema. |
| reviewdog | MIT | [GitHub: reviewdog/reviewdog](https://github.com/reviewdog/reviewdog) | [inspiration] | Go tool that takes any linter output (including SARIF / its own rdjson) and posts it as PR review comments. Design inspiration / optional integration: a ready-made multi-SCM reporter so CodeKavach does not have to implement every PR-comment API on day one. |

**Evidence integrity, signing and reproducibility**

| Tool | Licence | Link | Role tag | Notes incl. licence caveats |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| pyHanko | MIT | [GitHub: MatthiasValvekens/pyHanko](https://github.com/MatthiasValvekens/pyHanko); [docs.pyhanko.eu](https://docs.pyhanko.eu/) | [integrated] | Python library and CLI for digitally signing and validating PDFs (PAdES baseline profiles, RFC 3161 timestamps, long-term validation, PKCS#11 tokens, signature fields and visible stamps). Sign the final PDF with the auditor's or organisation's certificate and an RFC 3161 timestamp to meet 'signed report' expectations (CERT-In's three sign-offs map to multiple signature fields) and make later tampering evident. |
| in-toto attestation framework (and SLSA provenance) | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: in-toto/attestation](https://github.com/in-toto/attestation); [slsa.dev](https://slsa.dev/) | [planned] | Specification for signed, typed statements about software artefacts (subject digests + predicate). Emit an attestation per run: subject = repository commit and report hashes; predicate = tool version, rule-pack hashes, engine versions, privacy level, model identifier, prompt-template hashes. |
| Sigstore (cosign, sigstore-python, Rekor) | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: sigstore/sigstore-python](https://github.com/sigstore/sigstore-python); [GitHub: sigstore/cosign](https://github.com/sigstore/cosign) | [planned] | Keyless signing ecosystem: short-lived certificates bound to an OIDC identity, signatures recorded in the Rekor transparency log. Sign the machine-readable bundle (SARIF, JSON, evidence manifest) and CodeKavach's own release artefacts. |
| ISO/IEC 27037:2012 - digital evidence identification, collection, acquisition and preservation | Proprietary (paid) | [iso.org](https://www.iso.org/standard/44381.html) | [inspiration] | International guideline for handling potential digital evidence with principles of auditability, repeatability. Vocabulary and principles for CodeKavach's evidence manifest: SHA-256 of every analysed file and the commit ID, tool and rule versions, UTC timestamps, operator identity, and an append-only hash-chained log of what was sent to which LLM endpoint at which privacy level. |
| RFC 3161 - Time-Stamp Protocol (TSP) | not stated | [rfc-editor.org](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3161) | [planned] | IETF protocol by which a trusted time-stamp authority signs a hash, proving that the data existed at a given time. Timestamp the evidence manifest and signed PDF so the client can prove when the assessment result existed; only a hash leaves the network, which suits the privacy model. |
| msoffcrypto-tool | MIT | [GitHub: nolze/msoffcrypto-tool](https://github.com/nolze/msoffcrypto-tool); [PyPI](https://pypi.org/project/msoffcrypto-tool/) | [planned] | Python tool and library that decrypts password-protected Microsoft Office files and can also encrypt OOXML documents (docx, xlsx, pptx) with a password; the encryption path is labelled experimental by the author. Password-protect the DOCX and XLSX deliverables so every format - not only the PDF - meets the CERT-In distribution rule. |
| Reproducible Builds - SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH | not stated | [reproducible-builds.org](https://reproducible-builds.org/docs/source-date-epoch/) | [inspiration] | Specification of the SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH environment variable that lets tools replace 'now' with a fixed timestamp so outputs are bit-for-bit reproducible. Make report generation deterministic: same inputs + same cached LLM responses = identical SARIF/JSON and stable PDF metadata. |

| VulnRepo | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: kac89/vulnrepo](https://github.com/kac89/vulnrepo) | [inspiration] | Browser-based report builder that keeps reports client-side with AES encryption, offers issue templates. ### 3.9 LLM abstraction, serving, structured output, guardrails and evaluation

**Provider-agnostic abstraction layers and gateways**

| Tool | Licence | Link | Role tag | Notes incl. licence caveats |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| LiteLLM (Python SDK + Proxy/AI Gateway) | MIT for everything outside the enterprise/ directory; enterprise/ is under a separate commercial licence (read from repo LICENSE) | [GitHub: BerriAI/litellm](https://github.com/BerriAI/litellm); [docs.litellm.ai](https://docs.litellm.ai/docs/) | [integrated] | Python library and self-hostable proxy that exposes 100+ LLM providers (Anthropic, OpenAI, Gemini, Bedrock, Azure, xAI, Ollama, vLLM, OpenRouter, etc.) behind one OpenAI-style completion() call, with cost tracking, fallbacks, budgets, rate limits and caching. Integrate as the default provider adapter behind CodeKavach's own thin `LLMClient` interface, so every privacy level can target any cloud or local model with one code path; use the proxy mode for the server/GitHub-App deployment to centralise keys, budgets and per-client spend caps. Do not import anything from enterprise/. |

Threat model input: the LLM abstraction layer holds every provider API key, so it is the highest-value dependency in CodeKavach. |

Design note for the `LLMClient` interface: Chat Completions is still the lowest common denominator across every backend CodeKavach targets. |
| any-llm (Mozilla AI) | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: mozilla-ai/any-llm](https://github.com/mozilla-ai/any-llm) | [planned] [baseline] | A smaller single-interface Python library that routes to providers through their official SDKs rather than re-implementing each API, with an optional gateway component. Alternative/baseline to LiteLLM behind the same internal `LLMClient` interface; smaller dependency surface is attractive for the air-gapped build. |
| OpenRouter API and provider logging policies | Proprietary hosted service | [openrouter.ai](https://openrouter.ai/docs/quickstart); [related page](https://openrouter.ai/docs/guides/privacy/provider-logging) | [planned] | Hosted OpenAI-compatible aggregator that resells hundreds of closed and open models from many upstream inference hosts. Support as an optional backend but flag it in the privacy report: an aggregator adds a second data processor and the effective retention policy is that of whichever upstream host serves the request. |
| Portkey AI Gateway (open-source) | MIT (gateway repo); hosted control plane and some guardrail features are commercial | [GitHub: Portkey-AI/gateway](https://github.com/Portkey-AI/gateway); [portkey.ai](https://portkey.ai/docs/introduction/what-is-portkey) | [planned] | TypeScript gateway that fronts many LLM providers with retries, fallbacks, load-balancing, caching and pluggable guardrail hooks. Option for clients who already run an LLM gateway: CodeKavach should accept any OpenAI-compatible base_url so that Portkey/LiteLLM-proxy/Bifrost (github.com/maximhq/bifrost, Apache-2.0) can sit between it and the provider. |
| Vercel AI SDK (TypeScript) | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: vercel/ai](https://github.com/vercel/ai); [ai-sdk.dev](https://ai-sdk.dev/docs/introduction) | [planned] | TypeScript toolkit with one interface over many model providers (including any OpenAI-compatible endpoint). Consider only for TypeScript surfaces (VS Code extension, dashboard) if they ever need to call a model directly. |

Integrate: define one `TriageVerdict` schema (verdict enum, CWE id, confidence, pseudonymised evidence line refs, rationale) and pass it natively where supported. Keep schema field names and descriptions free of client identifiers, because the schema is retained/cached separately from the prompt. |

Candidate light-weight orchestration layer that matches CodeKavach's FastAPI + Pydantic stack far better than LangChain; evaluate against a hand-rolled client. Its OTel export must be pointed at a local collector only. |

Map: author the verdict and finding schemas once in Pydantic, export JSON Schema, and keep them inside the intersection of provider-supported keywords so the same schema works across all back-ends. |

Integrate: pre-flight budget estimation per finding and per scan ('this audit will cost about \$X at L3'), slice truncation, and hard per-request/per-scan token ceilings as the denial-of-wallet control. |

Integrate exact-match only: cache key = hash(model, prompt version, privacy level, pseudonymised slice). |

Integrate for 429/5xx/timeouts around provider calls, with a global retry budget and honouring Retry-After; combine with an asyncio semaphore and token-bucket limiter per provider key. |

Design inspiration for CodeKavach's pre-send scanner chain (invisible-Unicode stripping, token-limit, secrets re-check, injection heuristics). Do not take a runtime dependency on an archived project; vendor ideas, not code paths that will rot. |

Integrate optionally: Prompt Guard 2 (86M) is small enough to run on CPU over every comment/string literal in a slice as an injection pre-filter; report its detection rate on CodeKavach's code-comment attack corpus. Weight licence (Llama) means it is a user-downloaded optional component, not bundled. |

Optional, off by default: they are a second cloud service that receives the slice, so they are only acceptable when the client already uses that cloud and the content has passed the privacy layer. |

Reference; Pydantic validation plus CodeKavach-specific checks (every cited line must exist in the slice that was sent; CWE id must be in the allowed list) cover the need with fewer dependencies. |
| Lakera Guard (now Check Point AI Security) | Proprietary SaaS / commercial self-hosted | [lakera.ai](https://www.lakera.ai/ai-agent-security); [docs.lakera.ai](https://docs.lakera.ai/docs/landing) | [baseline] | Commercial prompt-injection and data-leak detection API; its documentation site is now branded 'Check Point AI Security'. Baseline to mention only. A cloud guardrail API would itself receive the code slice, which defeats the privacy layer; any detector CodeKavach uses must run locally. |

Reference: dialogue-oriented and heavier than needed; the input-rail/output-rail decomposition is a useful vocabulary for the architecture chapter. |
| Rebuff | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: protectai/rebuff](https://github.com/protectai/rebuff) | [inspiration] | Early prompt-injection detector combining heuristics, an LLM judge, a vector store of known attacks and canary tokens. Design inspiration only: the canary-token idea transfers well - CodeKavach can plant a random canary in the system prompt and treat its appearance in the model output as evidence of instruction hijacking. |

Reference only; the hosted proxy mode is incompatible with CodeKavach's threat model (third party sees prompts), and a product in maintenance mode is a poor foundation for new work. |

### 3.10 Privacy gateways and sanitisation tooling

**Open-source gateways, redaction layers and pseudonymisation libraries**

| Tool | Licence | Link | Role tag | Notes incl. licence caveats |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Stacklok CodeGate (archived) | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: stacklok/codegate](https://github.com/stacklok/codegate) | [inspiration] [related-work] | Local proxy between AI coding assistants and LLM providers that detected secrets and PII in prompts, replaced them before the request left the machine and restored them in the response; also flagged risky dependencies. Nearest prior art for L1 with reversible substitution, and reusable Apache-2.0 code. It never touched identifiers, business logic or slicing - precisely the gap CodeKavach's L2-L4 fill. Its discontinuation is a caution about sustainability. |
| LiteLLM (gateway + Presidio PII-masking guardrail) | MIT (except the enterprise/ directory, separately licensed) | [GitHub: BerriAI/litellm](https://github.com/BerriAI/litellm); [docs.litellm.ai](https://docs.litellm.ai/docs/proxy/guardrails/pii_masking_v2) | [baseline] | Unified SDK/proxy for 100+ LLM APIs (OpenAI, Anthropic, Gemini, Bedrock, Azure, Ollama, vLLM) with a guardrail hook that masks PII via Presidio on the way out and can unmask on the way back. Integrate as the provider abstraction; CodeKavach's privacy layer sits in front of it. Do not rely on its PII guardrail for code - it is text-PII only. Keep imports out of the enterprise/ folder. |
| JohnYezub/Code-obfuscator (Claude skill, Swift) | MIT | [GitHub: JohnYezub/Code-obfuscator](https://github.com/JohnYezub/Code-obfuscator) | [baseline] [related-work] | A Claude skill that deterministically renames Swift types and functions to meaningless identifiers. The only tool found in this search that does reversible identifier renaming aimed at LLM use. |
| llm-redact-proxy and LLM-Redactor (2026 community egress proxies for coding agents) | MIT (llm-redact-proxy); LLM-Redactor has no LICENSE file on main | [GitHub: CupOfGeo/llm-redact-proxy](https://github.com/CupOfGeo/llm-redact-proxy); [GitHub: WangYihang/LLM-Redactor](https://github.com/WangYihang/LLM-Redactor) | [inspiration] | Two small local proxies that sit between coding agents (e.g. Claude Code) and the LLM API and strip credentials/PII with gitleaks-style regexes plus a local classifier, failing closed. Evidence that developers are hand-rolling L1 for coding agents in 2026; neither pseudonymises identifiers nor minimises code. Without a licence, LLM-Redactor's code cannot be reused. |
| JetBrains-Research/dataset-anonymizer | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: JetBrains-Research/dataset-anonymizer](https://github.com/JetBrains-Research/dataset-anonymizer) | [related-work] | IntelliJ-platform plugin that strips comments and replaces variable, function and other names in code snapshots so that student-submission datasets can be published. Prior art for PSI/AST-based identifier anonymisation - one-way (no mapping back), dataset-oriented and unrelated to LLM privacy. |
| Commercial LLM data-protection gateways: Limina (ex Private AI), Protecto, Skyflow, Nightfall, Lasso, Prompt Security, Harmonic | Proprietary | [getlimina.ai](https://www.getlimina.ai/en/blog/private-ai-rebrands-limina-sensitive-data-privacy); [protecto.ai](https://www.protecto.ai/) | [baseline] | Group entry. Shows the commercial state of the art stops at L1 (entities and secrets in natural-language text). |
| mysto/python-fpe (FF3-1 format-preserving encryption) | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: mysto/python-fpe](https://github.com/mysto/python-fpe) | [planned] | Pure-Python implementation of the NIST FF3/FF3-1 format-preserving encryption mode, validated against the NIST test vectors. Candidate building block for keyed reversible pseudonyms. Caveat: NIST's 2025 Rev.1 draft drops FF3-1, so prefer FF1 or HMAC+vault for anything presented to auditors. |

**Commercial data-protection gateways (for comparison)**

| Tool | Licence | Link | Role tag | Notes incl. licence caveats |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Limina (formerly Private AI) | Proprietary | [getlimina.ai](https://www.getlimina.ai/en); [private-ai.com](https://www.private-ai.com/) | [baseline] | Commercial PII detection, redaction and synthetic-replacement engine (container or API) marketed for scrubbing prompts and training data. Commercial comparator for L1 detection quality; optional enterprise integration. Note the rebrand when citing. |
| Nightfall AI | Proprietary (SaaS) | [nightfall.ai](https://www.nightfall.ai/) | [baseline] | Cloud DLP platform with detectors for secrets, credentials and PII across SaaS apps and GenAI prompts. Commercial comparator for the 'DLP for GenAI' category; illustrates that the market stops at L1. |
| Protecto | Proprietary (SaaS) | [protecto.ai](https://www.protecto.ai/) | [baseline] | Commercial data-guardrail platform offering context-preserving masking/tokenisation of sensitive data for LLM and agent pipelines. Commercial comparator only. |
| Skyflow LLM Privacy Vault | Proprietary (SaaS) | [skyflow.com](https://www.skyflow.com/product/skyflow-for-genai) | [baseline] | Commercial data-privacy vault that tokenises sensitive fields before they reach an LLM and detokenises responses. Commercial comparator for the tokenise/detokenise pattern; not embeddable. |

**Obfuscation and deobfuscation tools used in the leakage experiments**

| Tool | Licence | Link | Role tag | Notes incl. licence caveats |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| humanify | MIT | [GitHub: jehna/humanify](https://github.com/jehna/humanify) | [evaluation] | CLI that un-minifies JavaScript by asking an LLM (OpenAI, Gemini or a local model) for better identifier names and applying the renames through Babel so the AST stays equivalent. Practical, scriptable attacker for the evaluation harness: run it over pseudonymised JS/TS slices and score how many recovered names match or are semantically close to the originals. |
| javascript-obfuscator | BSD-2-Clause | [GitHub: javascript-obfuscator/javascript-obfuscator](https://github.com/javascript-obfuscator/javascript-obfuscator) | [baseline] | Widely used open-source JS/Node obfuscator offering identifier renaming, string-array encoding. Baseline 'off-the-shelf obfuscator' for the JS/TS experiments, to contrast heavy obfuscation (destroys detection accuracy) with CodeKavach's analysis-preserving pseudonymisation. |

## 4. LLM provider and open-model documentation

API references for every provider adapter, the data-retention terms a client's legal team will ask about, and the open-weight models that can run inside the trusted boundary. Retention terms change; the Notes column records what each page said on the verification date, and the privacy layer is designed so that its measured properties do not depend on these terms.

### 4.1 Provider APIs and SDKs

| Item | Publisher | Licence / terms | Link | Role tag | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Anthropic Claude API and official Python SDK | Anthropic | MIT (SDK); API use governed by Anthropic Commercial Terms | [GitHub: anthropics/anthropic-sdk-python](https://github.com/anthropics/anthropic-sdk-python); [platform.claude.com](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/api/overview) | [integrated] | Official typed Python client for the Messages API (streaming, tool use, token counting, batches, files). Documentation has moved from docs.anthropic.com to platform.claude.com. Integrate as a first-class direct adapter (not only via LiteLLM) so that Anthropic-specific features - cache_control breakpoints, count_tokens, structured outputs, Message Batches - can be used for triage at scale. |
| OpenAI API (Responses API, Batch) and openai-python SDK | OpenAI | Apache-2.0 (SDK) | [GitHub: openai/openai-python](https://github.com/openai/openai-python); [developers.openai.com](https://developers.openai.com/api/reference/resources/responses) | [integrated] | Official Python client for the Responses and Chat Completions APIs, Structured Outputs, Batch and embeddings. Integrate: one 'OpenAI-compatible' adapter with a configurable base_url covers OpenAI, Azure OpenAI and nearly every self-hosted or third-party endpoint. |
| Provider model-deprecation schedules (Anthropic model deprecations; OpenAI deprecations) | Anthropic; OpenAI | not stated | [platform.claude.com](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/model-deprecations); [developers.openai.com](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/deprecations) | [inspiration] | Both vendors retire dated model snapshots on a published schedule. Research validity + engineering: (1) the privacy-utility experiments must record exact snapshot IDs and run dates, and the paper should include at least one open-weight model so results stay reproducible after hosted snapshots retire. |
| Amazon Bedrock Converse API | Amazon Web Services | Proprietary cloud service | [docs.aws.amazon.com](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/conversation-inference.html) | [planned] | A single message/tool-use schema across Bedrock-hosted models from Anthropic, Meta, Mistral, Amazon and other providers. Integrate (boto3 or LiteLLM) - the natural choice for bank clients who already have an AWS landing zone, because model providers have no access to prompts and traffic can stay inside the client's VPC. |
| Anthropic prompt caching and Message Batches API | Anthropic | not stated | [platform.claude.com](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-caching); [related page](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/batch-processing) | [inspiration] | Prompt caching bills repeated prompt prefixes (system prompt, rubric, few-shot examples) at a fraction of the input price; the Batches API runs asynchronous jobs at a discount. Cost engineering: put the fixed triage rubric + CWE guidance + output schema in a cached prefix and the per-finding pseudonymised slice last. |
| DeepSeek API platform and privacy policy | DeepSeek (Hangzhou DeepSeek Artificial Intelligence) | Proprietary hosted service | [api-docs.deepseek.com](https://api-docs.deepseek.com/); [cdn.deepseek.com](https://cdn.deepseek.com/policies/en-US/deepseek-privacy-policy.html) | [inspiration] | Low-cost OpenAI-compatible API for DeepSeek chat/reasoner models. Threat model input and provider-matrix entry: the first-party DeepSeek API is unsuitable for regulated Indian financial clients. |
| Google Gen AI SDK (google-genai) and Gemini API | Google | Apache-2.0 (SDK) | [GitHub: googleapis/python-genai](https://github.com/googleapis/python-genai); [ai.google.dev](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs) | [planned] | Unified Python SDK for both the Gemini Developer API (AI Studio keys) and Google Cloud's enterprise endpoint (formerly Vertex AI, now documented as 'Gemini Enterprise Agent Platform'). Integrate as a direct adapter. |
| Azure OpenAI / Microsoft Foundry models | Microsoft | Proprietary cloud service | [learn.microsoft.com](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/foundry-models/concepts/models-sold-directly-by-azure); [related page](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/responsible-ai/openai/data-privacy) | [planned] | Azure-hosted OpenAI (and other 'sold by Azure') models reachable through the OpenAI SDK with an Azure endpoint. Integrate via the OpenAI-compatible adapter (AzureOpenAI client). |
| Claude Agent SDK (Python) | Anthropic | MIT (repo LICENSE); bundled Claude Code CLI is subject to Anthropic's terms | [GitHub: anthropics/claude-agent-sdk-python](https://github.com/anthropics/claude-agent-sdk-python); [code.claude.com](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/overview) | [inspiration] | SDK that exposes the Claude Code agent loop (tools, hooks, sub-agents, MCP servers, permission modes) to Python programs. Design inspiration and optional L1/L2-only 'deep dive' mode. |
| OpenAI Agents SDK (Python) | OpenAI | MIT | [GitHub: openai/openai-agents-python](https://github.com/openai/openai-agents-python); [openai.github.io](https://openai.github.io/openai-agents-python/) | [inspiration] | Lightweight agent framework (agents, hand-offs, guardrails, tracing, sessions) that works with OpenAI and, via LiteLLM, other providers. Reference only. If ever used, tracing must be disabled or redirected, otherwise prompt content is exported to OpenAI's trace store outside the privacy layer's accounting. |
| VS Code Language Model API (vscode.lm) | Microsoft | not stated | [code.visualstudio.com](https://code.visualstudio.com/api/extension-guides/ai/language-model) | [planned] | Extension API that lets a VS Code extension select a chat model the user already has (vscode.lm.selectChatModels by vendor/family) and send requests to it (model.sendRequest); Copilot-provided models require an explicit per-extension consent dialog. Option for the VS Code extension: allow 'use my editor's model' without CodeKavach handling any API key. |

Support via the generic OpenAI-compatible adapter. |
| Alibaba Cloud Model Studio (Qwen / DashScope API) | Alibaba Cloud | Proprietary hosted service | [alibabacloud.com](https://www.alibabacloud.com/help/en/model-studio/what-is-model-studio); [related page](https://www.alibabacloud.com/help/en/model-studio/privacy-notice) | [planned] | First-party hosted API for the Qwen family (incl. Qwen3-Coder) with an OpenAI-compatible mode. Support via OpenAI-compatible adapter; mainly relevant as the reference host for Qwen3-Coder quality comparisons. |
| Mistral AI and Cohere platform APIs | Mistral AI; Cohere | Proprietary hosted services | [docs.mistral.ai](https://docs.mistral.ai/); [docs.cohere.com](https://docs.cohere.com/) | [planned] | European (Mistral, incl. the hosted Codestral/Devstral code models) and Canadian (Cohere, Command family with private/VPC deployment options) API platforms; both offer JSON-mode/structured outputs and tool calling. Support via LiteLLM. Mistral is relevant for EU-residency clients; Cohere for clients wanting a vendor-managed private deployment. Low priority for the first release. |

Support via the OpenAI-compatible adapter as the 'open model without owning GPUs' tier - useful for the paper's experiments comparing Qwen/DeepSeek/GLM/gpt-oss at each privacy level without local hardware. |
