Support through the generic adapter for completeness; same data-sovereignty caveat as DeepSeek. The open weights (MIT / modified-MIT) are the recommended path for confidential code. |

### 4.2 Data-retention terms and zero-data-retention programmes

| Document | Publisher | Link | Role tag | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Anthropic: API and data retention (ZDR scope, feature eligibility, Covered Models) | Anthropic | [platform.claude.com](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/manage-claude/api-and-data-retention); [privacy.claude.com](https://privacy.claude.com/en/articles/7996866-how-long-do-you-store-my-organization-s-data) | [inspiration] | Standard commercial API retention is deletion within 30 days; no training on commercial data without permission. Policy engine input: encode this table as machine-readable provider capabilities so that 'client requires ZDR' automatically disables batch/files features and blocks non-eligible models. |
| AWS: Amazon Bedrock data protection, data retention modes and abuse detection | Amazon Web Services | [docs.aws.amazon.com](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/data-retention.html); [related page](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/data-protection.html) | [inspiration] | Model providers have no access to Bedrock's model-deployment accounts, logs, prompts or completions. Policy engine input + deployment guide: recommend an SCP pinning data_retention_mode=none for bank accounts, warn that this makes some frontier models unavailable, and warn about cross-region inference profiles for data-residency. The frontier-model-requires-retention trend is a strong motivation paragraph for the paper. |
| Google: Gemini API Additional Terms of Service (paid vs unpaid data use) | Google | [ai.google.dev](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/terms) | [inspiration] | For unpaid (free-tier/AI Studio) use, Google may use prompts and responses to improve products and train models, and human reviewers may read them. Hard guard: CodeKavach must detect/declare free-tier Gemini keys and refuse to send anything but L4 abstractions (or synthetic demo code) through them; document prominently because students will reach for the free tier first. |
| OpenAI: Data controls in the OpenAI platform (ZDR / Modified Abuse Monitoring) and the NYT preservation order | OpenAI | [developers.openai.com](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/your-data); [openai.com](https://openai.com/index/response-to-nyt-data-demands/) | [inspiration] | API data is not used for training by default (since 1 March 2023); abuse-monitoring logs are kept up to 30 days unless law requires longer. Policy engine input + caveat for the paper: contractual retention limits can be overridden by litigation holds; only data that never leaves (L4/self-hosted) or is pseudonymised (L2/L3) is robust to that. India residency is directly relevant to RBI-regulated clients. |
| Google Cloud: Gemini Enterprise Agent Platform (formerly Vertex AI) and zero data retention | Google Cloud | [docs.cloud.google.com](https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-agent-platform/resources/zero-data-retention) | [inspiration] | Google will not train on customer data without permission. Policy engine input: for the Google Cloud backend CodeKavach should never enable grounding/search tools and should document the abuse-monitoring exception process in the deployment guide. |
| Microsoft: Data, privacy and security for Foundry Models sold by Azure (Azure OpenAI) | Microsoft | [learn.microsoft.com](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/responsible-ai/openai/data-privacy) | [inspiration] | Prompts and completions are not available to OpenAI, not used to train foundation models and not shared with other customers. Policy engine input + deployment guide: use regional Standard deployments, stateless chat-completions calls, and include the ContentLogging check as an automated pre-flight assertion in CodeKavach's Azure provider. |

### 4.3 Open-weight code and reasoning models and their licences

| Item | Publisher | Licence / terms | Link | Role tag | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| gpt-oss-120b and gpt-oss-20b (OpenAI open-weight models) | OpenAI | Apache-2.0 (plus a short usage policy) | [arXiv:2508.10925](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.10925); [GitHub: openai/gpt-oss](https://github.com/openai/gpt-oss) | [planned] [evaluation] | Open-weight reasoning MoE models with configurable reasoning effort; the 20B runs in roughly 16 GB and the 120B on a single 80 GB GPU, with native function calling and structured outputs. Strong default for air-gapped deployments: permissive licence, single-GPU footprint, and an adjustable reasoning-effort knob that maps naturally onto CodeKavach's cost/accuracy settings. |
| Qwen2.5-Coder and Qwen3-Coder | Hui et al. / Qwen Team, Alibaba | Apache-2.0 for Qwen2.5-Coder 0.5B/1.5B/7B/14B/32B and for Qwen3-Coder (480B-A35B, 30B-A3B, Coder-Next); the Qwen2.5-Coder-3B checkpoint is under the non-commercial 'qwen-research' licence | [arXiv:2409.12186](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.12186); [GitHub: QwenLM/Qwen3-Coder](https://github.com/QwenLM/Qwen3-Coder) | [planned] [evaluation] | Code-specialised model family; the 32B dense and the 30B-A3B MoE are the usual 'best model that fits one GPU / one Mac' choices, and the 480B MoE is frontier-class among open code models. Default local model candidates for L1-L3 triage and the main open-weight subjects of the privacy-utility evaluation. Avoid the 3B variant in any shipped bundle because of its research-only licence. |
| GLM-4.5 / 4.6 / 4.7 / GLM-5 (Zhipu / Z.ai) | GLM Team, Zhipu AI and Tsinghua University | MIT (weights, per Hugging Face tags for GLM-4.5, 4.5-Air, 4.6, 4.7 and GLM-5); GitHub code repo Apache-2.0 | [arXiv:2508.06471](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.06471); [Hugging Face: zai-org/GLM-4.6](https://huggingface.co/zai-org/GLM-4.6) | [planned] | MoE 'agentic, reasoning and coding' model family; the 4.5-Air size is servable on a single multi-GPU node. Candidate self-hosted model with the cleanest licence (plain MIT) among frontier-class open models - important if CodeKavach ships an appliance image with weights included. |
| Codestral and Devstral (Mistral AI) | Mistral AI | Codestral-22B-v0.1: Mistral AI Non-Production License (MNPL) - research/testing only, no production or commercial use without a paid licence. Devstral-Small (2507, and Small-2 2512): Apache-2.0. Devstral-2-123B: Mistral 'Modified MIT' - NOT plain MIT: clause 2 withdraws all rights if the user's (or employer's) global consolidated monthly revenue exceeds US\$20 million, unless a commercial licence is bought from Mistral | [mistral.ai](https://mistral.ai/news/codestral/); [related page](https://mistral.ai/licenses/MNPL-0.1.md) | [inspiration] | Mistral's code models. Licence trap to document: CodeKavach must not default to, bundle or recommend Codestral weights for client audits; use Devstral-Small (Apache-2.0) or the paid Codestral API instead. Devstral-2-123B must also be flagged: a bank or Big-Four firm is above the US\$20M/month revenue threshold and therefore has no licence to self-host it without a commercial agreement. |
| DeepSeek-V3 / V3.1 / V3.2 and DeepSeek-R1 | DeepSeek-AI | MIT for DeepSeek-R1, R1-0528, V3-0324, V3.1 and V3.2 (Hugging Face licence tags). The original December-2024 DeepSeek-V3 card carries no SPDX tag (code MIT, weights under the separate DeepSeek model licence) | [arXiv:2412.19437](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.19437); [arXiv:2501.12948](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.12948) | [planned] | 671B-parameter MoE general and reasoning models that are competitive with closed frontier models on code reasoning; R1's MIT licence explicitly allows distillation. High-end self-hosted option for clients with multi-GPU servers, and (via Bedrock/Azure/Together) a way to use DeepSeek quality without DeepSeek's own API. |
| Kimi K2 (Moonshot AI) | Kimi Team, Moonshot AI | Modified MIT: MIT plus a clause requiring prominent 'Kimi K2' attribution in the UI of products with >100M monthly active users or >US\$20M monthly revenue | [arXiv:2507.20534](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.20534); [GitHub: MoonshotAI/Kimi-K2](https://github.com/MoonshotAI/Kimi-K2) | [planned] | 1T-parameter (32B active) MoE model tuned for agentic coding; later K2-Instruct-0905, K2-Thinking and K2.5 releases use the same licence. Optional high-end self-hosted model. The attribution trigger is irrelevant to a university project but must be disclosed to large bank clients who embed it. |
| DeepSeek-Coder-V2 | DeepSeek-AI | Code: MIT. Weights: DeepSeek Model License (custom, use-restricted; permits commercial use) | [arXiv:2406.11931](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11931); [GitHub: deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-Coder-V2](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-Coder-V2) | [baseline] | 236B-parameter (21B active) and 16B 'Lite' MoE code models supporting 338 languages and 128K context. Baseline open code model for the evaluation (the Lite variant runs on a single GPU). The custom weight licence with use restrictions needs a legal note if weights are redistributed in an appliance. |
| IBM Granite Code models | Mishra et al., IBM Research | Apache-2.0 | [arXiv:2405.04324](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.04324); [Hugging Face: ibm-granite/granite-34b-code-instruct-8k](https://huggingface.co/ibm-granite/granite-34b-code-instruct-8k) | [inspiration] | 3B-34B code models trained on licence-permissible data under IBM's enterprise governance process; succeeded by the general Granite 3.x/4.0 models. Reference option for compliance-sensitive clients who want an enterprise-vendor provenance statement; not competitive with Qwen3-Coder/gpt-oss on capability. |
| StarCoder2 and The Stack v2 | Lozhkov et al., BigCode project | BigCode OpenRAIL-M v1 (weights; royalty-free with behavioural use restrictions that must be passed downstream); code Apache-2.0 | [arXiv:2402.19173](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.19173); [bigcode-project.org](https://www.bigcode-project.org/docs/pages/bigcode-openrail/) | [baseline] | 3B/7B/15B code models trained on permissively licensed, opt-out-respecting data with full training-data transparency. Reference baseline with the best data-provenance story (useful if a client objects to models trained on unknown code). OpenRAIL use restrictions are not OSI-open; note in the licence matrix. |
| Code Llama, Llama 3.x and Llama 4 (Meta) and their community licences | Rozière et al., Meta AI | Code Llama: Llama 2 Community License. Llama 3.3: Llama 3.3 Community License. Llama 4: Llama 4 Community License. All are custom, not OSI-approved: they require 'Built with Llama' attribution, 'Llama' in derivative model names, an acceptable-use policy, and a separate licence above 700M monthly active users | [arXiv:2308.12950](https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.12950); [GitHub: meta-llama/llama-models](https://github.com/meta-llama/llama-models/blob/main/models/llama3_3/LICENSE) | [baseline] | Meta's open-weight families. Baseline for comparability with prior LLM-vulnerability-detection literature; not recommended as the default. |

## 5. Platform integration documentation

Documentation and client libraries for the delivery surfaces: GitHub App, Action, code scanning, issues and Projects; other forges and trackers; IDE and agent protocols.

### 5.1 GitHub

| Document | Publisher | Link | Role tag | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| GitHub Docs - Authenticating as a GitHub App installation | GitHub | [docs.github.com](https://docs.github.com/en/apps/creating-github-apps/authenticating-with-a-github-app/authenticating-as-a-github-app-installation); [related page](https://docs.github.com/en/apps/creating-github-apps/registering-a-github-app/choosing-permissions-for-a-github-app) | [integrated] | Describes the two-step GitHub App auth flow: sign a short-lived JWT with the app private key, exchange it for a one-hour installation access token that can be down-scoped to specific repositories and permissions. Integrate: the CodeKavach GitHub App mints per-installation, repo-scoped tokens (contents:read, issues:write, checks:write, security_events:write, organization_projects:write) instead of asking clients for a PAT. Least-privilege token scoping is part of the threat model for a tool that reads bank source code. |
| GitHub Docs - Creating Docker container / composite actions and metadata syntax | GitHub | [docs.github.com](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/tutorials/use-containerized-services/create-a-docker-container-action); [related page](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/tutorials/create-actions/create-a-composite-action) | [integrated] | Tutorials for packaging an action either as a Docker container (Linux runners only, full control of the toolchain) or as a composite action (works on all runners, reuses steps). action.yml metadata defines inputs, outputs and branding. Integrate: ship `codekavach/scan-action` as a Docker action (Python 3.12 + bundled OSS scanners pinned by digest) with a thin composite wrapper that uploads SARIF. Docker packaging also gives the air-gapped self-hosted-runner story. |
| GitHub Docs - SARIF support for code scanning (properties and upload limits) | GitHub | [docs.github.com](https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/reference/code-scanning/sarif-files/sarif-support); [related page](https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/how-tos/find-and-fix-code-vulnerabilities/integrate-with-existing-tools/upload-sarif-file) | [integrated] | Defines the SARIF 2.1.0 subset GitHub consumes and its limits: 10 MB gzip per file, 20 runs/file, 25,000 results/run (only the top 5,000 by severity are kept), 25,000 rules/run. Map findings to it: the SARIF exporter must emit security-severity from the CVSS score, CWE tags (`external/cwe/cwe-89`), stable partialFingerprints (so de-pseudonymised re-scans do not duplicate alerts), codeFlows for taint paths, and must chunk/prioritise to stay under 5,000 results and 10 MB. |
| GitHub Docs - Secure use reference for GitHub Actions | GitHub | [docs.github.com](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/security/secure-use) | [inspiration] | Security hardening guidance for workflows: secret handling, script-injection via untrusted context values, pinning third-party actions to a full commit SHA, GITHUB_TOKEN permissions. Threat-model input: the CodeKavach Action holds an LLM API key and reads untrusted PR content, so documentation must prescribe `permissions:` minimisation, SHA pinning, and never running with secrets on untrusted fork PRs (the same prompt-injection caveat Anthropic prints for its own action). |
| GitHub Docs - Using the API to manage Projects (GraphQL ProjectV2) | GitHub | [docs.github.com](https://docs.github.com/en/issues/planning-and-tracking-with-projects/automating-your-project/using-the-api-to-manage-projects); [related page](https://docs.github.com/en/graphql/reference) | [integrated] | Walk-through of the ProjectV2 GraphQL surface: createProjectV2, addProjectV2ItemById, addProjectV2DraftIssue, updateProjectV2ItemFieldValue, deleteProjectV2Item, field/option look-ups. Integrate: Kanban-card creation. Flow = ensure project -> ensure single-select fields (Severity, CWE, Status) -> create issue -> addProjectV2ItemById -> updateProjectV2ItemFieldValue. |
| GitHub Docs - Validating webhook deliveries (and webhook events/payloads) | GitHub | [docs.github.com](https://docs.github.com/en/webhooks/using-webhooks/validating-webhook-deliveries); [related page](https://docs.github.com/en/webhooks/webhook-events-and-payloads) | [integrated] [inspiration] | Explains HMAC-SHA256 verification of the X-Hub-Signature-256 header with a shared webhook secret and constant-time comparison. Integrate: the FastAPI webhook receiver must verify signatures before queuing a scan; push/pull_request/installation events drive scan scheduling. Unsigned or replayed webhooks are a threat-model input for the hosted deployment. |
| GitHub REST API - Code scanning + github/codeql-action upload-sarif | GitHub | [docs.github.com](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/code-scanning/code-scanning); [GitHub: github/codeql-action](https://github.com/github/codeql-action/tree/main/upload-sarif) | [integrated] | REST endpoints to upload a gzip+base64 SARIF payload for a commit/ref and to list/dismiss alerts. Integrate: CI path uses `github/codeql-action/upload-sarif`; the GitHub App/server path calls the REST endpoint directly with security_events:write. Note that private-repo code scanning needs GitHub Code Security (paid) - CodeKavach must degrade to Checks + PR comments when it is absent. Licence: MIT (codeql-action repo). |
| GitHub REST API - Issues (and sub-issues) | GitHub | [docs.github.com](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/issues/issues); [related page](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/issues/sub-issues) | [planned] | Reference for creating, updating, labelling and searching issues; sub-issues endpoints allow a parent/child hierarchy. Integrate: the optional 'raise issues in the client repo' step. One parent issue per audit with sub-issues per finding; idempotency via a hidden fingerprint marker in the body so re-scans update instead of duplicating. No code fix is pushed - issues only. |
| GitHub REST API - Rate limits and integrator best practices | GitHub | [docs.github.com](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/using-the-rest-api/rate-limits-for-the-rest-api); [related page](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/using-the-rest-api/best-practices-for-using-the-rest-api) | [inspiration] | Primary limits (installation tokens start at 5,000 req/h and scale; GITHUB_TOKEN 1,000 req/h per repo) and secondary limits: max 100 concurrent requests, 900 points/min. Hard engineering constraint for bulk issue creation: an audit with 600 findings cannot be pushed as 600 issues in one burst. CodeKavach needs a throttled, resumable issue-sync queue (<= ~1 write/sec, back-off on 403/429) and should group low-severity findings. |
| GitHub REST API - Projects (items, fields, views) | GitHub | [docs.github.com](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/projects/items); [related page](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/projects/projects) | [planned] | A newer REST surface for Projects (v2): list/add/update/delete project items for org- and user-owned projects (`/orgs/{org}/projectsV2/{number}/items`). Integrate: simpler alternative to GraphQL for the Kanban sync in Python (plain httpx). Keep GraphQL as fallback for project creation and for GitHub Enterprise Server versions that predate the REST endpoints. |
| GitHub code scanning (SARIF upload, codeql-action) | GitHub | [docs.github.com](https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/code-scanning/introduction-to-code-scanning/about-code-scanning); [related page](https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/code-scanning/integrating-with-code-scanning/sarif-support-for-code-scanning) | [planned] | GitHub feature that displays alerts from CodeQL or any third-party tool that uploads SARIF 2.1.0, with PR annotations and alert lifecycle tracking. Output target for the GitHub Action and GitHub App: upload CodeKavach SARIF so findings appear in the Security tab. Constraints: private bank repos need a paid licence for the feature, so issues + Kanban cards remain the licence-free fallback; conform to GitHub's SARIF subset and limits. Licence: Proprietary service (codeql-action is MIT); free for public repos, paid GitHub Code Security / Advanced Security for private repos. |
| GitHub Docs - Best practices for creating a GitHub App | GitHub | [docs.github.com](https://docs.github.com/en/apps/creating-github-apps/about-creating-github-apps/best-practices-for-creating-a-github-app) | [inspiration] | GitHub's checklist for app authors: minimal permissions, securing private keys and tokens, webhook secrets, handling rate limits, and not acting outside the installation's scope. Design input for the GitHub App security section of the architecture document and for the self-assessment of CodeKavach itself (a security tool must not be the weak link). |
| GitHub REST API - Check runs (Checks API) | GitHub | [docs.github.com](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/checks/runs); [related page](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/guides/using-the-rest-api-to-interact-with-checks) | [planned] | Lets a GitHub App create check runs with a conclusion, markdown summary and line annotations on a commit. Integrate: PR gate ('CodeKavach: 2 High findings') with inline annotations, working on any plan without GitHub Code Security. Annotations are batched per request, so the reporter must page them. |
| GitHub REST API - Pull request review comments and reviews | GitHub | [docs.github.com](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/pulls/comments); [related page](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/pulls/reviews) | [planned] | Endpoints for line-anchored review comments and for submitting a whole review (COMMENT / REQUEST_CHANGES) with many comments in one call. Integrate: post one batched review per scan (one content-creating request instead of N) containing the de-pseudonymised finding text; this is how CodeRabbit/PR-Agent/claude-code-security-review surface results, so it is the UX baseline. |
| GitHub Docs - Publishing actions in GitHub Marketplace | GitHub | [docs.github.com](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/how-tos/create-and-publish-actions/publish-in-github-marketplace) | [planned] | Requirements for listing an action: public repo, single action.yml at the root, unique name. Distribution channel for the CI interface; dictates repo layout (one action per repository) and semantic-version tags. |
| GitHub REST API - Repository security advisories | GitHub | [docs.github.com](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/security-advisories/repository-advisories) | [inspiration] | Endpoints for creating and managing draft repository security advisories and privately reported vulnerabilities. Design option for the 'raise findings in the client's repo' feature: ordinary issues are visible to everyone with read access, so critical findings may be better filed as draft advisories (or issues in a restricted security repo). |

## 6. Competitor and adjacent product landscape

Commercial and open-source products that overlap with CodeKavach. Vendor claims are summarised from public documentation on the verification date and were not independently tested unless the Notes say so.

**Gap analysis.** Across the products and projects in this section, the privacy answers on offer fall into three groups. (1) *Contractual*: zero-data-retention terms, SOC 2 reports and no-training pledges, while full diffs, files or whole repositories reach the model provider in clear (CodeRabbit, GitHub Copilot code review, Cursor Bugbot, Gemini Code Assist, Claude Code Security, Codex Security, ZeroPath). (2) *Deployment location*: self-hosted, VPC or air-gapped installs with a customer-run or vendor fine-tuned model (Qodo, Tabnine, Tabby, GitLab Duo Self-Hosted, Corgea, Sourcegraph Cody Enterprise), which needs GPU capacity and gives up frontier-model accuracy. (3) *Entity-level redaction gateways* that mask secrets and PII in natural-language text (CodeGate, LLM Guard, the LiteLLM Presidio guardrail, Skyflow, Limina, Protecto, Nightfall; see section 3.10), which do not parse code, do not rename identifiers consistently across files and do not select which code is sent.

The nearest neighbours each cover one piece. Semgrep's LLM triage has the same pipeline shape (deterministic SAST, then LLM triage) but scopes the snippet and relies on provider contracts. Snyk's CodeReduce reduces the prompt to defect-relevant statements, for accuracy rather than privacy, and does not pseudonymise. IRIS and Vulnhuntr send signatures or call-chain fragments, in clear. The one reversible identifier-renaming tool found is a single-language Swift skill with no slicing, no security focus and no leakage evaluation; CodeCipher obfuscates at embedding level, needs white-box model access and targets completion rather than vulnerability triage.

In the survey behind this file, no product or open-source project was found that combines all of the following: (a) deterministic local analysis first, with the LLM used for triage and reasoning only; (b) taint-path slicing, so that only the vulnerability-relevant fragment is a candidate for egress; (c) reversible AST-level pseudonymisation of identifiers, strings and comments, with the verdict restored locally; (d) an abstract-IR mode in which no code leaves; (e) provider neutrality across hosted APIs and self-hosted open models; (f) audit-firm-style reports with CVSS, CWE, OWASP and compliance mapping; and (g) a published measurement of the privacy-utility trade-off, that is, detection accuracy at each privacy level against an attacker-model leakage metric. That combination is the gap CodeKavach targets. The statement is bounded by the search date (2026-09-21) and by public documentation; vendors may do more than they document.

Closest incumbent to CodeKavach's pipeline shape (deterministic SAST -> LLM triage). Cite as evidence that even the privacy-conscious vendor relies on snippet-scoping + contracts, not pseudonymisation; baseline for the 'L1-only' point on the privacy-utility curve. |

Best available non-vendor evidence for the competitor section and for evaluation design (run on real OSS targets, count TP/FP by hand). Supports the claim that privacy posture among AI-SAST vendors is contractual and often opaque. |

Key related work for L3: independent evidence that sending a minimal analysis-derived slice helps model accuracy. Snyk does it for context-window/attention reasons, not for privacy, and does not pseudonymise - CodeKavach's novelty is combining reduction with reversible renaming and measuring leakage. |

Primary commercial baseline for 'LLM finds logic bugs'; CodeKavach differs by doing deterministic local analysis first and sending only pseudonymised slices. |

Competitor whose privacy answer is 'private model deployment'. CodeKavach's thesis is complementary: keep frontier-model accuracy while leaking less. |

Competitor with the same 'orchestrate OSS engines + LLM triage' architecture; no pseudonymisation layer advertised. Pricing page is a reference for the hosted offering. |
| Amazon Q Developer code reviews (successor to CodeGuru Security) - end-of-support notice | AWS | Proprietary | [aws.amazon.com](https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/devops/amazon-q-developer-end-of-support-announcement/); [docs.aws.amazon.com](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/amazonq/latest/qdeveloper-ug/code-reviews.html) | [related-work] | Amazon CodeGuru Security was shut down on 20 Nov 2025 with users pointed to Amazon Q Developer code reviews (SAST, secrets, SCA, IaC). Caveat for the landscape table: CodeGuru Security and Amazon Q Developer are dead or sunsetting. |
| Secure Code Audits: Checklist and Best Practices (CodeAnt AI blog) | CodeAnt AI (vendor blog, no author byline) | All rights reserved | [codeant.ai](https://codeant.ai/blogs/source-code-audit-checklist-best-practices-for-secure-code) | [baseline] | Vendor marketing article that walks through a source code audit in three phases: pre-audit preparation ... Two uses. (1) Competitor landscape entry: CodeAnt AI is a commercial AI code review and security platform; it is not privacy-preserving in the CodeKavach sense. |
| GitHub Copilot Autofix for code scanning | GitHub (Microsoft) | Proprietary (free on public repos; GitHub Code Security licence for private) | [docs.github.com](https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/concepts/code-scanning/autofix-for-code-scanning); [related page](https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/responsible-use/security-and-quality-ai-features) | [related-work] | Generates fix suggestions for code scanning alerts by sending alert description/location and relevant code to an OpenAI model hosted for GitHub; no Copilot seat needed. Adjacent, not a competitor for review (CodeKavach does no auto-fix). |
| SonarQube AI CodeFix | SonarSource | Proprietary feature (Cloud Team/Enterprise; Server Enterprise/Data Center) | [docs.sonarsource.com](https://docs.sonarsource.com/agent-centric-development-cycle/in-your-long-living-branches-the-code-maintenance-loop/ai-codefix); [sonarsource.com](https://www.sonarsource.com/solutions/ai/ai-codefix/) | [baseline] | LLM-generated fix suggestions for Sonar issues; the affected snippet plus issue description go to a Sonar-selected LLM (self-hosted servers can configure their own LLM endpoint), with a contractual no-training guarantee. Incumbent comparison point; also a reminder that SonarQube Community Build lacks taint analysis and AI features, which matters if Sonar is considered as a bundled engine. |
| DryRun Security | DryRun Security | Proprietary | [dryrun.security](https://www.dryrun.security/); [docs.dryrun.security](https://docs.dryrun.security/) | [baseline] [inspiration] | 'Contextual Security Analysis' GitHub/GitLab app that evaluates PRs with LLM-backed analyzers and natural-language code policies. Competitor; natural-language policy idea is design inspiration for client-specific audit checklists. |
| Pixee (Pixeebot) and Codemodder | Pixee | AGPL-3.0 (codemodder-python and codemodder-java); service proprietary | [pixee.ai](https://www.pixee.ai/); [GitHub: pixee/codemodder-python](https://github.com/pixee/codemodder-python) | [baseline] | Automated triage and remediation platform; the open-source Codemodder frameworks apply deterministic codemods with optional LLM assistance. Reference only. Licence trap: Codemodder is AGPL-3.0 and must not be embedded in an MIT project. |
| Mobb (and Bugsy CLI) | Mobb | MIT (Bugsy CLI); service proprietary | [mobb.ai](https://www.mobb.ai/); [GitHub: mobb-dev/bugsy](https://github.com/mobb-dev/bugsy) | [related-work] | Vendor-agnostic auto-remediation: ingests SARIF/reports from Checkmarx, Snyk, Fortify, CodeQL, Semgrep etc. and produces deterministic-plus-AI fixes. Out of scope functionally (auto-fix) but demonstrates the 'consume any scanner's report' adapter pattern. |
| Socket (socket-cli) | Socket Inc. | MIT (CLI); service proprietary | [GitHub: SocketDev/socket-cli](https://github.com/SocketDev/socket-cli) | [related-work] | Supply-chain security service that uses static + LLM analysis of package behaviour to flag malicious or risky dependencies. Adjacent (SCA/malware) rather than code review; possible optional enrichment for the SCA stage. |
| Checkmarx One / Veracode Fix / OpenText Fortify Aviator / Black Duck Coverity (incumbent SAST with AI) | Checkmarx, Veracode, OpenText, Black Duck | Proprietary | [docs.veracode.com](https://docs.veracode.com/r/veracode_fix) | [baseline] | The enterprise incumbents a bank is likely to own already. Baselines for the Big-Four-style audit narrative and for import adapters (clients will want CodeKavach to triage an existing Fortify/Checkmarx report). |
| Codacy / DeepSource / Arnica / Backslash / Cycode / Endor Labs (ASPM and code-quality platforms with AI features) | various vendors | Proprietary (DeepSource's Globstar analyser is MIT) | [endorlabs.com](https://www.endorlabs.com/); [deepsource.com](https://deepsource.com/) | [related-work] | Group entry for commercial platforms that bolt LLM triage/fix onto SAST/SCA/ASPM: Codacy (codacy.com). Landscape table rows. None advertises slice minimisation or reversible pseudonymisation; all are SaaS-first. Endor/Backslash reachability ideas inform CodeKavach's SCA prioritisation. |

### 6.2 AI pull-request review

| Product / project | Vendor / maintainer | Licence / model | Link | Role tag | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CodeRabbit | CodeRabbit Inc. | Proprietary (SaaS; self-hosted only on Enterprise) | [coderabbit.ai](https://www.coderabbit.ai/); [docs.coderabbit.ai](https://docs.coderabbit.ai/faq) | [baseline] | Market-leading AI PR reviewer for GitHub/GitLab/Bitbucket/Azure DevOps plus IDE and CLI; combines LLM review with bundled linters/SAST. Baseline/competitor. Privacy posture = contractual (ZDR, SOC 2) rather than technical minimisation: full diffs and surrounding code reach the LLM provider in clear. This is the contrast case for CodeKavach's L2-L4. |
| PR-Agent (community-owned, formerly Qodo/CodiumAI) | The PR Agent community (donated by Qodo) | MIT | [GitHub: The-PR-Agent/pr-agent](https://github.com/The-PR-Agent/pr-agent); [qodo.ai](https://www.qodo.ai/) | [baseline] [inspiration] | The original open-source AI PR reviewer (/review, /improve, /describe, /ask) for GitHub, GitLab, Bitbucket, Azure DevOps and Gitea, using any LiteLLM-reachable model including Ollama. Closest OSS reference architecture for the GitHub App/Action + multi-provider LLM plumbing, and an OSS baseline to compare against (it sends raw diffs). MIT licence (since July 2026) permits code reuse in CodeKavach, but only from commits at or after the MIT switch - releases from May 2025 to May 2026 were AGPL-3.0. |
| Anthropic Claude Code - Code Review (managed multi-agent PR review) | Anthropic | Proprietary (Team/Enterprise; billed per token) | [code.claude.com](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/code-review); [thenewstack.io](https://thenewstack.io/anthropic-launches-a-multi-agent-code-review-tool-for-claude-code/) | [baseline] | Managed GitHub PR reviewer in research preview for Team and Enterprise plans: several agents examine the diff and surrounding code in parallel on Anthropic infrastructure, a verification step filters false positives, and findings are posted as inline comments plus a neutral check run. Competitor and UX reference (severity-tagged inline comments + a machine-readable check-run summary that never blocks merges). |
| GitHub Copilot code review | GitHub (Microsoft) | Proprietary (Copilot subscription) | [docs.github.com](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/concepts/agents/code-review); [related page](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/how-tos/use-copilot-agents/request-a-code-review/use-code-review) | [baseline] | Copilot can be requested as a PR reviewer (or run in the IDE) and leaves suggested changes; custom instructions steer it. Competitor with the strongest distribution. No client-side pseudonymisation; privacy rests on GitHub/Microsoft enterprise terms. Useful UX baseline for PR comments. |
| Cursor Bugbot | Anysphere (Cursor) | Proprietary (usage-based billing) | [cursor.com](https://cursor.com/docs/bugbot); [related page](https://cursor.com/bugbot) | [baseline] | Cursor's automated PR reviewer: analyses diffs for bugs, security issues and quality problems on every PR update or on demand, comments with explanations and suggested fixes, and is steered by `.cursor/BUGBOT.md` files plus team rules. Competitor with large distribution (and now the owner of Graphite). |
| Gemini Code Assist for GitHub (PR review) | Google | Proprietary (consumer version free; enterprise version in preview) | [developers.google.com](https://developers.google.com/gemini-code-assist/docs/review-github-code) | [baseline] | Google's GitHub app that summarises pull requests and posts Gemini-generated review comments; reviewers can be invoked with `/gemini` commands and customised per repository or, in the enterprise version, across repositories from the Google Cloud console. Competitor row for the landscape table (the third frontier-lab PR reviewer alongside Copilot and Claude). No redaction layer is described; code goes to Google's service. |
| Bito AI Code Review Agent | Bito | Proprietary | [bito.ai](https://bito.ai/); [docs.bito.ai](https://docs.bito.ai/) | [related-work] | AI code review agent for GitHub/GitLab/Bitbucket and IDEs that also runs bundled static analysers. Minor competitor; illustrates 'self-hosted runner, hosted model' which does not solve the code-leaves-the-perimeter problem. |
| Ellipsis | Ellipsis (YC W24) | Proprietary | [ellipsis.dev](https://www.ellipsis.dev/); [related page](https://www.ellipsis.dev/docs) | [related-work] | AI reviewer and bug-fix agent installed as a GitHub App; per-seat SaaS. Minor competitor; listed for completeness. |
| Greptile | Greptile | Proprietary (self-host option for enterprise) | [greptile.com](https://www.greptile.com/); [related page](https://www.greptile.com/security) | [baseline] | AI PR reviewer that indexes the whole repository into a code graph to give codebase-aware comments; SaaS with a self-hosted/VPC enterprise option. Competitor. Whole-repo indexing is the opposite of CodeKavach's minimal-slice principle - a useful contrast in the privacy-utility discussion. |
| Qodo (formerly CodiumAI; Qodo Merge) | Qodo | Proprietary | [qodo.ai](https://www.qodo.ai/); [docs.qodo.ai](https://docs.qodo.ai/) | [baseline] | Commercial code-review/quality platform (PR review, IDE, CLI agents) with SaaS. Competitor; shows that the enterprise market already asks for air-gapped deployment, but solves privacy by deployment location, not by data minimisation. |
| Graphite (Graphite Agent / Diamond) - now part of Cursor | Graphite (acquired by Anysphere/Cursor, Dec 2025) | Proprietary | [graphite.com](https://graphite.com/); [cursor.com](https://cursor.com/blog/graphite) | [baseline] | Stacked-PR workflow platform with an AI reviewer; Cursor announced the acquisition in December 2025 and says Graphite continues as a product. Competitor/market signal: AI review is consolidating into AI-coding vendors. graphite.dev now redirects to graphite.com. |
| Sourcery | Sourcery | Proprietary (free for OSS) | [sourcery.ai](https://www.sourcery.ai/); [docs.sourcery.ai](https://docs.sourcery.ai/) | [related-work] | AI code reviewer for GitHub/GitLab and IDEs, historically Python-refactoring focused, now general PR review with a security-review offering. Minor competitor; per-seat SaaS pricing model reference. |

### 6.3 Frontier-lab and autonomous security agents

| Product / project | Vendor / maintainer | Licence / model | Link | Role tag | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Claude Code Security (Anthropic) | Anthropic | Proprietary | [anthropic.com](https://www.anthropic.com/news/claude-code-security); [support.claude.com](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/14661296-use-claude-security) | [baseline] | Hosted whole-codebase vulnerability scanning announced as a limited research preview for Enterprise and Team customers (with expedited access for open-source maintainers). Strongest evidence that frontier LLMs can do audit-grade review - and the clearest example of the adoption blocker CodeKavach targets: the entire repository is uploaded to the provider. Design inspiration: adversarial self-verification stage and confidence scores. |
| anthropics/claude-code-security-review (GitHub Action) | Anthropic | MIT | [GitHub: anthropics/claude-code-security-review](https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code-security-review) | [baseline] [inspiration] | Open-source Action (and the /security-review command in Claude Code) that has Claude analyse the PR diff with repository context. Direct OSS baseline to beat/compare: same interface (Action -> PR comments) but raw code goes to one provider. Reuse ideas: FP-filter prompt categories, JSON finding schema. Its prompt-injection warning is a threat-model item for CodeKavach too (code comments are attacker-controlled text; L2 comment stripping helps). |
| OpenAI Aardvark -> Codex Security | OpenAI | Proprietary | [openai.com](https://openai.com/index/introducing-aardvark/); [developers.openai.com](https://developers.openai.com/codex/security) | [baseline] [inspiration] | Agentic security researcher that builds a repo-specific threat model, scans commit-by-commit, validates suspected bugs in an isolated sandbox and proposes patches. Competitor and design inspiration (threat-model-first scanning, sandbox validation). Same privacy limitation: connected repositories are processed in OpenAI's cloud. |
| Google DeepMind CodeMender and Project Zero/DeepMind Big Sleep | Google DeepMind / Google Project Zero | not stated | [deepmind.google](https://deepmind.google/blog/introducing-codemender-an-ai-agent-for-code-security/); [projectzero.google](https://projectzero.google/2024/10/from-naptime-to-big-sleep.html) | [related-work] | Big Sleep (evolved from Project Naptime) is an LLM agent with debugger/code-browser tools that found a real exploitable SQLite ... Related work for the 'LLM + program-analysis tools beats LLM alone' argument that justifies CodeKavach's deterministic-first pipeline. Not products a bank can buy. |
| XBOW and the XBOW validation benchmarks | XBOW | Apache-2.0 (benchmarks) | [GitHub: xbow-engineering/validation-benchmarks](https://github.com/xbow-engineering/validation-benchmarks); [xbow.com](https://xbow.com/) | [related-work] | XBOW is an autonomous offensive-security (pentest) agent; it published 104 dockerised web-security challenges with source code and flags used to validate the agent. XBOW itself is dynamic testing, i.e. adjacent. |

### 6.4 Open-source LLM-assisted security scanners

| Product / project | Vendor / maintainer | Licence / model | Link | Role tag | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Vulnhuntr | Protect AI (now Palo Alto Networks) | AGPL-3.0 | [GitHub: protectai/vulnhuntr](https://github.com/protectai/vulnhuntr) | [baseline] | LLM-driven zero-shot vulnerability hunter for Python: starts at request handlers, iteratively asks the model which functions/classes it needs next, and follows the call chain to a sink. Closest OSS precedent for iterative, slice-at-a-time prompting - but it sends raw source. Baseline to compare against at L0 (no privacy). AGPL-3.0: study and benchmark it, do not import code. |
| OpenAnt | Nahum Korda, Gadi Evron (Knostic) | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: knostic/OpenAnt](https://github.com/knostic/OpenAnt); [arXiv:2606.19149](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19149) | [baseline] [inspiration] | Open-source pipeline that splits a repository into self-contained units reachable from external entry points (cutting the analysed surface sharply). Closest open-source architectural cousin for the reachability-filtered unit idea; Apache-2.0 so code can be studied and, with attribution, reused. It sends code units to the LLM unredacted, which is the gap CodeKavach fills. |
| Vulnhalla (CodeQL findings triaged by an LLM) | CyberArk Labs (CyberArk Threat Research) | Apache-2.0 (Vulnhalla itself). It drives the CodeQL CLI, so the GitHub CodeQL Terms and Conditions still govern which codebases may be analysed (see the CodeQL entry). | [GitHub: cyberark/Vulnhalla](https://github.com/cyberark/Vulnhalla) | [baseline] | Open-source Python pipeline that fetches or loads CodeQL databases for C/C++, Python and JavaScript projects, runs CodeQL queries. Baseline / prior art for the 'SAST candidate -> LLM triage' stage. Not embeddable for bank code because of the CodeQL CLI licence. |
| sast-ai-workflow (Red Hat) | Red Hat Ecosystem AppEng | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: RHEcosystemAppEng/sast-ai-workflow](https://github.com/RHEcosystemAppEng/sast-ai-workflow) | [baseline] [inspiration] | LangGraph agent pipeline that takes existing SAST reports plus source, filters known false positives by embedding similarity (FAISS). Reference implementation for the triage stage (state graph, confidence scoring, known-FP memory) under an MIT-compatible licence; built for on-prem models, so again no pseudonymisation. |
| OSS-CRS | Andrew Chin, et al. (Georgia Tech SSLab) | MIT (repo) | [arXiv:2603.08566](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.08566); [GitHub: sslab-gatech/oss-crs](https://github.com/sslab-gatech/oss-crs) | [baseline] | Framework that ports AIxCC cyber reasoning systems off the defunct competition infrastructure so they run locally against OSS-Fuzz projects with budget-aware resource management; already used to find new bugs. MIT-licensed way to run AIxCC-grade systems as comparators in the evaluation without touching GPL/AGPL code directly. |
| Atlantis (Team Atlanta AIxCC CRS) | Team Atlanta (Georgia Tech, Samsung Research, KAIST, POSTECH); tech report lead author Taesoo Kim | GPL-3.0 with third-party components | [GitHub: Team-Atlanta/aixcc-afc-atlantis](https://github.com/Team-Atlanta/aixcc-afc-atlantis); [arXiv:2509.14589](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14589) | [baseline] [inspiration] | AIxCC-winning system combining multiple independent bug-finding and patching pipelines (C and Java) with LLM agents in an ensemble. Reference for ensemble orchestration and for Java-side LLM-guided analysis; GPL-3.0 - read, do not reuse code. |
| Buttercup (Trail of Bits AIxCC CRS) | Trail of Bits | AGPL-3.0 | [GitHub: trailofbits/buttercup](https://github.com/trailofbits/buttercup); [blog.trailofbits.com](https://blog.trailofbits.com/2025/08/09/trail-of-bits-buttercup-wins-2nd-place-in-aixcc-challenge/) | [baseline] [inspiration] | Second-place AIxCC system: LLM-assisted fuzzing harness/seed generation, vulnerability triage and multi-agent patching built around deterministic workflows, with a laptop-scale version released. Design inspiration for 'LLM only where deterministic tools fall short'. AGPL-3.0 prevents embedding in MIT CodeKavach. |
| Fraim | Fraim (fraim-dev) | MIT | [GitHub: fraim-dev/fraim](https://github.com/fraim-dev/fraim) | [baseline] | CLI and GitHub Action offering LLM security workflows - PR risk flagging, code security analysis with SARIF output, and others. Comparable MIT-licensed OSS tool; useful for CLI/SARIF ergonomics and as an LLM-only baseline without a privacy layer. |
| GitHub Security Lab Taskflow Agent | GitHub Security Lab | MIT | [GitHub: GitHubSecurityLab/seclab-taskflow-agent](https://github.com/GitHubSecurityLab/seclab-taskflow-agent) | [baseline] [inspiration] | MCP-based agent framework in which security researchers encode audit workflows as YAML 'taskflows' (e.g. triage CodeQL alerts, variant analysis) executed by an LLM with tool access. Design inspiration for declarative, auditable LLM workflows - attractive for a Big-Four-style methodology where each review step must be documented and repeatable. |
| Strix | usestrix | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: usestrix/strix](https://github.com/usestrix/strix) | [related-work] | Open-source multi-agent 'AI hacker' that runs an application, probes it dynamically and validates findings with PoCs; supports multiple LLM providers. Adjacent (dynamic). Possible future 'validate the static finding' stage; not part of the privacy study. |
| Fabric (patterns for security analysis) | Daniel Miessler | MIT | [GitHub: danielmiessler/fabric](https://github.com/danielmiessler/fabric) | [inspiration] | CLI framework of crowd-sourced prompt 'patterns' runnable against any provider. Prompt-library inspiration only; no program analysis. |
| Nuclei (with AI template generation) | ProjectDiscovery | MIT | [GitHub: projectdiscovery/nuclei](https://github.com/projectdiscovery/nuclei); [docs.projectdiscovery.io](https://docs.projectdiscovery.io/opensource/nuclei/running) | [related-work] | YAML-template DAST/vulnerability scanner; recent versions can generate templates from a natural-language prompt through ProjectDiscovery's cloud API. Out of scope for source review. Could verify a static finding against a running demo app. |

| Continue | Continue Dev, Inc. | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: continuedev/continue](https://github.com/continuedev/continue); [docs.continue.dev](https://docs.continue.dev/) | [baseline] [inspiration] | Open-source coding agent / IDE extension and CLI with pluggable model providers (any cloud API, Ollama, vLLM) and configurable context providers; supports running checks on PRs. Design inspiration and code reference for a provider-agnostic config schema and VS Code extension structure; Apache-2.0 is MIT-compatible. |
| Tabnine (private / air-gapped deployment) | Tabnine | Proprietary | [tabnine.com](https://www.tabnine.com/code-privacy/); [related page](https://www.tabnine.com/pricing/) | [baseline] | AI coding assistant whose differentiator is deployment control: SaaS, VPC, on-prem and fully air-gapped, with a zero-code-retention and no-training policy. Shows the market's current answer to 'code must not leave': move the model inside. CodeKavach supports that too (Ollama/vLLM) but adds L2-L4 for clients who want frontier-model accuracy without full disclosure. |
| GitLab Duo Self-Hosted (and Duo Code Review in merge requests) | GitLab | Proprietary (Premium/Ultimate plus a Duo add-on) | [docs.gitlab.com](https://docs.gitlab.com/administration/gitlab_duo_self_hosted/); [related page](https://docs.gitlab.com/user/project/merge_requests/duo_in_merge_requests/) | [baseline] | Lets a self-managed GitLab instance run Duo features, including AI review of merge requests. The incumbent answer a bank on self-managed GitLab will be offered: privacy by moving the model inside. |
| Sourcegraph Cody (enterprise-only) and Amp | Sourcegraph; Amp (independent company since Dec 2025) | Proprietary | [sourcegraph.com](https://sourcegraph.com/blog/changes-to-cody-free-pro-and-enterprise-starter-plans); [ampcode.com](https://ampcode.com/) | [related-work] | Sourcegraph discontinued Cody Free/Pro in July 2025, keeping Cody for enterprise (self-hosted Sourcegraph, BYO LLM) and steering individuals to the Amp agent, which was later spun out. Landscape caveat: the product changed status. Cody Enterprise's BYO-LLM + self-hosted code graph is a precedent for CodeKavach's provider-agnostic design. |

OSS counterpart to Tabnine's 'move the model inside' answer; reference for packaging local-model serving. Same ee/ carve-out pattern as LiteLLM - keep imports out of ee/. |

Reference for how a mainstream IDE exposes local-model configuration; informs the VS Code extension's provider settings UI. |

## 7. Deliberately vulnerable applications and test suites

Targets for demos, regression tests and the accuracy experiments. Applications without per-vulnerability ground truth are demo and smoke-test material only; accuracy figures come from the suites in 7.2 and the datasets in section 2. Do not vendor these projects into the repository; fetch them at test time and respect each licence.

### 7.1 Deliberately vulnerable applications

| Application / suite | Licence | Link | Role tag | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| vuln-bank | MIT | [GitHub: Commando-X/vuln-bank](https://github.com/Commando-X/vuln-bank) | [evaluation] | Deliberately vulnerable banking web app and API (Python/Flask) covering SQLi, BOLA, JWT flaws, race conditions, file upload and, more recently, vulnerabilities in an integrated AI chatbot. Best-fit demo for the bank story: banking business logic (transfers, loans, virtual cards) is exactly what L2/L3 must hide while still finding the flaws. |
| PyGoat | MIT | [GitHub: adeyosemanputra/pygoat](https://github.com/adeyosemanputra/pygoat) | [evaluation] | Intentionally vulnerable Django application with labs for OWASP Top 10 categories. Primary Python/Django target (MIT, actively maintained). |
| OWASP Juice Shop | MIT | [GitHub: juice-shop/juice-shop](https://github.com/juice-shop/juice-shop); [pwning.owasp-juice.shop](https://pwning.owasp-juice.shop/) | [evaluation] | Flagship modern vulnerable web shop (Node.js/Express, Angular, TypeScript) with 100+ challenges across the OWASP Top 10 and a companion guide that documents every flaw. Primary JS/TS demo + evaluation target; challenge list doubles as ground truth. MIT, so snippets can appear in reports, papers and demo videos. |
| DVWA - Damn Vulnerable Web Application | GPL-3.0 | [GitHub: digininja/DVWA](https://github.com/digininja/DVWA) | [evaluation] | Classic PHP/MySQL training app with low/medium/high/impossible variants of each vulnerability - handy paired vulnerable/fixed code. Primary PHP target; the per-level source files give natural positive/negative pairs for false-positive measurement. |
| OWASP WebGoat | GPL-2.0-or-later | [GitHub: WebGoat/WebGoat](https://github.com/WebGoat/WebGoat) | [evaluation] | Lesson-based insecure Java/Spring Boot application covering injection, auth, XXE, deserialisation, JWT, SSRF and more. Primary Java target. GPL only matters if code is redistributed - scanning it is fine; keep it out of the CodeKavach repo (fetch at test time). |
| OWASP crAPI | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: OWASP/crAPI](https://github.com/OWASP/crAPI) | [evaluation] | 'Completely ridiculous API': a polyglot microservice system (Java Spring, Python Django, Go) exhibiting the OWASP API Top 10. Multi-language, multi-service repo to test cross-service slicing and polyglot ingestion. |
| Damn Vulnerable Bank (Android) | MIT (both) | [GitHub: rewanthtammana/Damn-Vulnerable-Bank](https://github.com/rewanthtammana/Damn-Vulnerable-Bank); [GitHub: dineshshetty/Android-InsecureBankv2](https://github.com/dineshshetty/Android-InsecureBankv2) | [evaluation] | Intentionally vulnerable Android banking app (Java) with a Node.js back-end; InsecureBankv2 is the older equivalent with a Python back-end. Java/Android + back-end target themed on banking; covers hard-coded secrets, weak crypto, exported components. |
| VAmPI | MIT | [GitHub: erev0s/VAmPI](https://github.com/erev0s/VAmPI) | [evaluation] | Vulnerable Flask REST API built around the OWASP API Security Top 10 with an OpenAPI spec and a switch to turn vulnerabilities on/off. API-security target (BOLA, mass assignment, excessive data exposure) - the logic-flaw classes where LLM reasoning should beat pattern SAST. |
| OWASP VulnerableApp | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: SasanLabs/VulnerableApp](https://github.com/SasanLabs/VulnerableApp) | [evaluation] | Spring Boot app designed specifically for benchmarking scanners: each vulnerability type has multiple graded levels exposed with machine-readable metadata. Scanner-benchmark-oriented Java target with explicit per-level ground truth. |
| java-sec-code | not stated | [GitHub: JoyChou93/java-sec-code](https://github.com/JoyChou93/java-sec-code) | [evaluation] | Spring Boot project where each controller demonstrates a vulnerability class (SSRF, XXE, deserialisation, SpEL, path traversal...) often alongside the safe variant. Dense Java Spring sink/source catalogue for rule and taint-spec development. No licence file - reference only, do not vendor. |
| Vulpy | MIT | [GitHub: fportantier/vulpy](https://github.com/fportantier/vulpy) | [evaluation] | Flask app shipped in two trees - `bad` (vulnerable) and `good` (fixed) - covering SQLi, weak sessions, CSRF, broken auth, insecure crypto. Excellent paired vulnerable/fixed Python corpus for measuring FP rate at each privacy level. |
| OWASP NodeGoat | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: OWASP/NodeGoat](https://github.com/OWASP/NodeGoat) | [evaluation] | Express/MongoDB app demonstrating the OWASP Top 10 in Node.js with a tutorial explaining each issue and fix. Secondary JS target (NoSQL injection, SSJS injection, ReDoS). |
| AltoroJ (Altoro Mutual demo bank) | Apache-2.0 | [GitHub: HCL-TECH-SOFTWARE/AltoroJ](https://github.com/HCL-TECH-SOFTWARE/AltoroJ) | [evaluation] | Sample J2EE online-banking application (plain Java servlets and JSP, Apache Derby) built to demonstrate what happens when functionality is written without security; the README says it dates from 2008 and is used in academic curricula. Second bank-themed demo target next to vuln-bank, this time in Java: accounts, transfers and an admin area give realistic business logic for the L2/L3 leakage experiments. |
| DataDog vulnerable-java-application and Veracode verademo | Apache-2.0 (DataDog); MIT (verademo) | [GitHub: DataDog/vulnerable-java-application](https://github.com/DataDog/vulnerable-java-application); [GitHub: veracode/verademo](https://github.com/veracode/verademo) | [evaluation] | Two small vendor demo apps in Java: Datadog's shows command injection and SSRF; verademo is a Spring MVC social app with a broad CWE spread used in Veracode demos. Extra Java fixtures that commercial vendors themselves demo on - good for side-by-side comparison screenshots. |
| Damn Vulnerable GraphQL Application (DVGA) | MIT | [GitHub: dolevf/Damn-Vulnerable-GraphQL-Application](https://github.com/dolevf/Damn-Vulnerable-GraphQL-Application) | [evaluation] | Flask/Graphene GraphQL service with injection, DoS, information-disclosure and authorisation flaws. GraphQL-specific target (resolver-level authz, batching). |
| InsecureShop (Kotlin) and OVAA (Java) - vulnerable Android apps | MIT (InsecureShop); BSD-2-Clause (OVAA) | [GitHub: hax0rgb/InsecureShop](https://github.com/hax0rgb/InsecureShop); [GitHub: oversecured/ovaa](https://github.com/oversecured/ovaa) | [evaluation] | Two intentionally vulnerable Android apps with source: InsecureShop is written in Kotlin. Gives the Java/Kotlin plugin a Kotlin target (none of the other testbeds is Kotlin) and Android-specific sinks for the taint engine. |
| dvcsharp-api and WebGoat.NET (Core port) | MIT (dvcsharp-api); none declared (WebGoat.NET port) | [GitHub: appsecco/dvcsharp-api](https://github.com/appsecco/dvcsharp-api); [GitHub: tobyash86/WebGoat.NET](https://github.com/tobyash86/WebGoat.NET) | [evaluation] | Damn Vulnerable C# API (ASP.NET Core) and a .NET Core port of the original OWASP WebGoat.NET (the jerryhoff original targets legacy .NET Framework). C#/.NET targets. |
| we45 Vulnerable-Flask-App | not stated | [GitHub: we45/Vulnerable-Flask-App](https://github.com/we45/Vulnerable-Flask-App) | [evaluation] | Small intentionally vulnerable Flask REST API used in we45 DevSecOps demos (SQLi, insecure JWT, SSTI, deserialisation). Tiny API fixture for demos. No licence file detected - do not redistribute its code. |
| DVNA - Damn Vulnerable NodeJS Application | MIT | [GitHub: appsecco/dvna](https://github.com/appsecco/dvna) | [evaluation] | Small Express/Sequelize app with documented OWASP Top 10 flaws and a fixes branch. Small, fast JS fixture for CI regression tests of the pipeline. |
| GoVWA | not stated | [GitHub: 0c34/govwa](https://github.com/0c34/govwa) | [evaluation] | Go Vulnerable Web Application: SQLi, XSS, IDOR etc. in a plain net/http app. Go target; no licence file, reference only. |

| django.nV | GPL-2.0 | [GitHub: NetSPI/django.nV](https://github.com/NetSPI/django.nV) | [evaluation] | Vulnerable Django project-management app with tutorial walkthroughs. Secondary Django target; archived, so pin a commit. |

Source for extending the evaluation corpus by language without hand-searching. |

### 7.2 SAST benchmarks with ground truth

| Application / suite | Licence | Link | Role tag | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| OWASP Benchmark for Python (BenchmarkPython) | GPL-3.0 | [GitHub: OWASP-Benchmark/BenchmarkPython](https://github.com/OWASP-Benchmark/BenchmarkPython) | [evaluation] | Python port of the OWASP Benchmark concept: labelled true/false-positive test cases for Python vulnerability detectors, scored with the same utilities. Evaluate on it: Python counterpart of the Java curve; new enough that few tools publish scores - an opening for the paper. |
| NIST SARD and the Juliet test suites (C/C++ 1.3, Java 1.3, C# 1.3) | Public domain in the United States (stated on the SARD suite page; NSA/NIST US Government work) | [samate.nist.gov](https://samate.nist.gov/SARD/test-suites); [related page](https://samate.nist.gov/SARD/test-suites/112) | [evaluation] | SARD hosts hundreds of thousands of synthetic and real test programs. Evaluate on it: C/C++ and C# coverage and per-CWE recall. Juliet's tell-tale names (`bad`, `goodG2B`, CWE ids in file names) leak labels to an LLM - L2 pseudonymisation conveniently removes that contamination, which is itself a publishable observation. |
| OWASP Benchmark for Java (BenchmarkJava) + BenchmarkUtils scorecards | GPL-2.0 (BenchmarkJava); GPL-3.0 (BenchmarkUtils) | [GitHub: OWASP-Benchmark/BenchmarkJava](https://github.com/OWASP-Benchmark/BenchmarkJava); [GitHub: OWASP-Benchmark/BenchmarkUtils](https://github.com/OWASP-Benchmark/BenchmarkUtils) | [evaluation] | Thousands of small runnable Java servlet test cases across 11 CWE categories, each labelled true or false positive in expectedresults CSV. Evaluate on it: headline accuracy-vs-privacy-level curve for Java. Write a BenchmarkUtils-compatible SARIF so CodeKavach appears on the same scorecard as commercial tools. GPL: keep as an external checkout. |
| flawgarden reality-check (real-CVE SAST benchmark) and BenchmarkJava-mutated | Apache-2.0 (reality-check); GPL-2.0 (BenchmarkJava-mutated) | [GitHub: flawgarden/reality-check](https://github.com/flawgarden/reality-check); [GitHub: flawgarden/BenchmarkJava-mutated](https://github.com/flawgarden/BenchmarkJava-mutated) | [evaluation] | reality-check packages 165 real Java CVEs (C#, Go, Python planned) with vulnerable/fixed versions and ground truth in SARIF (`truth.sarif`). Evaluate on it: real-world complement to synthetic suites, and SARIF ground truth plugs straight into CodeKavach's SARIF output for automated scoring. |
| OpenSSF CVE Benchmark | MIT | [GitHub: ossf-cve-benchmark/ossf-cve-benchmark](https://github.com/ossf-cve-benchmark/ossf-cve-benchmark) | [evaluation] | Code and metadata for 200+ real JavaScript/TypeScript CVEs with pre- and post-patch commits and drivers for running SAST tools against both. Evaluate on it: real-CVE JS/TS recall plus 'does the tool stay quiet on the patched version' precision. |
| SonarSource sonar-benchmarks-scores | LGPL-3.0 | [GitHub: SonarSource/sonar-benchmarks-scores](https://github.com/SonarSource/sonar-benchmarks-scores) | [evaluation] [baseline] | Sonar's corrected ground-truth files for popular SAST benchmarks (Java, C#, Python) and Sonar's own scores on them. Cross-check ground truth (several benchmarks contain labelling errors) and a vendor baseline to compare against. |
| Damn Vulnerable C Program | not stated | [GitHub: hardik05/Damn\_Vulnerable\_C\_Program](https://github.com/hardik05/Damn_Vulnerable_C_Program) | [evaluation] | Single-file C image parser with integer overflow, heap/stack overflow, UAF, double free and OOB read, made for fuzzing tutorials. Tiny C fixture for demos and unit tests of the C/C++ slicer. No licence file. |

## 8. Public audit report corpora

Published security assessment reports used to derive the report structure, finding template and tone of the CodeKavach deliverables. They are copyright of their authors: study the structure, do not copy text.

### 8.1 Report corpora and exemplar reports

| Corpus / report | Publisher | Licence | Link | Role tag | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| X41 D-Sec - Source Code Audit on Git for OSTIF (Jan 2023) | X41 D-Sec GmbH (E. Sesterhenn, J. Schneeweisz, M. Vervier) with GitLab, for OSTIF | Public report, copyright X41 D-Sec | [x41-dsec.de](https://x41-dsec.de/static/reports/X41-OSTIF-Gitlab-Git-Security-Audit-20230117-public.pdf); [related page](https://x41-dsec.de/security/research/news/2023/01/17/git-security-audit-ostif/) | [inspiration] | A 96-page public source code audit report (41 person-days) of Git 2.38.1. Model for CodeKavach's document-control block and the one-page dashboard (severity histogram + CWE distribution chart). Also shows the convention of separating security findings from non-security 'informational notes', which maps well to LLM-triaged true positives vs. hardening suggestions. |
| Trail of Bits - cURL Security Assessment (Dec 2022) | Trail of Bits (E. Lopez, S. Michaels, P. Platek) for OSTIF/OpenSSF | Public report, all rights reserved by Trail of Bits | [GitHub: trailofbits/publications](https://github.com/trailofbits/publications/blob/master/reviews/2022-12-curl-securityreview.pdf); [ostif.org](https://ostif.org/the-ostif-audit-of-curl-with-trail-of-bits-is-complete/) | [inspiration] | A full public source-code security review of curl/libcurl commissioned through OSTIF. Template to imitate: each finding has Severity + Difficulty + Type + Finding ID (TOB-CURL-n) + Target + Description + Exploit Scenario + short/long-term Recommendations. CodeKavach should emit the rating-definition appendix automatically so every severity label in a report is defined inside the same document. |
| OSTIF audit catalogue | Open Source Technology Improvement Fund (OSTIF) | not stated | [ostif.org](https://ostif.org/audits/) | [evaluation] [inspiration] | The Open Source Technology Improvement Fund lists 100+ completed audits (2016-2026) of critical open-source projects. Evaluation corpus: projects with a published professional audit give ground truth for a 'would CodeKavach have found what the human auditors found?' experiment (e.g. curl, Git, Paramiko, PHP-SRC). Also the best source of source-code-audit (not web pentest) report samples. |
| public-pentesting-reports (Julio Cesar Fort) | Julio Cesar Fort and contributors | No licence file (individual reports are third-party copyright) | [GitHub: juliocesarfort/public-pentesting-reports](https://github.com/juliocesarfort/public-pentesting-reports) | [inspiration] | Community-curated GitHub archive of several hundred publicly released penetration test and code audit reports. Design inspiration and qualitative corpus: sample 20-30 source-code-audit reports across firms to derive CodeKavach's canonical report outline and finding template; cite as the corpus behind the 'Big-Four-grade' claim. Do not redistribute the PDFs - link only. |
| Trail of Bits Publications repository (Security Reviews) | Trail of Bits | CC-BY-SA-4.0 (repository); reports individually copyrighted | [GitHub: trailofbits/publications](https://github.com/trailofbits/publications); [related page](https://github.com/trailofbits/publications#security-reviews) | [inspiration] | Trail of Bits' index of its public work: hundreds of security review PDFs (technology products, cloud-native, cryptography, AI/ML, blockchain), plus papers, guides and talks. Primary design reference for the finding schema (ID, severity, difficulty, type, target, description, exploit scenario, short-term/long-term recommendation) and for the 'Codebase Maturity Evaluation' scorecard that CodeKavach can reproduce as an executive-summary table. |
| Cure53 publications (pentest and source code audit reports) | Cure53 (Dr.-Ing. Mario Heiderich et al.) | Public reports, copyright Cure53/clients | [cure53.de](https://cure53.de/#publications); [related page](https://cure53.de/pentest-report_curl.pdf) | [inspiration] | Cure53 publishes roughly a hundred client-approved reports as PDFs on its home page. Contrast case to the heavier Trail of Bits/X41 template: shows a minimal report profile CodeKavach can offer ('lean' vs 'full' template) and the ticket-style ID scheme PROJECT-ROUND-NNN that survives re-tests. |
| NCC Group public reports | NCC Group | Public reports, copyright NCC Group | [nccgroup.com](https://www.nccgroup.com/research/?category=public-reports); [related page](https://www.nccgroup.com/research/public-report-zcash-frost-security-assessment/) | [inspiration] | NCC Group's research portal, where client-sanctioned 'Public Report:' assessments (cryptography reviews, language/runtime and product security assessments) are released with downloadable PDFs. Second major template family to compare against; useful for the Impact/Exploitability two-axis rating and for the per-finding 'Location' field that CodeKavach fills with file:line ranges. |
| Quarkslab public-reports | Quarkslab | not stated | [GitHub: quarkslab/public-reports](https://github.com/quarkslab/public-reports); [blog.quarkslab.com](https://blog.quarkslab.com/security-audit-of-php-src.html) | [inspiration] | Git repository holding Quarkslab's public audit PDFs with links to companion blog posts, actively maintained (2026 entries include Cortex, Scala, Paramiko and EVerest audits). Recent (2024-2026) source-code audit samples in languages CodeKavach targets (Python: Paramiko; C: PHP-SRC; JVM: Scala) - usable both as report-style reference and as evaluation ground truth. |

Design inspiration for CodeKavach's re-scan/re-test mode: keep stable finding IDs across runs and print Open / Fixed / Risk-accepted status plus a retest summary table. |

Additional corpus for the report-structure study: shows how a small firm packages code audit + threat model + supply-chain (SLSA/SBOM) sections in a single report. |

### 8.2 Reporting and audit practice write-ups

| Document | Publisher | Link | Role tag | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Daniel Stenberg - 'Death by a thousand slops' | Daniel Stenberg (curl) | [daniel.haxx.se](https://daniel.haxx.se/blog/2025/07/14/death-by-a-thousand-slops/) | [inspiration] | curl's maintainer documents how fluent but fabricated AI-written vulnerability reports (invented functions, plausible CWE labels, no reproducible evidence) overwhelmed the project's security intake in 2025. Threat-model and quality input: every LLM-produced claim in a CodeKavach report must be anchored to a verifiable local artefact (file path, line range, snippet hash, analyser rule ID, taint path). |
| Trail of Bits - How to prepare for a security review | Garret Reece (Trail of Bits) | [blog.trailofbits.com](https://blog.trailofbits.com/2018/04/06/how-to-prepare-for-a-security-audit/) | [inspiration] | Short practitioner checklist for clients about to be audited: set an explicit goal for the review. Basis for CodeKavach's pre-audit intake form and 'audit readiness' section: review goal, prior assessments, build/deploy instructions, and known issues are collected before the scan and printed in the scope chapter of the report. |
| SANS white paper: Writing a Penetration Testing Report | Mansour Alharbi (SANS Institute) | [sans.org](https://www.sans.org/white-papers/33343) | [related-work] | A SANS Reading Room paper describing report planning, content for management versus technical audiences, and design. Secondary citation for the dual-audience principle (executive vs. technical sections) used in CodeKavach templates. |
| Trail of Bits Testing Handbook (appsec.guide) | Trail of Bits | [appsec.guide](https://appsec.guide/); [GitHub: trailofbits/testing-handbook](https://github.com/trailofbits/testing-handbook) | [inspiration] | Trail of Bits' public handbook on configuring and using static analysers (Semgrep, CodeQL), fuzzers and sanitizers the way their auditors do, including CI integration. Practical reference for how a top-tier firm operationalises the same OSS engines CodeKavach orchestrates; cite in methodology and reuse its guidance on rule selection and triage. Licence: CC-BY-4.0. |

## Maintaining this file

1. Add a resource only after its URL returns HTTP 200 (or, for a DOI behind publisher bot protection, after Crossref confirms the metadata) and the page says what we cite it for.
2. Record the licence as stated upstream on the day you check, and state any constraint on MIT distribution or on scanning proprietary code in the Notes column.
3. Every new runtime dependency is recorded here in the same commit that adds it (AGENTS.md, section 3).
4. Give each entry one or two role tags from the legend. `[integrated]` is reserved for components of the default pipeline in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`; promoting a `[planned]` entry to `[integrated]` goes with an architecture change (ADR).
5. Frameworks, standards, regulations and methodologies go to REFERENCE.md.
6. Do not cite a source that is not in this file or in REFERENCE.md.
